# Project: MCP Factory

DFY (Done-For-You), BYOK (Bring Your Own Keys) automation platform on MCP. User gives URL + budget; the platform handles lead finding, outreach, email generation, reporting.

## Commands

```bash
pnpm dev                    # All services via Turbo
pnpm dev:dashboard          # Dashboard only (Next.js, port 3001)
pnpm dev:<service-name>     # Any individual service
pnpm build                  # Build all (Turbo-orchestrated)
pnpm lint                   # Lint all packages
pnpm generate:readme        # Regenerate README.md from shared/content

# Per-package testing — scripts use `test`/`test:unit`, NOT `vitest` directly
pnpm --filter @distribute/<package> test                              # all tests
pnpm --filter @distribute/<package> test:unit                         # unit only
pnpm --filter @distribute/<package> test tests/unit/specific.test.ts  # single file
```

**Verify frontend with `tsc --noEmit`, NOT lint — `next lint` is GONE in Next 16.** Any `next lint` invocation reads `lint` as a directory arg and fails; no standalone `eslint` binary in the repo. Real check: `cd apps/<app> && node_modules/.bin/tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.json` (dashboard has ~3 pre-existing test-file `TS1501` regex errors — ignore, only care about your files). (#1223) **The suite passing is NOT a substitute: the guards are source-substring, so a file that does not even PARSE can keep them all green.** A `{/* comment */}` dropped directly inside ANY single-child JSX expression above the root element is two children in one expression — 10 `TS1005`/`TS1382`/`TS17015` errors, 1702 tests still passing. It is NOT only the `{cond && ( … )}` form: a ternary branch (`) : ( {/* … */} <div>`) fails identically, and that one reads as safe because the branch already has parentheses around it. Put the comment ABOVE the expression or INSIDE the element, and run `tsc` on every JSX edit however cosmetic (2026-08-28, the campaign Outcome chart: a comment explaining a new wrapper class, added between `) : (` and its `<div>`).

**Don't trust RTK's `next build` summary** — the hook replaces stdout with a `Errors: N | Warnings: M` one-liner that can be wrong (phantom `Errors: 1` on a clean run). Real run: `rtk proxy -- npx next build` (the `--` is required), trust the log + exit code.

**Running `pnpm dev:dashboard` (or any `next dev`) REWRITES the tracked `apps/<app>/next-env.d.ts`**, flipping its import from `./.next/types/routes.d.ts` to `./.next/dev/types/routes.d.ts`. A `git add -A` after a local render check therefore commits a dev-mode type path into a file whose own header says *"This file should not be edited"*, and nothing goes red — `tsc` and the suite pass either way, because the path only matters to whichever mode generated it. `git checkout -- apps/<app>/next-env.d.ts` before staging. Generalise: after booting the app to LOOK at a change (the render-it-and-look rule), read `git status` for files you never opened — a dev server writes to the tree, and the screenshot step is the one place in the workflow where that is easy to miss.

**Build shared workspace packages before app tests/build.** Vitest/Vite resolve workspace deps via `dist/`; an unbuilt `shared/*` surfaces as `Failed to resolve entry for package "@distribute/<name>"`. Run `pnpm -r build` after install or after pulling `shared/` changes.

**Source-substring page tests: a `not.toMatch(/X/)` guard trips on the fix's own explanatory comment** (a comment that writes the forbidden literal fails the test). Reword the comment, or assert against a comment-stripped copy (`pr-expert-public-report.test.ts` ships `stripComments(src)`). **Before adding a `not.toContain("<literal>")` guard, grep the WHOLE target file for that literal, not just the JSX you edited — a big page carries PRE-EXISTING comments mentioning it far from the code you touched, and each one fails the guard.** Reword every hit in the same commit. (#1235, DIS-112; #3033 removed an "AI-suggested" badge from `onboarding.tsx` and two unrelated comments ~1600 and ~300 lines away still wrote the literal.) **When the grep shows the literal also lives in LEGITIMATE code elsewhere, rewording is wrong — SCOPE the guard to a slice of the function you actually changed** (`sliceFrom("async function saveX(", N)` then `expect(body).not.toContain(...)`), because a file-wide `not.toContain` on a common expression asserts something you do not mean. #3039 forbade restating `replyToMeetingPct: nextRates.r2m` in the two economics SAVE paths, but the same expression legitimately builds the projection helper's econ overrides one screen away — a read, not a write; the file-wide guard failed on correct code until it was scoped to the two save functions. **The trap also runs in the OTHER direction, and that one has no target file to grep: some guards SCAN ALL of `src/` for a co-occurrence heuristic, so a DOC COMMENT you add to any file can trip a guard you never opened.** `api-proxy.test.ts` flags any file containing both `getToken` and `apiCall` as token-passing to the api client; the SWR rewrite of `use-tenant-switcher.ts` (which legitimately calls `getToken` for the org-switch re-mint) failed it purely because a comment explained that its reads "go through `apiCall`" — the code passes no token. Rewording the comment ("the shared api client") fixed it. So when a guard you didn't touch goes red on a file you only commented, read the guard's predicate before assuming a real violation. **Third face: the `sliceFrom(marker)` ANCHOR itself collides with a doc comment, silently slicing prose instead of code.** `indexOf` takes the FIRST hit, and a good doc comment above a component names the very token you want to anchor on (`role="button"`, a field name, a class), so the guard then asserts against explanatory English and fails with a confusing "expected 'role=\"button\">`, NOT a `<button>`. Se…' to contain 'tabIndex={0}'" — the quoted haystack is your own comment. Use `lastIndexOf`, or anchor on a JSX-only string (`aria-label="More info"`, `onKeyDown={(e) => {`). This bit twice on 2026-07-30 alone, once as a `not.toContain` on a comment and once as a mis-anchored slice. **Fifth face: a `const` declaration is a bad anchor, because a later change can HOIST or duplicate it and `indexOf` then slices from somewhere else entirely — silently, on a guard nobody touched.** A guard anchored on `const onePath = selectedFunnels.length === 1;` inside the budget step kept passing until that flag was hoisted to the top of the flow for a second reader; the first hit moved ~2800 lines up, so every assertion (including a `not.toContain("—")`) ran against unrelated code. Anchor on something only the target can contain: the function signature, or a string literal from its own copy. **Fourth face, the slice LENGTH: compute the offset, never guess it.** A `slice(at, at + N)` whose `N` falls short cuts the assertion's target out of the haystack, and the failure message quotes the SLICE (which starts with the function signature you anchored on), so it reads as "the code is missing" for code that is right there — and every comment you add to the function pushes the boundary further, so a guard that passed at commit time breaks on the next edit. Measure it once (`python3 -c "s=open(f).read(); a=s.index(anchor); print(s.index(target)-a)"`) and give the real distance real headroom. Cost 2026-07-30 (leads-table mobile fit): a 5000-then-7000-char slice and a 900-char slice each dropped their last assertion, two wasted suite runs. **And the danger flips with the assertion's POLARITY, so "when unsure, give it headroom" is wrong half the time: a `toContain` fails when the slice is too SHORT (the target got cut out), a `not.toContain` fails when it is too LONG (the slice ran past the function into a NEIGHBOUR that legitimately contains the forbidden literal — very often the next function's doc comment).** So for a `not.toContain` guard, measure to the function's CLOSING BRACE and do not pad; write the measured number in a comment beside it so the next editor does not "fix" it by widening. Cost 2026-07-31 (#3167): a 400-char slice from `function continueFunnelStats()` — asserting the step persists nothing — reached the next function's comment *"Saved via sales-economics"* and failed on correct code; the body is 188 chars. **Fifth face, the slice DIRECTION: `sliceFrom` only ever looks FORWARD, so an anchor that sits AFTER its target can never see it.** Gating existing copy behind a new condition puts the gate BEFORE the string, so a guard anchored on the string and asserting the gate reads the wrong side of the file and fails no matter how long the slice is — widening it looks like the fix and is not. Anchor on the GATE and assert the copy, not the reverse. The same `python3` measurement that sizes a slice also reveals the sign (`print(s.index(target) - s.index(anchor))` going NEGATIVE is the tell), so measure before writing either direction. Cost 2026-08-24 (service-suggest-loading): a guard anchored on `We drafted these from` asserting `servicesDrafted` — the gate is 70 chars EARLIER. **Seventh face, and it fires on a file you are ADDING to rather than editing: a guard that COUNTS occurrences or takes the FIRST `indexOf` over a whole file is silently scoped to "there is one component in here", so landing a SECOND component in that file breaks it — and the failure reads as a shape regression in the component you did not touch.** `expect((src.match(/hidden md:table-cell/g) ?? []).length).toBe(10)` is a statement about ONE table; with two in the file it counts 23 and reports the original table as having grown seven columns. `src.indexOf("<thead>")` finds whichever table is declared first, which is the new one, so an ordering assertion about the old table's columns runs against the new table's header and fails on correct code. Both are ordinary consequences of co-locating a sibling, and neither is visible until the suite runs. So when you add a component beside a guarded one, bound EVERY existing count/first-index guard to the component it means (a `const listTable = src.slice(src.indexOf("export function X("))` at the top of the test file, used by each guard), and give the new component its own bounded slices in its own test. Cost 2026-08-28 (distribute.you #3683): four guards in `campaigns-page.test.ts` went red on a table whose markup had not changed by one character.

**The `hidden` ATTRIBUTE loses to a display CLASS, so `hidden={cond}` on a `flex`/`grid`/`block` element does nothing.** `[hidden] { display: none }` lives in the UA stylesheet and any author `display` declaration outranks it, so `<div className="flex …" hidden={boardOnly}>` renders exactly as it did before — no error, no red test, the control simply stays reachable to anyone who looks. It reads as obviously correct, which is what makes it dangerous on a gate. Conditionally RENDER (`{!cond && (<div …>)}`) — which is the right shape anyway, since markup a page still carries is a control anybody can still reach — or put the state in the class (`${cond ? "hidden" : ""}`), where Tailwind's own `hidden` utility is a real `display: none` at author specificity. (2026-08-28, the campaign leads board.)

**Sixth, cheapest face: a guard asserting user-facing COPY must use the SOURCE spelling, not the rendered one.** JSX escapes an apostrophe as `&apos;`, so `toContain("We couldn't read your site")` fails against source reading `We couldn&apos;t read your site` while the rendered page is exactly what you typed. Copy-paste the literal out of the file rather than out of the design.

**Run `pnpm --filter @distribute/dashboard test` before pushing a dashboard change — `tsc` alone misses source-substring guards** (many tests assert a page contains a literal expression/copy string). `tsc` stays green while the guard goes red in CI and silently blocks auto-merge. Run it (~3s, source-only) and update the matching guard in the same commit. (#1252) **Run the package SCRIPT, never the raw `vitest` binary — the script is `vitest run --exclude 'tests/e2e/**'`, so a bare `node_modules/.bin/vitest run` also collects the three Playwright specs under `tests/e2e/`, which cannot run under vitest and report as 3 FAILED FILES. Every actual test still passes (`1504 passed / 0 failed` beside `3 failed` files), so the summary reads as a regression you just introduced when it is only the missing exclude.** Same shape in `apps/admin`. Tell them apart by the failed-FILE list being entirely `tests/e2e/*.spec.ts` and the failed-TEST count being zero.

**`apps/admin` tests are NOT a CI merge gate — only `apps/dashboard`'s are (`.github/workflows/test.yml` = build + `--filter @distribute/dashboard test` + lint; NO `--filter admin test` job).** So admin source-substring guards ROT SILENTLY: a rename/nav change that doesn't update the matching admin test ships red and nothing blocks it (#2689's Signups-tab rename left 4 stale assertions across `public-metrics-page.test.ts` + `context-sidebar.test.ts` + `last-brand.test.ts`, all red on main). Because there's no gate, you MUST run `pnpm --filter @distribute/admin test` locally before pushing an admin change and update every matching guard in the same commit — CI will not catch it for you. When editing an admin test file, also fix any pre-existing red assertions in THAT file (they're this same rot); sibling test files reading code you didn't touch are follow-ups, not scope. (#2698)

## `apps/admin` is the STAFF GOD-MODE console — a dashboard fork, NOT a customer surface, and it runs on the HETZNER BOX

**It is a container on `167.233.196.79` beside the whole backend fleet (#3298), reachable at `admin.distribute.you`.** It left Vercel on 2026-08-07 (#3293/#3297) because the allowlist held one person's two addresses and a hosted build cost 1,231 build minutes a month to serve nobody outside the team; `apps/admin/vercel.json` is vestigial, kept in case something goes back. `pnpm dev:admin` (port 3002) still works locally — seed `apps/admin/.env.local` from the box: `ssh -i ~/.ssh/oracle-distribute root@167.233.196.79 'cat /root/distribute/env/admin-app.env'` (and `admin-app.build.env` for the `NEXT_PUBLIC_*`), which carries every var admin needs including `ADMIN_DISTRIBUTE_API_KEY`.

- **`apps/admin/Dockerfile`, built from the MONOREPO ROOT.** `output: "standalone"` with `outputFileTracingRoot` pinned to the repo root — `next build` runs with `apps/admin` as its cwd, so without it the output silently misses everything pnpm hoisted above the package. The image installs only admin's subtree (`--filter @distribute/admin...`).
- **`NEXT_PUBLIC_*` is inlined at BUILD time** from `apps/admin/.env.production` (written on the box, gitignored). Changing one needs a rebuild, not a restart. Server-side secrets arrive at run time via the compose `env_file`.
- **Two runtime secrets did NOT survive the Vercel cutover, and a `process.env.X` grep cannot find that class of hole — `requireEnv("X")` reads the name as a STRING.** `env/admin-app.env` reached the box with 16 keys while `apps/admin/src` needs 18: `POSTHOG_PERSONAL_API_KEY` (the `/metrics` PostHog visitor + signup queries) and `STRIPE_SECRET_KEY` (the paid-users tab's per-customer payment-method fan-out) were both absent. They fail LOUD and correctly, so **four of the five `/metrics` tabs threw on render** — every one but `?view=active-users`, which is the only tab that skips `fetchPublicStatsSummary`. The symptom is a bare `digest` on screen and nothing else; the actual line is in `docker logs distribute-admin-app-1` (`⨯ Error: [public-stats] POSTHOG_PERSONAL_API_KEY is required`). Both values already sat on the same box in `env/dashboard-app.env`, so the recovery was two appended lines plus `docker compose up -d admin-app` — a RECREATE, not a rebuild, since neither is `NEXT_PUBLIC_*`. **Generalise: after moving an app between platforms, diff what it READS against what landed, and enumerate the reads with `grep -rhoE 'requireEnv\("[A-Z0-9_]+"\)'` ALONGSIDE the `process.env.` grep** — the env-var name inside a `requireEnv()` helper is a string literal, invisible to the obvious search, so the obvious search reports a clean bill of health on a container that is missing credentials. The existing "a deleted platform takes its secrets with it" rule below covers the values with no second source; this is the other half, where a second source existed and nobody checked whether the copy had been made.
- **The box is NOT a git repo** — `/root/distribute` is hand-managed like the rest of the fleet: `docker-compose.override.yml` holds `admin-app` (the base compose file is generated from the Railway bundle and says not to hand-edit it), `env/admin-app{,.build}.env`, a Caddy vhost, and `deploy-admin.sh` (fetch main → refresh `.env.production` → rebuild → restart).
- **Two gates, and the second one is the load-bearing one.** A Cloudflare Access application allows `kevin.lourd@gmail.com` only, so the email allowlist in middleware is now the second gate rather than the only one. But **Access protects a HOSTNAME, not an origin** — a request straight to the box's IP carrying the right `Host` and SNI sails past it, and it did (verified 200 before the fix). The Caddy vhost therefore 404s any peer outside Cloudflare's published ranges. Residual gap: an attacker proxying through their OWN Cloudflare zone arrives from a Cloudflare IP. Closing that needs a managed Transform Rule injecting a shared header (or Authenticated Origin Pulls), and **no token on this machine has the Transform Rules scope** — the MCP and the `cloudflare` skill's token both 403. **Every other service on that box has the same origin bypass and no allowlist at all.**
- **Admin REGISTERS NOTHING — `apps/admin/src/instrumentation.ts` is DELETED, and the dashboard is the single registrar for every shared platform store.** Do NOT re-add one. Both apps used to write email templates, platform keys, chat configs, the `cold-email` prompt and the welcome-gift pin, keyed by name and last-writer-wins; 6 of the 9 template names differed, so each boot silently overwrote the other app's copy (read at the destination: the dashboard wrote its 14 at 10:17:52, admin replaced 9 of them at 10:19:53). The dashboard's set is a strict SUPERSET for templates and platform keys and byte-identical for the rest, so deleting admin's copy removed nothing from the store. **A change to a chat config, the platform prompt, an email template or the gift amount therefore goes in `apps/dashboard`, even when the surface consuming it is admin-only** — admin's duplicate guards for those were deleted with it (the dashboard's own `workflow-chat-context` / `platform-config-startup` / `welcome-gift-pinned` cover the same ground). Owner-decided when the clobber was found: **the customer dashboard wins any conflict between the two.** (#3302)

⚠️ **Before deleting a deployment, diff its boot registrations against the app that survives.** `apps/admin/src/instrumentation.ts` was the ONLY registrar of the `posthog` platform key, which features-service consumes — a staff console with one user was supplying a backend credential. That entry moved to the dashboard first (#3292). The same class of gap applies to email templates and platform prompts: a registration that stops running fails SILENTLY, because the stored row survives and nothing re-writes it until the store moves. Verify at the DESTINATION (`SELECT … FROM platform_keys` on key-service's Neon), never by reading the registration code.

`apps/admin` is the dashboard cloned for internal staff. Two load-bearing facts before touching it:
- **The admin BRAND MARK is intentionally DISTINCT from the customer dashboard's GREEN charter dot — do NOT re-sync it in a fleet-wide rebrand.** Admin uses a WHITE mark on the theme-neutral surfaces (tab favicon `src/app/icon.svg`, `src/app/apple-icon.png`, `public/favicon.jpg`) and a theme-responsive header mark (`components/header.tsx`, inline SVG dot — **blue in light mode, white in dark mode**, no dark square). This is so a staff tab reads distinct from a customer tab at a glance. A "swap the mark across all apps" PR (like #2614 which green-ified everything) MUST skip admin or re-clobbers this — keep the customer dashboard green, admin white/blue. The green `text-brand-600` "distribute" wordmark text was left as-is (only the mark differs). (#2625)
- **Access = email allowlist, single source `apps/admin/src/lib/admin-allowlist.ts`** (`ADMIN_ALLOWED_EMAILS` + `isAdminEmail`). Edge-gated in `proxy.ts` off the session `email` claim (403 for any signed-in non-staff on every non-public route); the `/api/admin/*` routes re-check it (defense-in-depth). Add/remove staff in that ONE file — don't re-inline the list. **The api-service `requireStaff` gate (staff-only `/v1/billing/credits/*` grant proxies + `/v1/billing/usage-discount` + admin-brands/features-admin, on the forwarded `x-email`) HARDCODES the same allowlist in `src/middleware/auth.ts` — the `STAFF_EMAILS` Railway env var was REMOVED (api-service v0.84.2, #698) because Kevin doesn't want a Railway var for this + it silently drifted. It is still a THIRD copy that must stay byte-equal with the admin/dashboard allowlists, but now via a code edit (no env). Whenever you add/remove staff here, edit the hardcoded array in api-service `src/middleware/auth.ts` to the SAME set + ship it, or the missing staff get a 403 on credit-granting / discount-setting. Do NOT re-introduce a `STAFF_EMAILS` env read.** (admin free-credits #2059; hardcode #698)
- **Cross-org "god-mode" = option A (real auto-join), NOT read-only impersonation.** The breadcrumb org switcher lists EVERY platform org via `GET /api/admin/orgs?q=` (Clerk Backend `getOrganizationList`, `CLERK_SECRET_KEY`), not `useOrganizationList({ userMemberships })`. On switch, `handleOrgSwitch` first `POST /api/admin/orgs/:orgId/join` (makes the admin a real `org:admin` member, idempotent) THEN `setActive` — because Clerk `setActive` rejects a non-member org. This keeps the whole **DIS-143 membership-scoped data path unchanged** (Clerk active org → JWT → gateway `x-external-org-id`); brands/features/writes all follow the active org with zero extra change. Accepted tradeoff: staff emails appear in each customer's Clerk member list. Why A over a read-only `x-admin-view-org-id` impersonation header: the gateway admin path (`api-service auth.ts`) trusts `x-external-org-id` with no membership check BUT resolves the (org,user) PAIR via client-service `/internal/resolve`, which **upserts the caller's `users.orgId` to the viewed org** (single org per user) — so a naive "forward URL org + staff user id" would churn/mis-attribute the staff user across customer orgs. Real membership (A) makes that churn legitimate + lets staff act, which is what's wanted. (#1643)
- **The SAME god-mode also lives in the customer `apps/dashboard`, but STAFF-GATED — and there the gating model flips.** `apps/dashboard/src/lib/admin-allowlist.ts` (byte-equal copy) + the same `GET /api/admin/orgs` + `POST /api/admin/orgs/:orgId/join` routes; breadcrumb shows the all-orgs switcher only when `isAdminEmail(useUser().email)` (else the unchanged `userMemberships` list — customers see only their own orgs). CRITICAL DIFFERENCE vs the admin app: the dashboard is the customer surface with **NO edge allowlist**, so the route-level `isAdminEmail` **403** is the SOLE security boundary — it's what stops a logged-in customer from enumerating/joining every other tenant. The client `isStaff` flag is cosmetic (hides the UI); never rely on it for access. Keep the dashboard allowlist + routes byte-equal with admin's. (#1655)
- **The god-mode join route MUST be idempotent via Clerk's RESPONSE, never via a `getOrganizationMembershipList` pre-check — that pre-check reads only the FIRST PAGE and silently breaks for staff who joined many orgs.** Symptom: god-mode switch into a (real, onboarded) customer org lands on a **blank org page** (`/orgs/:id` renders, sidebar + header fine, main empty). Causal chain (took a full session to trace, documented so it's instant next time): `POST /api/admin/orgs/:id/join` 502s → `OrgActivator` (dashboard, the URL-driven god-mode variant) does `if (!res.ok) throw` BEFORE `setActive`, so the active org never switches → URL org ≠ Clerk active org → `useAuthQuery`'s org-consistency gate (`urlOrg === activeOrg`) DISABLES every org-scoped read → the org-landing page's `["brands"]` query stays `undefined` → `if (!brandsData) return null` → blank; meanwhile every `/api/v1/*` returns **409 org_desync** (proxy `checkProxyOrg`: client-sent URL org ≠ JWT active org). The 502 root cause: the join route did `getOrganizationMembershipList({userId})` (FIRST PAGE only) → for a staff member of many orgs an existing membership beyond page 1 read as absent → re-`createOrganizationMembership` → Clerk **400 `already_a_member_in_organization`** → route wrapped it 502. **Tell it apart from the legit case:** an org the staff is NOT meant to enter (incomplete/never-onboarded org, no brand) bouncing to `/onboarding` is CORRECT (that's the first-run gate, not this bug) — THIS bug is a blank page on a COMPLETE org (has brands). **Fix (KevinLourd/godmode-join-idempotent):** drop the paged pre-check; `createOrganizationMembership` directly and treat a Clerk error whose `errors[].code === "already_a_member_in_organization"` as success (`{ok:true}`) — trust Clerk (server-side source of truth) over a paged list. Applied byte-equal to BOTH `apps/dashboard` + `apps/admin` join routes. Hardening in the same PR: `OrgActivator` now `await session?.getToken({skipCache:true})` AFTER `setActive` (mirrors `handleOrgSwitch`, CLAUDE.md DIS-143 item 3) to close the stale-token 409 window. **Diagnostic approach that nailed it:** the route only returned `err.message` ("Bad Request", Clerk's generic) — the real `errors[].code` was in the **Vercel runtime logs** (`get_runtime_logs query:"admin-join"` → `already_a_member_in_organization`); and the "is this org actually complete?" question was answered by querying **brand-service** (`org_brands` for the org → 2 brands = onboarded → blank is a bug, not the onboarding gate). Org NAMES live only in Clerk (client-service `orgs.name` is null for everyone), and the prod `CLERK_SECRET_KEY` Backend API is IP-restricted from a dev machine (403 code 1010) — so map a Clerk-org-name to data via brand-service, not Clerk. (godmode-join-idempotent)

- **The admin WORKFLOW-EDIT chat (`apps/admin/.../workflows/[workflowId]/page.tsx` → `WorkflowChat`) MUST NOT inline the full workflow DAG (`workflow.dag`) in the chat `context` — api-service caps the JSON request body (`express.json({ limit: "10mb" })` today; it was the 100KB Express default when this shipped, and a large workflow's DAG serializes to >300KB) → `PayloadTooLargeError: request entity too large` on api-service → 500 → `{"error":"Internal server error"}` in the chat panel on EVERY message.** The `context` is re-sent on every turn (chat-service `buildSystemPrompt` JSON.stringifies it fresh each turn from the client payload — it is NOT persisted per session), and it travels through the api-service gateway, so the 100KB cap bites on the very first message. The model reads the DAG ON DEMAND via its existing `get_workflow_details(<uuid>)` tool (already the HARD RULE for edits); the page's `instructions` point at that tool, and only small fields (`summary`, `feature`, identity) stay inline for a tool-free overview. Guarded by `apps/admin/tests/workflow-chat-context.test.ts` (`not.toContain("dag: workflow.dag")`). RULE for ANY chat/Edit-with-AI surface (admin OR dashboard): never inline a large payload (DAG, full lead list, big profile) into `context` — fetch it via a tool on demand. Persisting context server-side by session does NOT fix this (the first message already carries the full payload). (#2978)

- **An IDENTIFIER a downstream service validates against a closed set (`model`, prompt-template `type`, `service`, an endpoint `path`) is READ from that service's schema, NEVER transcribed from the user's words — and `validate_workflow` does not catch the difference, so a validated DAG is not a proven one.** The user speaks product language ("DeepSeek Flash v4"); the service accepts an exact token; turning one into the other produces a slug that looks right, passes every check, and 400s on the first live send. **Answering from memory is the specific failure mode** — training data lags the platform, so a model/template/provider shipped last week is absent from what the LLM "knows" and present in the schema, one `get_endpoint_details` call away. The rule lives in the `workflow` chat's system prompt (`apps/dashboard/src/instrumentation.ts`, the single registrar, so it covers the org-scoped editor AND the feature-stats panel) and in the panel's own context (`workflowCatalogueInstructions`): read the permitted set, copy the exact string, and if what the user named is absent **say so BEFORE writing anything**. Do NOT "fix" this by inlining a model list into the prompt — it goes stale on the next alias and reproduces the bug with extra steps. **`validate_workflow` checks DAG structure, body field NAMES against the request schema, and referenced output paths — never whether a VALUE is one the service accepts**, so it returns `valid: true` on a DAG that cannot run; never report such a workflow as "tested". Cost 2026-08-15: asked to duplicate three cold-email workflows onto DeepSeek, the chat wrote `deepseek-flash-v4` (real alias: `deepseek-flash`, provider `vercel`, chat-service #376) into three PRODUCTION DAGs, called them "testé, 100% valide", and three turns later asserted DeepSeek has no Flash V4 — wrong in both directions. Zero blast radius only because no campaign referenced them; all three dynasties were deprecated. Guards: `apps/dashboard/tests/platform-config-startup.test.ts` + `apps/admin/tests/feature-stats-workflow-ai.test.ts`. Sibling fix spawned in workflow-service (make the validator reject a static body literal outside the target schema's enum) and content-generation-service (accept the DeepSeek alias at all — its `/generate` model set is its OWN, and had no DeepSeek entry on either branch, so the request was unsatisfiable however the DAG was authored).

- **An image in an investor update is UPLOADED, never linked — staff pick a file and it lands on our own R2.** `InvestorUpdateComposer` reads the file as a data URL and posts it to api-service `POST /v1/platform-uploads` (staff-gated) → cloudflare-service `POST /internal/upload/base64` → back comes the public URL, which is what goes in the markdown. An image hosted somewhere else can be moved, expire or start blocking hotlinking long after the update has landed in forty inboxes, so there is no URL field left (guarded: the composer must carry no `type="url"`). PLATFORM-owned on purpose — the file is ours, so it is billed to no customer org and the route carries no org identity. Three gates, each catching a different failure: `imageFileProblem` before the upload (SVG is refused by Gmail, Outlook and Yahoo; WebP is deliberately absent because Outlook on Windows renders through Word; 5MB cap), `imageUrlProblem` on the URL that came BACK (the public domain is resolved at upload time from key-service, so a misconfiguration surfaces as an unusable link rather than a broken picture), then an actual decode. The accept list and the gate read ONE catalogue (`ACCEPTED_IMAGE_TYPES` in `lib/investor-update-html.ts`, alias-free so it carries real unit tests). Guards: `tests/investor-update-html.test.ts` + `tests/investors-surface.test.ts`.

- **CHOOSING the file is the whole action, and the send is BLOCKED while a picked file is not in the body.** A separate confirm button stood between the two, and that is how the first real update went out with no picture at all: the file sat chosen in the form, the button was never pressed, nothing was ever uploaded, and the send said nothing. Diagnosed against prod, not the code — `body_markdown` carried no `![…]`, `html_body` no `<img>`, and R2 `investor-updates/` held only the two verification files, so the producer (which renders `![alt](url)` through a dedicated renderer) was never in it. Generalise: **a two-step control whose FIRST step looks like the action will be left half-done, and if the surrounding form can then be submitted, the omission is silent.** Fix both halves — make step 1 do the work, and let the entity's own gate refuse while the half-state exists (`investorUpdateBlocker`'s `pendingImageName`). The insertion states that it landed (the line drops below the fold of a 14-row textarea) and alt text falls back to the filename, which is what an investor reads while their client blocks remote images.
- **The update AUTOSAVES to `localStorage` (`lib/investor-update-draft.ts`, alias-free, versioned key), and the hydration latch is STATE, never a ref.** With a ref the saving effect's next pass still holds the EMPTY values of the render it was created in, decides they differ from what was restored, and schedules a write of the blank form over a stored draft — only the effect cleanup happens to cancel it, and the line sits on `Saving draft...` on a page nobody typed into. React batches the restore's four updates into one commit, so a state flag in the deps sees the restored values and the flag together. Verified by REPRODUCTION (esbuild-bundled harness + Playwright over a real http origin — `localStorage` is unavailable on `about:blank` and `file://`, which would make every assertion vacuously pass), not by reading the classes. Draft never reaches the network, which is what makes autosaving safe beside a button that mails forty investors; it is purged on a successful send, and the line says it is local to that browser. NOT a cookie: the remembered-state rule is about the FIRST frame and pays for it on every request, which a multi-KB markdown body has no business doing. Guards: `tests/investor-update-draft.test.ts`.
- **A filename the author picked becomes a PUBLIC URL, so it is sanitized at the upload boundary (`sanitizeUploadFilename`) — and `imageMarkdown` wraps a destination containing a space or a parenthesis in angle brackets.** cloudflare-service builds the object key from the filename we send, and macOS names every screenshot `Screenshot 2026-08-02 at 10.46.50.png`; a bare markdown destination ends at the FIRST SPACE, so the line emitted no `<img>` at all and the update went out displaying its own markup, in the preview and the send alike. Angle brackets rather than `encodeURI`: the body doubles as the email's plain-text part so it stays readable, and re-inserting an already-encoded URL cannot turn `%20` into `%2520`. Alt text keeps reading the ORIGINAL name, not the slug. **Generalise, and this is the deeper miss: proving an artifact was PRODUCED is not proving it WORKS — reproduce it through the real consumer.** The insertion was verified end to end (the file uploaded, the line landed in the body) and the one thing never checked was the line through the producer's own `marked`, which is where it failed. Whenever a surface emits input for another system to parse, run the emitted value through that system's actual parser in the guard, not a shape assertion.

## `apps/docs` is a STATIC EXPORT whose ROOT LAYOUT must declare no canonical, and whose routes live in ONE list

`apps/docs` builds with `output: "export"` and ships to Cloudflare Pages through `.github/workflows/deploy-static-sites.yml`, so a merge to `main` reaches it through that Actions run and NOT through the box's deploy cron. It has no server: no route handler, no middleware, no request-time API. Content negotiation (`Accept: text/markdown`) is therefore impossible there by construction, and the answer to "serve markdown" is a published file, never a handler.

**A canonical declared in the root layout is INHERITED by every page, so it tells search engines that the home page is the real document and all the others are duplicates of it.** `alternates: { canonical: SITE_URL }` sat in `src/app/layout.tsx` for months and every one of the 28 pages emitted `<link rel="canonical" href="https://docs.distribute.you/">`. Nothing errors, every page renders, the sitemap lists all of them, and the pages simply do not surface: an Is Agentic audit reported "name search surfaced no pages on distribute.you, although developer resources exist on the site". The same trap exists for any Next app whose layout sets `alternates` — a canonical belongs to the page it names and to no other page.

- **`src/lib/docs-routes.ts` is the ONE list**: path, title and description for every route. The `<title>`, the meta description, the canonical and `sitemap.ts` all read it, because those four have to agree and a second hand-written array drifts on the next page added. `docsMetadata(path)` (`src/lib/docs-metadata.ts`) throws on an unknown path, so a new page cannot ship with the layout's default title and no canonical of its own.
- **Canonicals carry the trailing slash**, because `trailingSlash: true` means `/api/brands` is served at `/api/brands/` and the bare form points at a redirect.
- **The product is named `distribute.you`, never the bare word `distribute`** — that word is an ordinary English verb and matches nothing a person searching for the product would type. A title that already contains the name opts out of the layout's `%s | distribute.you Docs` template so the tab does not say it twice.
- **`/openapi` is the fixed address for the machine-readable entry points**: `api.distribute.you/openapi.json`, the explorer at `api.distribute.you/docs`, and the hosted MCP server at `mcp.distribute.you/mcp`. All three existed before and were named only inside prose, which is not an address an agent can guess.
- **`public/llms.txt` indexes every page** with a one-line summary plus those three URLs, and `robots.txt` announces it. A guard asserts it covers every route in the list, so a page added without an llms.txt line fails the suite.
- **`src/app/not-found.tsx` is what gives `404.html` a body.** Static export writes that file and Cloudflare Pages serves it with a real 404, so the status was never the problem; the default body was the empty app shell, which tells an agent following a stale link nothing.
- **Per-page `.md` mirrors are deliberately NOT published.** The markdown exists per page as the `Copy for AI/LLM` payload, but it is heterogeneous (most pages hold a template literal, `/mcp/tools` computes it from a data structure), so mirroring it needs either an extractor over page source or a second copy that rots. If that changes, move the markdown into one module first and generate from there.
- Guards: `apps/docs/tests/unit/agent-discoverability.test.ts`. Note the docs suite is NOT a CI merge gate (only `apps/dashboard`'s is), so run `pnpm --filter @distribute/docs test` locally before pushing a docs change. (#3582)

**BEING FOUND AND BEING USABLE are two different findings, and closing the first can leave the second wide open — check that a developer resource RUNS before doing any more work on making it discoverable.** A discoverability audit reports what a name search surfaced, so a repeat "partial" reads as "publish more, link harder, name it better". Twice now that half was already shipped (#3582, #3619) and the real defect was that every resource the search DOES surface told a developer to paste something that fails: an `npx` command for a package that has never existed on npm, an auth header the API answers `401` to (it is api-service's separate ADMIN path), a TypeScript client package that 404s, a catalogue of 35 MCP tools against a server that exposes 6, and four whole pages documenting REST routes that are in none of the 185 paths the API serves. Every one renders perfectly, so nothing is red and nothing is missing; a reader only finds out at the shell. That is WORSE than not being found, and it is what a partial score is measuring.

The checks are seconds each and none of them can be done by reading the page: `npm view <pkg>` (or the registry JSON) for every package name printed; the deployed `openapi.json` `components.securitySchemes` for the auth scheme, which is the only authority on it; a connected MCP client's own tool list for the tool catalogue; and the deployed path set for every REST route a page prints (extract them with a regex, diff against `openapi.json`, it is one script). Do all four BEFORE writing copy, because each one changes what the copy should say.

**A catalogue of ANOTHER system's capabilities is read back from that system, never maintained by hand.** The tools list, the auth scheme, the endpoint set and the package names all have an authority that answers on request, and a hand-written copy of any of them rots silently in the direction that looks fine. `apps/docs/src/lib/developer-surfaces.ts` is the one home for every value a reader PASTES (header, key prefix, MCP endpoint, client configs, CLI package, tool catalogue) precisely because 22 files spelled them out separately and went stale together. A page that prints a command imports it from there; `agent-discoverability.test.ts` fails if any retired literal reappears anywhere in `src` or `public`, and if a command-printing page carries its own copy.

⚠️ **Two mechanical-sweep traps, both hit on this ship.** (a) A find-and-replace across the app REWRITES YOUR OWN EXPLANATORY COMMENTS: the `X-API-Key` -> `Authorization` sweep edited the doc comment that explained why `X-API-Key` was wrong, inverting its meaning into a claim that the API rejects the correct header. Same family as the guard-trips-on-its-own-comment rule; re-read every comment the sweep touched, and word the module's comments so they DESCRIBE a retired literal rather than spelling it (otherwise the guard fails on the file that explains the guard). (b) An import-insert `str.replace` no-ops silently when the target line differs by one symbol (`import { URLS }` vs `import { URLS, DISTRIBUTION_FEATURES }`), and `tsc` is the only thing that catches it. Assert EVERY scripted replace including the import insert, not just the body edits.

⚠️ **Two more sweep traps, from the vocabulary sweep that retired the second word for a sales funnel (#3647).** (c) **A banned word usually carries SEVERAL SENSES, and a word-level find-and-replace flattens them into degenerate English.** Replacing every occurrence of one noun with one replacement produced *"a funnel is a funnel"*, *"the funnel's own funnel"*, *"walks each funnel's own funnel"* and *"the prewarm funnels ICP -> suggestAudiences"* (that last one was a VERB). None of it fails `tsc` and none of it fails a test, so the only thing that catches it is reading the diff — budget for a corrective pass, and grep the result for the replacement word appearing TWICE in one short span (`grep -inE "funnel[^.]{0,45}funnel"`), which finds nearly all of them in one command. Classify the senses BEFORE the sweep (here: a sales funnel, a sequence of service calls, a chained verb, a blockchain, a JS operator) and apply the narrow phrases first, the generic rule last. **And scope the sweep to PROSE, because a source file's identifiers carry the word too.** Running the replacement list over a whole `.ts` file renamed a local variable and produced `const funnel = funnel.stepKeys` — a self-shadowing declaration whose every later use silently read the wrong thing. That one `tsc` does catch, which is the lucky half; the unlucky half is the same sweep flattening a doc comment into `"at most one of them is ever in a given funnel's funnel"`, which nothing catches. So run the sweep, then read the whole diff, then `tsc` — and when the file is mostly new, rewriting it by hand beats patching a mangled sweep. (2026-08-28, the leg rename: both happened in one pass over three files.) (d) **A sweep scoped by FILE EXTENSION silently breaks a pairing that spans extensions.** `static-html.ts` emits the class names that `public/landing/index-v1.html` styles and animates; a sweep over `*.ts,*.tsx` renamed the emitter's half and left the stylesheet's, so the hero console would have rendered unstyled with nothing red anywhere. Whenever a sweep touches a CSS class, a `data-` attribute, a keyframe name or a DOM id, run it over the HTML/CSS/JS in the same pass and diff the two sides for parity (`grep -o "<token>" both-files | sort | uniq -c`).

**A page documenting a surface the API no longer serves is deleted, not footnoted.** Outlets, journalists, articles and press kits left the product (the dashboard cluster went in #2196/#2593) and their docs pages outlived them by months. Deleting a page means its route entry, its sidebar entry and its `llms.txt` line go in the same commit, and the guard pins all four as absent. (#3632)

## `/model` states the acquisition model, and it KEEPS no catalogue of its own

`admin.distribute.you/model` (sidebar section Features) answers "what are all these objects, and which of them can actually be sold together". One band is documentation and everything under it is read live, which is the whole design: a local copy of another service's list rots silently, and the tell is always that the copy is where the missing entries would go (`acquisition-channels.ts` named 2 channels of 33 for months).

- **A channel states a LEG, not just what it produces.** `stepTransitions` is a list of `{from, to}`; `from: null` means the lead was not on the chain at all, which is what every entry channel does. That is what makes a chain sellable one leg at a time, and `operatedBy` (`platform` / `customer`) says who puts the hours in — a customer-operated channel spends none of our money, so its daily operating cost is a stated ZERO rather than a blank, and what such a leg costs THEM is declared per lead against lead-service. The page renders the leg and the operator; a channel with no leg reads `—`.
- ⚠️ **The root step vocabulary is `steps`, NOT `producibleSteps`, and `producibleSteps` per channel is deliberately NOT read.** It survived the widening as a DERIVED value (the legs whose `from` is null), so it is EMPTY for all 8 channels that convert an internal step — precisely the set this page exists to show. Reading it would render a dash for them and nobody would notice, which is the silent twin of the rename that took the page down on 2026-08-27. Guards pin both wire names in the reader and in the page.
- **The two reads are `GET /v1/public/channels` and `GET /v1/public/channel-funnel-economics`** — public, no auth, no org scope, so plain `useQuery` (there is no active org to gate on). features-service publishes the join itself: a channel states the first signals it can PRODUCE (`producibleSteps`), a funnel states the signal it STARTS on, and `salesFunnels` is DERIVED from the two upstream, so the pairs on sale can never drift from what a channel can do. Nothing here decides which pairs exist.
- **The step, family and operator vocabularies are read as PLAIN STRINGS, never `z.enum`** (guarded). The step set already grew from 4 entry signals to 9 (it gained `meeting_booked`, `meeting_attended`, `signup`, `form_filled`, `paid_client`) and the families gained `conversion`, both within a day of this page shipping. Both are expected to GROW — a channel that converts a step in the MIDDLE of a chain (a human closer taking `Meeting attended` to `Paid client`) has no token today — and a reader that closes the set throws on the catalogue the day it widens. `channelFamilyLabel` / `unmeasuredReasonLabel` / `unpricedStepLabel` render an unknown token verbatim rather than blank.
- **A matrix cell has FOUR states and they must not collapse into one dash**: `not_sellable` (the channel produces no signal this funnel starts on), `unmeasured` (sellable, and features-service names WHICH ingredient is missing), `unknown` (sellable, and the economics read carries no row for it — its own state, because reading a missing row as "not sellable" would contradict the channel table one band up), `measured` (the price). Prod today: 107 sellable pairs, 7 measured.
- **Every figure is rendered verbatim** through the shared `fmtRoi` / `fmtUsd`; the page divides nothing (guarded). The one derived number is `summariseCells`, a count of the cells ON SCREEN — that describes the table, it is not a metric off served fields. `dailyOperatingCostCents` renders in WHOLE dollars: it is a commercial per-day TERM we set, the same reading as a daily budget, where cents are noise.
- **`lib/acquisition-model.ts` is alias-free** (its only import is type-only and erased at build) so it carries real unit tests. Keep it that way, and keep it data-pure — formatting belongs to the page.
- ⚠️ **`text-gray-200` has NO `html.dark` remap in admin**, so it renders BRIGHT on the dark surface: the blank marker would be the loudest thing in the matrix. The remap covers `text-gray-900` down to `text-gray-300`; a quiet tone means gray-300 or gray-400. Same class of gap as the `text-gray-950` one below, in mirror image. Verified by reproduction (compiled Tailwind off the app's own `globals.css` + Playwright at 1440 and 390, light and dark), not by grepping the class.
- Guards: `tests/acquisition-model.test.ts` (real unit tests) + `tests/model-page.test.ts`. Admin tests are NOT a CI gate, so run `pnpm --filter @distribute/admin test` locally before pushing.

## The Claude config console edits a REPO, not a machine — and markdown is the only thing it writes

`/audit/config` (`audit/config/page.tsx`, rules in `lib/config-files.ts`, GitHub in `lib/github-config.ts`) reads and edits the global CLAUDE.md, RTK.md, the 26 skills, the hooks and each repo's own CLAUDE.md. The config Claude Code actually loads lives on a laptop under `~/.claude` and `~/.agents/skills`, which are SYMLINKS into a clone of `shamanic-technologies/agent-config`; admin is a container on the Hetzner box and cannot see that laptop. So every read and write goes through the GitHub Contents API against the repo, and a save is a commit. **That indirection is the design, not a limitation to route around** — do not add a filesystem path, and do not try to reach the machine.

- **A save does NOT reach the laptop until it pulls.** The page prints `git -C ~/conductor/repos/agent-config pull` with a copy button for exactly that reason: a commit that lands on the remote and never reaches the machine that reads it is the one way this feature quietly does nothing. The durable fix is a `SessionStart` hook in agent-config that pulls; it lives in that repo, so it is a separate ship.
- **Hooks and `scripts/` are READ ONLY, and that is a security decision, not an omission.** A hook is code that runs on the laptop, unattended, on every Bash call, so editing one from a browser is arbitrary local execution one click away. Cloudflare Access and the email allowlist bound WHO reaches the page; they do not make the two actions the same class. `settings.template.json` is read-only for a different reason: `install.sh` copies it into `~/.claude`, so an edit here would not reach the live settings. Everything unwritable is still fully readable.
- **The write rule is markdown-only in agent-config, and exactly `CLAUDE.md` in a product repo. It is enforced in the ROUTE, never by the UI** — a control the page does not render is still a request anyone can send, so `configWriteProblem` runs before any network call and 403s. Read is narrow too: widening it past one file per product repo turns a config console into a source browser for the whole organisation.
- **A save sends the blob `sha` it read**, so a file that moved in between is REFUSED rather than overwritten. That 409 is the feature; do not retry past it. `configFile` is therefore in `EPHEMERAL_QUERY_ROOTS` — a restored copy carries a stale sha, so every save in that session would conflict.
- **Saving a product repo's CLAUDE.md commits to its default branch, so the box rebuilds and redeploys that service** within ~5 min (health-checked with rollback, so bounded). The page states it on those files.
- **MCP servers, the live `~/.claude/settings.json` and `.credentials.json` are machine-local and unreachable.** The page says so in its own card rather than omitting them — an absent section reads as a gap someone forgot.
- Auth is `GITHUB_CONFIG_TOKEN` (fine-grained PAT) read from admin's own container env, like `CLERK_SECRET_KEY` in the god-mode routes; not a key-service platform key, which exists for secrets several services resolve. **Absent token is a first-class state**: the page names what is missing and the command to supply it. With a fine-grained PAT the repo list IS the grant, so what shows up is exactly what can be touched.
- `config-files.ts` is alias-free (real unit tests) and carries no em-dash anywhere, comments included: nearly every string in it is copy a person reads. Guards: `tests/config-files.test.ts` + `tests/audit-config-surface.test.ts`.

**A SCOPE sold by campaigns — an offer, or the brand — states `Learning` on its RATIOS while EVERY campaign selling it is still learning, and clears the moment ONE is measured.** `scopeIsLearning` (`lib/learning-threshold.ts`, real unit tests) reads the rows `useCampaignRows` already built, on the same query keys, so it costs no network and a header can never state a return that every row beneath it is declining to state. Owner-decided rule: *"show Learning except at least one of the offer's campaigns is NOT in learning stage"* — deliberately NOT "does the scope's TOTAL clear the bar", because three campaigns at five outcomes each are three unreliable prices and adding them does not make one reliable. A scope with NO campaigns is UNMEASURED, not learning: there is nothing to have an opinion about, so it reads exactly as it does today.

**The same rule states itself PER ROW in the brand Overview's Offers table** (`useOfferLearning` -> `offerLearningFor`, `lib/use-offer-learning.ts`): `ROI` and `% CAC` read `Learning` while every campaign selling that offer is, and a learning row sinks below the measured ones rather than being ranked on a return it declines to show. `$ Revenue` and `$ Invested` are never gated — a total that grows with each outcome, and money already spent, neither divided by an outcome count (there is no `$ CAC` column here). The rows come from `useCampaignRows(brandId, featureSlug, ALL_OFFERS)`: **the offer-scoped branch run once for every offer, never the brand-grain one** — that branch is pinned to ONE channel, and an offer is routinely sold through several, so an offer's single measured campaign on a second channel would be invisible and the row would read `Learning` for good. Per-offer hook calls are impossible (hooks are not loopable), which is what `ALL_OFFERS` exists for; every query key is one the page already polls, so the second question costs no network. ⚠️ An offer with NO campaigns is UNMEASURED, not learning — absent from the map reads `false`, the same default `scopeIsLearning` takes on an empty list, and the opposite of `audienceLearningFor` one level down. Guards: `tests/brand-offers-page.test.ts`. (#3778)

**One level down, an AUDIENCE clears the bar the moment ONE of the scope's campaigns has priced IT** (`audienceIsLearning` + `useAudienceLearning`). Counts from different campaigns are deliberately NOT pooled: five replies in each of two campaigns is two unreliable prices, and the table states a price per audience. The read is per CAMPAIGN — features-service answers per-audience evidence scoped to one — and every key is byte-equal to the one that campaign's own Audiences page polls, so drilling in costs no second request. ⚠️ **The default here is the OPPOSITE of `scopeIsLearning`'s**: an audience no campaign reports on IS learning (the campaigns exist and have no outcomes from it), while a scope with no campaigns at all is UNMEASURED and gates nothing. `audienceLearningFor` holds both: unsettled or no-campaigns → false, present-and-thin → true, absent-with-campaigns → true. It gates `ROI` / `% CAC` / `$ CAC` on the Audiences table and the Top-3 card's return — from the ONE map, so the card and the table cannot disagree about the same row — and never `$ Invested`.

⚠️ **The Return-on-spend gate shipped BROKEN for a day because the prop was never threaded**, and the guard did not catch it: it asserted `roi-trend-card.tsx` HANDLES `learning` and never that the page PASSES it, so the card's branch was correct while the page kept drawing the chart. The edit that added it used a `str.replace` with no assertion, so the no-op was silent. Two rules from that: assert every scripted replace, and **a guard for a threaded prop must pin the CALL SITE, not only the component that would honour it** (`overview.slice(indexOf("<RevenueOverviewSection"), …)`).

Gated: `ROI` / `$ CAC` / `% CAC` on the stat row (`economicsLearning`), the **Return on spend** line, which is drawn PROVISIONAL rather than withheld — the same curve in dotted grey, with NO plotted points and no fill under it, so a reader sees the shape their money has traced without reading it as a trend to act on (the paragraph this replaced made people read a sentence to find out there was nothing to see). ⚠️ **A placeholder curve states its SHAPE and no value, so every way of taking a reading off it is off: no plotted dot, no per-point label, NO HOVER TOOLTIP (and therefore no `activeDot`, which was only ever its anchor), and a y-axis stating only its two ENDS.** The tooltip is the one that reads as harmless — it is on demand, so it feels like the reader asking rather than the chart telling — and it is exactly the same number the dots and the labels were removed for, wearing an interaction. **A scope still under break-even gets a FIXED `LEARNING_CEILING` of 10x rather than an axis scaled to its own best day**: at 0.0x a self-scaled axis draws the flat line across the top of a 0-to-1 band, which reads as a result, while against the multiple a return is judged on it reads as barely started with break-even visible above it as the thing still to reach (owner-picked, twice: *"je ne veux pas voir cette card"* and *"quand le user est < 1x et en learning, mets le max Y axis value a 10x"*). Those ends (`learningBounds`) span the data AND break-even, so the dashed break-even line is never clipped out of the domain; a degenerate domain — every point exactly at break-even — is padded and states the one real value rather than the padding. The marked dots and the per-point `LabelList` were BOTH shipped first and both removed after the owner asked twice; do not re-add either. The hover tooltip is the one place a value is stated, on demand, which is why the `activeDot` stays. The measured chart is untouched (solid, filled, five ticks — `tickCount={learning ? undefined : 5}`), and that surviving `activeDot` needs its OWN colour class: recharts renders it outside the `<Area>`'s element, so a bare `fill: "currentColor"` there resolves against the root and came back BLACK against the grey line — measured with a Playwright probe, not assumed. Its `LearningTag` is the ONE that keeps its `(i)`, since the prose that explained the muting is gone. And on the Campaigns header both `Cost per acquisition` and `#1 acquisition channel` — the second being a RANKING BY that price, which is exactly as thin as the price. NOT gated: `Pipeline revenue` and `Pipeline generated`, per the ratio-vs-total rule. It applies at BRAND level as well as offer, because the two pages state the same money for a single-offer brand and tagging one of them would be one number described two ways.

## A brand Overview states MONEY; a channel- or funnel-scoped figure belongs to the campaign

The brand Overview answers one question — what did the whole brand return — and a brand runs several acquisition channels and several sales funnels AT ONCE. So a figure whose meaning is scoped to one channel or one funnel is narrower than the page it sits on, and putting it there labels a sum with one member's vocabulary. The campaign Overview is where those belong: it runs exactly one channel and, since #3344, states exactly one funnel.

Concretely, the brand Overview shows `Outreach` plus four money cards — **Pipeline revenue** (`headline.totalPipelineUsd`), **ROI** (`costEconomics.roiMultiple`), **$ CAC** (`costEconomics.costPerAcquisitionUsd`), **% CAC** (`costEconomics.costOfAcquisitionPct`) — charts **Return on spend** (`roiHistory`) where the one-funnel signal line used to be, and ranks its **Top-audiences** card on `projection.returnPerDollar`. It does NOT show the per-day `Outreach activity` bars (they count the emails cold outreach sends) nor the funnel step pairs (`Website Visits` / cost per visit, and the goal's outcome pair). All of it is the SAME components, prop-gated (`showActivityChart`, `showEconomics`, `showFunnelMetrics`, `showRoiTrend`) — never a second copy, or the two Overviews drift into describing one result two ways.

⚠️ **`$ CAC` reads `costPerAcquisitionUsd`, NOT the lens-only `costPerConversionUsd`.** The latter is served on a `?lens=` response only and is ABSENT on the brand Overview's un-lensed read, which is what left the card on a dash for a while; features-service pins the two equal for the same scope. Whichever is absent, do NOT "fix" a dash by dividing — neither `committedCostUsd ÷ an outcome count` nor `pipeline ÷ roiMultiple` is the figure features-service computes, and putting either on screen is the compute-a-stat-in-the-browser bug.

**`roiHistory` is CUMULATIVE on both legs, its spend leg is COMMITTED and its pipeline leg is what actually landed** (spend dated by runs' own cost buckets, pipeline by the per-lead event timestamps). The spend leg rides the same single basis `costEconomics.committedCostUsd` does, so the curve's last point reconciles with the ROI card above it rather than charting a different currency. Do not re-derive it per-period: spend on a day buys outcomes that land days later, so a period-grain ratio oscillates between 0 and absurd. A day whose cumulative spend is still 0 carries `roiMultiple: null` and is DROPPED, never plotted at 0. Pipeline whose outcome has no timestamp (`undatedPipelineUsd`) counts in the ROI card but cannot be in the line, so the card STATES that gap rather than letting the two disagree silently. `roiHistory` is fail-soft server-side (null on a blip) and null on `?lens=` — render the reason, never a skeleton.

**Every ROI figure in the dashboard reads ONE function, `formatRoi` (`lib/format-roi.ts`) — one decimal under 10×, a whole number from 10× up.** `2.4×`, `30×`. Under ten the decimal changes a decision (2.4 and 2.9 are different answers about whether a campaign pays); at thirty it does not, and it reads as false precision on a figure that moves several points a day. Same adaptive shape as `formatUsdAdaptive`. ⚠️ **The threshold is not the load-bearing part — the SINGLE source is.** Coarsening was tried once on the chart ALONE and correctly reverted: at a real 11.7 the headline read `12×` two inches under a stat card still reading `11.7×`, one number disagreeing with itself on one screen. That reverted attempt is why the helper exists rather than a convention. Callers: the Audiences table at every scope, the Top-3 audiences card, the ROI stat card, the Return-on-spend chart (headline AND y-axis ticks), the Campaigns table (`RoiCell`, which the Offers table also renders), the best-model card, the workflow scorecard, and the daily digest email. A new ROI cell, tick, headline or email token calls `formatRoi` — never its own `toFixed`, which is what a guard in `tests/format-roi.test.ts` pins. `null` means the producer could not measure a return, so the caller passes its own word for that (`-` in the two audience surfaces, `—` everywhere else); the helper never fabricates a zero.

**Where an ROI reads GREEN is the same module's `roiIsGood`, and the single source matters more than the threshold.** Above break-even the money is coming back and that reads green; below it stays the ordinary text colour and NEVER red, because a campaign that has not finished learning is under 1x by construction and red is a verdict. Three surfaces sit within inches of each other on one screen — the ROI stat card, the Return-on-spend headline and the Campaigns table's `RoiCell` — so three copies of `multiple > 1` is exactly how they come to disagree about where green starts, the same failure the formatter beside it exists to prevent. An unmeasurable return is not a bad one: `null` reads false and renders in the ordinary colour beside the caller's own word for "we have no figure". The stat card takes it through `ScoreCard`'s `valueClassName`, which defaults to the ordinary `text-gray-800` every other card wears — a card passes it only when the number carries a reading on its own, never as decoration. ⚠️ The card reads `costEconomics.roiMultiple` while the chart headline reads `roiHistory`'s last point, so the two can legitimately differ by the undated pipeline and flip green at different moments; that is the documented gap, not a drift. Guard: `tests/format-roi.test.ts` pins the rule, every caller, and the card's CALL SITE.

**Rank audiences on RETURN, never on cost per outcome** — cost per outcome ranks by cheapness, so an audience converting to nothing outranks an expensive one that pays. `projection.returnPerDollar` is the same definition `funnel-ranking` uses, so an audience's return and the brand's are one statistic at two grains.

**The Top-3 card is BRAND-scoped by default and states NOTHING but the return there** (`TopAudiencesCard`, `campaignScoped` opts the campaign Overview out). Two things were removed from it, and both are the same rule:
- **It states no brand-wide return of its own.** It used to render `brandProjection.returnPerDollar` above the rows, so a reader could see an audience beating the brand or not. That number is **PROJECTED** (conversion rates × lifetime revenue) while the ROI stat card two inches above is **REALIZED** (`costEconomics.roiMultiple`, actual pipeline ÷ actual spend) — so one page carried two different numbers under one word (prod: `2.5×` under `2.7×`) and read as contradicting itself. Both were correct; printing both was the bug. **One return per page.** Do NOT re-add a second brand-grain figure here, and do NOT "reconcile" them — they answer different questions and a card is the wrong place to explain that.
- **No cost per outcome at brand level.** `$45` per sales interest under an audience names ONE funnel's step on a surface that sums several — the same reason the brand Overview drops the funnel step pairs. A row with no projection prints `-` rather than falling back to a funnel cost.

**And the mirror at CAMPAIGN scope: the card LEADS with the cost per outcome and never states a return at all** (`ranksByReturn = brandLevelMoney`, guarded). A campaign buys ONE outcome and is run to make that outcome cheaper, so a projected return is a brand's question wearing a campaign's name — and the Audiences table one click away already leads with that cost, so a return above it made one screen rank the same audiences two ways. The cost was previously only a FALLBACK for a row features-service could not project, which meant the card flipped to `Return` the moment any row carried a projection, i.e. always. It ranks cheapest-first, its subtitle states the outcome COUNT that price divides by, and the header reads the funnel's own words (`Cost per sales interest`) byte-equal with the table's column. ⚠️ `audienceRankMetric` is still keyed on the retired GOAL, not on the campaign's funnel — so a `visit_meeting` campaign (goal `sales_meetings`) leads with the reply cost instead of the visit cost. Pre-existing, listed with the other goal-keyed stragglers below.

Guard: `tests/brand-overview-roi-focus.test.ts`. (#3349, #3355; features-service v0.127.0)

**The rule runs one level down too: the brand AUDIENCES table and the brand Overview's campaigns section.**

`customer-audiences-page.tsx` is shared by the brand route and `campaigns/[id]/audiences`, and `brandLevelMoney = !campaignScoped` is the switch. At BRAND level it shows **ROI / % CAC / $ CAC** (all three read off `projection.{returnPerDollar, costOfAcquisitionPct, costPerPaidClientUsd}`, features-service v0.128.0) sorted by RETURN DESC, and every funnel-scoped pair is off — `Positive replies`/`CPPR`, `Website Visits`/`Cost per website visit`, and the signup / form-submission / sale variants. Cost per outcome ranks by CHEAPNESS, so an audience converting to nothing outranks an expensive one that pays; that is why the default flips to desc-on-return rather than asc-on-cost. The campaign route keeps every column: it sells exactly one funnel, so its own steps ARE what it buys. ⚠️ **`% CAC` is READ, never derived by inverting the return beside it** — `100/cacPct` is on the banned-browser-math list, and the producer implements it AS the reciprocal so the two null together. It is PROJECTED (like the return) and must not be paired with `/revenue`'s REALIZED `costEconomics.costOfAcquisitionPct`.

**The pairs are keyed on the CAMPAIGN's own FUNNEL now, not on the goal — `showVisitCols = hasStep("website_visits") && !brandLevelMoney`, read off the same `stepsFor(goal, funnelKey)` the stat cards use.** The paragraph below was written when the gate was `!isPositiveReplies`, and that flag is the retired GOAL's answer: it is true for every campaign whose goal is not the reply-terminal one, INCLUDING `sales_meetings_from_conversation`, whose chain is reply -> meeting -> paid and buys no website visit at all. So a customer's reply-led campaign listed `Website Visits` / `Cost per website visit` beside its audiences for months, and the reply pair was hidden on a visit-led one by the mirror error. `reply_meeting` and `visit_meeting` both answer to `sales_meetings`, so no goal-keyed test can separate them — this is the same reason the stat cards moved to the funnel. `isPositiveReplies` and `showReplyColsForGoal` are DELETED rather than left unused, and the table's `min-w` narrow branch reads `!showVisitCols` for the same reason. Guards: `customer-audiences-table.test.ts` + `brand-overview-roi-focus.test.ts`.

**A per-outcome COST states `Learning` until ten of that outcome have landed** (`lib/learning-threshold.ts`, the same bar the stat cards use — see the UPDATE 6 block above). `COST_COL_OUTCOME` maps each gated column to the count it divides by (`cppr`->positiveReplies, `cpc`->websiteClicks, `cps`->signups, `cpfs`->formSubmissions, `cpsale`->sales), so a new cost column is gated by adding one line rather than by remembering to. Two invariants: an ABSENT stats row is NOT learning (the table already prints `-` for a row the producer returned nothing for, and replacing one honest answer with a different one helps nobody), and a learning row has NO RANK under that column — it sinks below every measured one and orders among the rest by that outcome COUNT descending, which is the column immediately beside it. Sorting them by the price they are deliberately not showing is the display-one-field-order-by-another bug. The COUNT columns and `$ Invested` are never gated: the counts are what show the bar approaching, and invested money already happened.

**Each funnel-scoped pair carries its own `&& !brandLevelMoney`, so a NEW pair added here is off by default only if you remember to write it — and the visit pair proves that is not a safe assumption.** `Website Visits` / `Cost per website visit` shipped gated on `isPositiveReplies` ALONE, which reads as a complete rule (a click is genuinely not in the reply→paid funnel) and hides the second, larger reason: at brand level a visit names one funnel's first step while the rows beside it are attributed across every funnel the brand sells. So the pair kept rendering for every brand on any other goal, months after #3358 dropped its four siblings. It is now ONE named flag, `showVisitCols = !isPositiveReplies && !brandLevelMoney`, pinned by name in `brand-overview-roi-focus.test.ts` + `customer-audiences-table.test.ts`. Generalise: when a sweep turns N sibling conditions off at a scope, grep for the ones that were ALREADY conditional on something else — those read as done and are the ones it misses.

**`$ Invested` sits right of `$ CAC` and is the one REALIZED figure in that group.** It renders `evidence.totalCostInUsdCents` verbatim (features-service, `pricing=net`, the same net spend billing charges and the same field the CPPR floor reads), sorted on the field it displays. The three columns to its left are PROJECTIONS of what the audience is worth going forward; this is what it has cost so far. That is not the one-return-per-page contradiction — the labels differ and they answer different questions — but the tooltip states it outright, because a reader who assumes one basis will try to multiply `$ Invested × ROI`.

**It renders at BOTH scopes, and at both it sits immediately left of `Outreach`** — at brand level that falls out for free, since every funnel pair above it is off there. So the campaign copy is a separate `{campaignScoped && …}` header/cell pair placed before `Outreach` rather than a widening of the `brandLevelMoney` block: moving the brand column would have re-ordered a table nobody asked to change. Two things differ from the brand column and only two — the campaign read is `campaignId`-scoped (its own cache key), and the TOOLTIP is its own string, because the brand copy says "the three columns to its left are projections", which is false beside per-outcome costs. The rest is byte-equal: same `evidence.totalCostInUsdCents`, same `col="invested"` sort case, same skeleton. ⚠️ The campaign `min-w` widths count this column — a new campaign-only column means bumping all four campaign branches, and the brand branch stays put. Guard: `tests/brand-overview-roi-focus.test.ts` pins it at both scopes AND asserts the campaign header precedes `Outreach`. (#3377, #3407)

**A FEATURE IS AN ACQUISITION CHANNEL, so `/features/{slug}/revenue` answers for ONE channel — every surface asks at the grain it PRESENTS (campaign → offer → brand), and repointing one surface means sweeping every sibling that reads the same thing.** The per-feature read is correct for a campaign, which runs exactly one channel, and wrong the moment a surface presents its answer as an offer's or a brand's: it describes whichever channel it happened to name and understates the subject by whatever the others did. Nothing errors, every figure is real, and the two surfaces simply disagree — so it is reported as "the stats don't match" rather than as a bug, and the first instinct is to look for a caching lag that is not there.

The grains and their reads: campaign → `getFeatureRevenue(slug, brandId, {campaignId})`; offer → `getOfferRevenue(offerId, brandId)`; brand → `getBrandRevenue(brandId)`; the brand's offers one row each → `getBrandOfferMoney(brandId)`. Keys carry the grain (`["offerRevenue", brandId, offerId]` / `["brandRevenue", brandId]` / `["brandOfferMoney", brandId]`), so surfaces at one grain dedupe to a single poll and cannot print different money for one subject.

**The sweep is the part that gets missed.** #3468 moved the brand Overview onto the brand and offer reads and left three siblings on the per-feature one: the Campaigns header (whose own comment said *"Brand-level (ungrouped) revenue"*), `OutreachStatCardsAuto` (which renders on every brand- and offer-scoped page, so its cost cards described one channel throughout), and the Offers table. Measured on the brand that surfaced it, whose single offer runs four channels — `sales-cold-email-outreach` $2,625.44 / 2.67×, plus $40.94, $2.24 and $0 — against $2,670.44 / 2.62× for the brand and for that offer. The customer read `2.6× / $2,669` on the cards and `2.7× / $2,625` in the table directly beneath. So when you repoint ONE surface to a new grain, grep every remaining caller of the old read in the same PR and decide each one's grain explicitly; a comment claiming a grain is not evidence of one. Guard: `tests/money-asked-at-page-grain.test.ts`.

**WHICH CHANNEL a surface is about is the same question one level down, and `useSoleFeatureSlug()` is the wrong answer under a campaign — `useScopedFeatureSlug(campaignId)` (`lib/scoped-feature-slug.ts`) is the ONE narrowing.** That hook returns the brand's single GA feature, which is right for a brand-scoped surface and silently wrong for every campaign that is not on it: a campaign is (offer x funnel x channel) and states its channel on its own row. Nothing errors, every read fires, every figure is real, and the page simply describes a channel the reader is not looking at. Two shapes, and the first is the one that reads as a missing feature rather than a wrong number:

- **A row list filtered by `featureSlug === soleSlug` does not CONTAIN the open campaign**, so anything derived from those rows derives nothing. The campaign Leads page took its funnel that way, so `panelFunnel` was null and the lead panel drew **no `Funnel progress` section at all** — reported as the status control being absent, not as a wrong figure.
- **`/revenue?campaignId=` fired under the sole slug answers for the WRONG CHANNEL's money**, so the stat row above states one channel's spend under another's name — the `$2,625 vs $2,668` shape one grain up, inside a single screen.

Three surfaces share that screen and all three now read the hook: `engaged-leads-page`, `customer-audiences-page`, `OutreachStatCardsAuto`. The campaign Overview had already been fixed by hand (`campaign?.featureSlug`) and its comment describes the same incident. Four invariants: the slug is **NULL while the campaign read is in flight**, never the sole slug as a stand-in (a read on a guessed channel lands in that channel's cache entry); the money gate under a campaign is the **channel CATALOGUE**, never `isRevenueFeature` (the brand's GA set, which blanks every campaign on another channel); a campaign's revenue is asked with **`campaignId` ALONE** so the args are byte-equal to the Overview's under the same key; and the **sole slug survives only where the question is genuinely brand-level** (`useCampaignRows` at brand grain, `useAudienceLearning`), spelled `soleFeatureSlug` so a campaign-scoped read cannot pick it up by accident. Measured on `feedback-request-cold-email-outreach`: one campaign, one offer, and a Leads page that fetched `sales-cold-email-outreach` throughout. Guard: `tests/campaign-channel-scope.test.ts`.

**Rows do NOT sum to their parent, deliberately, and that is not this bug.** Money adds across an offer's channels because a run belongs to exactly one, but people do not (a lead worked through two channels is one lead) and no ratio does (a ratio of sums is neither the sum nor the average of ratios). Across several offers the rows likewise do not add to the brand, and a campaign stating no offer is in no row at all. features-service combines the parts at each grain; summing them in the browser would be wrong on top of being the client-computed metric this repo forbids.

⚠️ **The brand's per-offer money is `GET /v1/features/brands/:brandId/offers`, NOT `/v1/brands/:id/offers`** — the latter is already the brand's offer CATALOG from brand-service, and `brandRoutes` mounts first, so a same-path declaration would be dead code. Two different questions sharing a noun. (#3473, #3474; features-service#816 → v0.140.0, api-service#855)

**The Campaigns table is a COMPONENT (`components/campaigns/campaigns-table.tsx`), rendered by the Campaigns page AND full-width under the brand Overview's chart** — never a second copy, or a campaign reads one way on the Overview and another one click over. The ordering lives in the shared `useCampaignRows` hook because the Campaigns page's "#1 acquisition channel" tile reads the same sorted list; two sorts would let the tile and the first row name different campaigns.
