Both queries keep their original keys, so the extra surface costs no network. (#3358, #3359)

**It lists PAUSED campaigns too, one line per IDENTITY, ordered status → ROI desc → last-updated desc.** A campaign the customer paused is still one of their campaigns, and a list that drops it tells them they have none — the controls modal exists precisely so they can turn it back on. What must NOT come back with it is campaign-service's history: a campaign IS (offer × funnel × channel), that service keeps every superseded row (it used to mint a fresh one on each workflow switch), and features-service totals the identity server-side — so the stored rows are many where the campaign is one. Measured on the brand that surfaced this: 1 `ongoing`, 1 `stopped` with `stop_reason: manual`, and **45 `stopped` ancestors of the ongoing one**. The old active-only filter was right about the 45 and wrong about the 1. `listedCampaigns` collapses on the identity key (`offerId|funnelKey|featureSlug`) — the live row wins outright, else the latest by `updatedAt` — so the ancestors keep riding on their live row (their money is already in it) while an identity with no live row states its paused campaign. Status leads the sort so a paused row never sits between two live ones on a return it no longer earns; `updatedAt` breaks the tie between rows with no figures at all. ⚠️ **The surfaces whose question is about LIVE campaigns read the hook's `activeRows`, never `rows`** — the "#1 acquisition channel" tile and the brand-level Leads tabs, because naming a channel or offering a funnel tab off a campaign that stopped months ago describes something the brand no longer sells. `activeRows` is derived FROM `rows` rather than from a second filter, so one collapse and one ordering serve both. A campaign's OWN Leads page still reads its own row whatever its status. Consequence: the empty state is one sentence again — every campaign belongs to an identity and every identity states a row, so the list is empty exactly when the brand has no campaign on this feature. Guard: `tests/campaigns-page.test.ts`.

**Its money block reads `ROI · % CAC · $ Revenue · $ Invested`, and `$ Invested` is the one already-happened figure among them** — `costEconomics.committedCostUsd` off the `groupBy=campaignId` group the table already fetches with `pricing=net`, i.e. net spend already billed PLUS the holds open on sends the campaign has queued, AND the exact number `ROI` and `% CAC` divide by, so a row cannot contradict its own return. Rendered verbatim (`fmtUsd(revenue?.committedCostUsd)`); a campaign with no group reads `—` rather than `$0`, because "we have no figure" and "it cost nothing" are different statements. Same field, same net basis and near-identical tooltip as the `$ Invested` on both Audiences scopes (#3377, #3407) — one cost question, one answer fleet-wide. The tooltip states the projection-vs-realized split outright, since a reader who assumes one basis will try to multiply `$ Invested × ROI`. `Revenue` was renamed `$ Revenue` in the same pass so the two money columns read as a pair. ⚠️ A new column here means bumping `md:min-w-[760px]` and BOTH `colSpan`s (7 today, after the Sales-funnel and Channel columns merged into `Campaign`) — the skeleton and the empty state each carry one, and a stale `colSpan` silently narrows those rows. Guard: `tests/campaigns-page.test.ts` pins the column order, the tooltip copy and that the cell reads the served field rather than deriving one. (#3419)

**A campaign IS (offer × funnel × channel), so EVERY surface that names one states BOTH halves in ONE place, in the same order and the same words — `components/campaigns/campaign-identity.tsx` holds that vocabulary and nothing else may re-spell it.** The funnel LEADS because it is what the campaign buys; the channel follows quieter behind **Via** because it is where it buys it. Two layouts, differing only in stacking: `CampaignIdentity` stacks them wherever a campaign occupies a ROW (the Campaigns table's first column, and every row of the budget modal at all three grains — brand, offer and campaign Overview all mount the SAME `CampaignControlsModal`), and `CampaignIdentityInline` says the same two halves on ONE line for the top bar, where a two-line crumb would set the bar's height off one crumb and leave every other floating in it. ⚠️ **There is NO separator between the halves** — a middle dot makes peers of them and they are not peers; it was removed from the crumb for exactly that. `FunnelCell` / `ChannelCell` are DELETED, not merely unused: two spellings of one identity is what this removes, and re-adding either brings the drift back. **The stacked block's height is the funnel MARK's** (`h-8` = the `sm` tile's 32px) via two leadings that add to it (`leading-[14px]` + `leading-[18px]`, the 18 being the `xs` channel mark), so a row is the height of its icon rather than of whatever the text needs; the inline layout pins BOTH its marks to `xs` itself, so the crumb lines up with the offer tile by construction rather than by a `size` prop a call site can get wrong (`CampaignTitle` therefore takes none). Measured rather than grepped, per the reproduce-the-render rule: stacked reads 32/32, and the crumb's funnel mark, channel mark and offer tile all read 18px on one 20px row. Consequence for the TABLE: `Campaign` is the FIRST column and there are no separate `Sales funnel` / `Channel` columns at any width — below `md` only `Campaign` + `ROI` render (`w-[70%]` / `w-[30%]`, released at `md:w-auto`), the four money columns and `Status` fold away, and the floor is `md:min-w-[760px]`. A new column means bumping that floor and BOTH `colSpan`s (currently 7 — the skeleton and the empty state each carry one, and a stale one silently narrows those rows). Guards: `tests/campaigns-page.test.ts` + `tests/campaign-title.test.ts` + `tests/header-breadcrumb.test.ts`. (#3526, #3529)

**A campaign is named for the LEG it performs, not for the whole funnel — `campaignLegLabel` (`lib/campaign-leg.ts`) states the arrow and `CampaignIdentity` renders it.** A funnel is sold leg by leg, so naming a row after the entire chain overstates almost every campaign and outright misdescribes some: `founder-led-closing` converts only *Meeting attended* into *Paid client*, and its row read "Sales Meeting from Conversation", which is the name of the funnel it closes the last arrow of. Eight published channels perform an internal leg and four are already fundable, since a customer-operated channel passes `channelIsFundable`.

- **The legs are WIRE data**: features-service states them on the channel's own feature row and `acquisition-channels.ts` has read them since #3638, as bare step tokens (`{from, to}`, `from: null` meaning onto the funnel from nothing).
- **`SALES_FUNNELS` carries `stepKeys` purely to JOIN against them** — the same steps under the producer's tokens, parallel to `steps` and NEVER rendered. The two lists cannot be one: the producer calls the reply funnel's first step `conversation` while the customer reads "Positive reply", so matching on labels finds nothing. A guard pins the two arrays to the same length.
- **The WORDS come from the funnel's own `steps`**, so this introduces no vocabulary at all: an entry leg reads `Positive reply`, an internal one `Meeting attended → Paid client`.
- **The funnel disambiguates a multi-leg channel.** Cold email produces a conversation AND a website visit; an in-house closing team converts a signup AND a filled form; at most one of those belongs to any given funnel. An entry leg counts only when its `to` is the funnel's FIRST step; an internal leg only when `from`/`to` are ADJACENT, in that order — a shortcut past a step is not one of the funnel's own arrows and no funnel prices it.
- **A channel stating no placeable leg falls back to the funnel's NAME**, never a dash: that is the sentence this surface read before legs existed, so a feature row predating the field loses nothing.
- ⚠️ **Consequence at BRAND grain**, where the table lists campaigns across several funnels: two campaigns buying the same step of different funnels read the same words. The funnel MARK still tells them apart and the funnel's own name is on the `title`. `lib/campaign-leg.ts` is alias-free so it carries real unit tests — keep it that way. Guards: `tests/campaign-leg.test.ts` + `campaign-title.test.ts` + `campaigns-page.test.ts`. (#3664)

**The two RATIOS state `Learning` under ten outcomes; the three MONEY TOTALS never do.** ROI and % CAC are one statement in two units, a return and its reciprocal, so they move together — showing one beside a tag would let a reader trust the number we just said we could not stand behind. `$ Revenue`, `$ Invested` and `$ Budget` are NOT gated, and `$ Revenue` is the one worth stating outright because it IS a projection: a total GROWS with each outcome instead of being decided by whichever one landed, so a campaign with two outcomes has a SMALL pipeline rather than an unreliable one, and withholding it hides a real figure behind a word about a precision problem it does not have. The test is not projected-vs-realized, it is **ratio vs total** — only a figure that DIVIDES by the outcome count is unstable at a low count. The other two are money already spent and a ceiling the customer set, neither derived from an outcome count. A learning row also has NO RANK under the return: it sits below the measured rows of its status and falls through to last-updated, because ordering a table by a number it is deliberately not showing reads as unordered.

**The count rides the grouped read the table already makes** — features-service answers the volume half on `?groupBy=campaignId` (v0.143.0, features-service#827), the same `outcomes` block it already served per workflow, read under its own names (`recipientsRepliesPositive` / `recipientsClicked`) and declared `.optional()` so an older payload still parses. ⚠️ **Do NOT reintroduce a per-row `/revenue?campaignId=` fan-out.** That was the stopgap while the group answered with money and no volume, and it cost one extra read per listed row on every poll; a consumer fanning out to a second, heavier endpoint to patch a lean group is the workaround this repo names, not the fix. **The gate is the funnel's first MEASURED step** (`campaignSignalCount`: a positive reply on the reply-led chains, a website visit on the visit-led ones) — never its terminal outcome, which needs the brand's conversion tracker live and is legitimately 0 for most campaigns, so gating on it would print `Learning` forever on a campaign that is measurably working. An ABSENT payload is "cannot tell", never zero: the row then reads exactly as it does today. Guards: `tests/learning-threshold.test.ts`.

**The `Learning` tag says a figure is withheld; the band at the top of the Campaigns surfaces says WHEN it stops — one number, in DAYS.** `lib/learning-progress.ts` (alias-free, real unit tests) reads the campaign as a reservoir: it needs about ten outcomes' worth of spend (`LEARNING_MIN_OUTCOMES` x the expected cost per outcome, the same projection the monthly forecast prices), it has spent some of it, and it spends a known amount each day. Everything else is that division. Days rather than dollars because days are what a person acts on, and the CTA follows from it: doubling the ceiling halves the SPENDING half.

- **The GATE is unchanged and stays the outcome count.** The band renders only while the campaign is learning, so a bar approaching its end can never sit beside a figure that has already been stated. Nothing here re-derives whether a row is thin.
- ⚠️ **A threshold that MULTIPLIES a price must price the SAME step the gate COUNTS — this is the general trap, and nothing goes red when you get it wrong.** The gate counts the funnel's first measured step (a sales interest, a website visit); the threshold multiplied `LEARNING_MIN_OUTCOMES` by `outcomeUnitCostUsd`, which is the cost of the funnel's TERMINAL outcome (a booked meeting) and is the figure the monthly forecast is about. Both are real served numbers, both parse, both typecheck, and the band simply promised a spend target for ten of one thing under a sentence counting ten of another — out by the reply-to-meeting rate, in whichever direction that rate happens to point. Reported by the owner as *"le calcul est faux"*, not as a mismatch, because a plausible wrong number reads as a bad estimate. `learningSignalUnitCostUsd` (`workflow-projection-choice.ts`) reads the step's own served cost off the projection the page already fetches; its precedence is byte-equal to the gate's. **Generalise: whenever a surface states `N x <price>` beside a count of N, name the step ONCE and derive both from it.**
- **The band states the number and offers the raise; it explains NOTHING.** It carried a line under the bar spelling out the reservoir (the spend target, the daily rate, the settling window) and the owner cut it: three clauses on a surface whose whole job is a glance. The lever likewise states what the raise BUYS in the band's own unit (`Invest $16/day instead of $8/day -> save 27 days of learning`), never what it would LEAVE (`about 42 days`, which makes the reader subtract), and it opens `CampaignControlsModal` with the daily budget ALREADY SET to the figure it named (`prefillBudgetUsd`, honoured only at campaign grain — at brand or offer grain there are several rows and no single one it belongs to). A control that promises an amount and then hands over a blank field asks the question twice.
- **ONE learning callout per surface, and it is the BAND — the first-outcome reassurance banner is DELETED from the fleet** (`first-outcome-reassurance-banner.tsx` + `lib/first-outcome-reassurance.ts` are gone, guarded by a `src/`-wide check in `campaign-funnel-steps.test.ts`). It promised "2 to 4 weeks" on a window it was not counting, priced that window in the funnel's terminal outcome while the band counted the first step, and sat under the band on the campaign Overview: one state in two boxes, disagreeing about what they were both waiting for. The band answers the same question with a date, so it replaced the banner at BRAND and OFFER grain too. Do not re-add a reassurance banner anywhere.
- **Cold email carries a 14-day settling tail** (`REPLY_SETTLING_DAYS`, `REPLY_SETTLING_CHANNEL_SLUGS` as a CLOSED set, never a substring test on "email"). A send bought today is not answered today, so the last dollar of the learning budget buys outcomes that appear well after it is spent. The tail only starts once the spend is in, dated off `roiHistory.daily`'s cumulative committed leg (the same basis the threshold is measured against). An UNKNOWN elapsed is read as the whole window still ahead, never as a finished one: shortening a promise on no evidence is the one direction that costs trust.
- **Any missing input renders NOTHING.** No expected price means there is no tank; no daily budget means nothing is filling it. Substituting the nearest figure we hold (total spent, the fleet average) would state a date nobody can stand behind, on a surface whose whole job is to state one.
- **The CTA offers nothing when doubling buys no day back** (`learningProgressIfDoubled` returns null at one spending day or none), and it opens the SAME `CampaignControlsModal` every other budget surface opens: several windows onto one number are fine, a second narrowing is not.
- **Every surface speaks for the campaign that finishes SOONEST, through ONE derivation (`lib/use-scope-learning-lead.ts` -> `ScopeLearningBand`).** The scope clears the moment one campaign is measured, so the honest subject is the soonest finisher — NOT the one with the most outcomes so far, which ranks by a proxy for the answer: two campaigns at the same count are a week apart if one is funded at twice the other's ceiling or prices a different step. ⚠️ The rule that matters more than the pick is the SINGLE derivation: the price, the committed spend, the daily ceiling, the settling tail and how much of it has run were assembled by hand at four call sites, and that is exactly how one campaign came to read `13 days` on its own page and `27 days` one click up (prod 2026-08-29, same $144.39 spent, same $24/day, same $44.97 per sales interest — the campaign page simply never added the 14-day settling tail, which is also why its CTA vanished). A band is a promise about a date, so two of them for one campaign is the self-contradictory-surface bug wearing a countdown. Every read the hook makes is a key the surrounding surfaces already poll: `useCampaignRows` for the campaigns and their money, the projection keyed on the FUNNEL so campaigns selling one share an answer, and the lead's own `/revenue?campaignId=` (the only carrier of the dated spend curve) byte-equal to the campaign Overview's key.
- ⚠️ `bg-purple-200` had NO `html.dark` remap, so the bar's unfilled TRACK painted the loudest thing on the dark surface, brighter than its own fill. One line in `globals.css`, added with the band, same class of gap as `text-gray-200`. Verified by REPRODUCTION (compiled Tailwind off the app's own `globals.css` + Playwright at 1280 and 390, light and dark), not by grepping the class.
- Guards: `tests/learning-progress.test.ts`, which pins the CALL SITE on both pages, not only the component.

**A number on a customer surface is explained in ONE short sentence a child could restate, or it is not explained at all.** The reflex on a figure with real machinery behind it is to write the machinery down — the basis, the window, the caveat — and each clause is individually true, which is what makes the paragraph feel earned. It is not: a reader who has to parse three clauses to learn what a bar means reads none of them, so the honest measure of the copy is whether someone could say the rule back after one pass. The learning band went through exactly this: a first draft explained the threshold, the projection behind it and the settling tail, and the owner cut it to *"affiche simplement le % ou le nombre de jours restants"*. What survived is a reservoir anyone can restate (this much to spend, this much a day, plus two weeks for replies) and the machinery moved into the module's doc comments, where it belongs and costs a reader nothing. Same instinct as the plain-language rule for explaining architecture, applied to what ships on screen rather than to what is said in chat: lead with the thing, drop the qualifiers that change no decision, and let the tooltip or the surface underneath carry the rest.

**`$ Budget` is the row's fifth number and it sits with the STATUS, not with that money block** — the four above are charges and projections of charges, and a ceiling is neither, so parking it beside them invites a reader to add it to spend. Beside the pill it answers the other half of one question: is this campaign running, and how hard. It is billing's own `(org, brand, funnel, channel, offer)` row — a campaign IS that triple — narrowed by **the ROW's own `campaign.offerId`**, never by the surface's: billing's per-pair figure spans every offer selling that pair, so a row that borrowed the pair total would print a sibling offer's money under this campaign's name, and the brand-scoped list and the offer-scoped one would disagree about one campaign. Whole dollars always (a ceiling is a configured whole-dollar value); `$0` is a real answer meaning stopped, while `—` means billing had none — different statements. The read is `["brandFunnelBudgets", brandId]`, the key Offer Settings and Campaign Settings already poll, so it dedupes to no new network, and it joins the reveal-on-settle gate so a failed read shows dashes rather than an eternal skeleton. Guard: `tests/campaigns-page.test.ts`.

**At brand level there is NO goal — only the return.** The brand goal (`salesEconomics.optimizationGoal`) is a RETIRED brand-service column: `NOT NULL` with a server default, so it reads `websitePurchase` for a brand that stated nothing (`schema.ts`: *"Nothing reads it"*), and it collapses `reply_meeting` and `visit_meeting` onto one word. Do NOT key a brand-level surface on it, and do NOT replace it by PICKING one declared funnel — that answers "which funnel", which is not the brand's question.

- **Leads tabs are the UNION of the funnels the brand's ACTIVE campaigns sell** (`leadTabsForFunnels`, built on #3362's `funnelSteps` so one chain definition serves both scopes). A campaign filters that same list to itself; a brand takes the union. `outreach` is always present and always last — every lead we contacted is in it whatever the funnel, so a brand with no live campaign still has one truthful tab. The auto-select latch is one-shot, so it waits on the CAMPAIGNS query, not the economics one.
- **(RETIRED) The brand Overview's reassurance banner** counted outcomes of every kind and retired on TIME (28 days from the brand's first spend). It is deleted: the band states the same thing with a date, and a promise nobody is counting down is worse than no promise. Its one durable lesson survives above — a surface that NAMES an outcome must also disappear on that outcome, so the sentence and the gate are one decision.
- **The brand Overview resolves NO workflow.** `/workflow-projection` answers "how many of the GOAL's outcome does this budget buy", and it fed only the Outcome line's dashed forecast (replaced by the return chart) and that spend cap. `getWorkflowProjection` / `salesObjectiveForOptimizationGoal` / `selectWorkflowForOptimizationGoal` / `workflowOutcomeUnitCost` are all off this page; the campaign Overview still resolves one, correctly.
- **Brand-level audience reads name NEITHER a funnel NOR a goal** — features-service v0.129.0 treats that as a first-class request and prices every audience through the best-returning declared funnel (`max`, never a blend), sorting on return. A campaign names its `funnel`. Each row carries its own `basisFunnelKey`, because an audience's best chain is routinely not the brand's.
- **The daily digest fires on the RETURN**: it sends only when the brand's return went UP on the day (two SERVED points of `roiHistory.daily`, never a browser-computed ratio), and names every kind that landed (`"3 positive replies and 1 signup"`), each person badged for what THEY did. A flat or falling day sends nothing — an inbox that gets the email whatever happened learns to ignore the one that matters. An absent curve means "could not measure", never "improved". `OutcomeGoal` / `fetchBrandGoal` / the goal-keyed `GOAL_CONFIG` are deleted. (#3364)

Open follow-up: `audience-stats` now prefers a `funnel` param and marks `goal` deprecated — the dashboard still sends `goal`, which features-service keeps working.

## The Revenue tab counts THREE different monies and must never merge them

`/metrics?view=revenue` (`components/revenue-view.tsx`) shows three figures that are all "revenue" in English and are not the same number. Prod 2026-08-02: **cash $10,351.46**, **consumed $5,758.88**, **committed MRR $1,980**. Someone reading two of them together will try to reconcile them; the page is laid out in named bands so they can't.

- **Cash collected** — Stripe charges minus settled refunds and lost disputes. Served ready-made by `/public/stats/billing`: `total_paid_cents` (gross), `total_returned_cents`, `total_revenue_cents` (net), and `revenue_cents` per period on `monthly_growth`/`weekly_growth`. Nothing here recomputes it. A return is attributed to the period it HAPPENED in, so a past bucket is never rewritten and a net-negative period is real.
- **Revenue consumed** — `getFleetRevenue()` → features-service, summing net actualized cold-email spend off the runs cost ledger. **Stripe is nowhere in this chain.** It trails cash by the prepaid credit not yet burned. **Refunds must NOT be netted into it**: a refund reverses a payment, it cannot un-send an email.
- **Committed run-rate** — Σ active daily budget × 30, a daily snapshot recorded going forward. It does not reach back to inception, so its growth labels say "since the first snapshot".

Two traps:
- **Every "since inception" label holds only while the producer's TRAILING window still contains the first billed day** (2026-03-12 / 2026-W11) — at its defaults the weekly window stopped doing so around Sept 2026. `getFleetRevenue` requests the maximum windows (36 months / 104 weeks) and `trimLeadingZeroBuckets` drops the pre-inception buckets. A zero INSIDE the history is kept; dropping it makes the growth line skip a period.
- **`Number("")` is `0`, not `NaN`** — `centsStringToUsd` rejects blank before finiteness, or an empty field renders "$0" and reads as "we took in nothing".

**NRR** (`retentionSeries`) is features-service-owned (v0.120.0, `netRevenueRetention`), read `.optional()`. Cohort fixed at period start, new logos excluded from both sides — the industry definition, so it is comparable to the >100 / >120 bands. A `retentionPct: null` period is UNMEASURABLE and is dropped, never charted as 0; the headline is the last COMPLETE period (mid-period retention always reads low) with its cohort size beside it. Guards: `tests/revenue-view.test.ts`. (#3243, #3244, features-service #727)

**Chart chrome (`period-compound-chart.tsx`, and any recharts chart with NAMED y-axes): NO `CartesianGrid`, and the only horizontal is the one that means something.** `CartesianGrid` defaults to `yAxisId` 0; these charts name their axes (`"value"` / `"growth"`, or `"capacity"` on `audit/capacity-history-chart.tsx`), so recharts resolves no ticks for it and degenerates into ONE dashed line across the top of the plot — a reference the chart does not have. Reported as *"cette barre horizontale en pointillé blanc dans tous mes graphes qui ne veut rien dire"*. Do not re-add it; a chart on a DEFAULT axis renders a real grid and keeps its own. On retention the 100% line takes that role (`referenceValue`), dashed, coloured from `currentColor` off a `text-gray-400` wrapper — a hardcoded hex is invisible on one of the two themes, and the `html.dark` remap does not reach an SVG `stroke` attribute. **Y ticks are ANCHORED on that reference** (`referenceTicks`, walking up from it rather than from zero) so 100% carries its own label and coarsening a tall axis can never drop it. `niceCeiling`'s steps include 1.5/2.5/3/4 on purpose: under the classic 1/2/5/10 grammar a 255% series rounds to a 500% ceiling and every bar renders half as tall as it should. Guards: `tests/chart-domain.test.ts`. (#3251, #3256)

## The feature-stats surface is THREE pages behind a SECOND sidebar — and "Outreach" is people contacted, never a run count

`/feature-stats/sales-cold-email-outreach` was one 641-line page holding two unrelated questions. It is now Economics (`page.tsx`, the cross-org price of every outcome), Cost details (`details/`, the objective-selectable trend + per-workflow cost split) and Workflow (`workflows/`, every workflow scored across all client brands), with a sub-nav column of its own.

- **The second sidebar renders BESIDE the Platform one, not in place of it.** `getNavigationLevel` leaves `/feature-stats/...` at nav level `app`, so `ContextSidebar` keeps drawing `AppLevelSidebar`; the route's `layout.tsx` mounts `FeatureStatsSidebar` next to `{children}` in a `md:flex-row`. The campaign layout looks identical and is the OTHER case — its level returns `null`, so its sidebar replaces. Adding a `segments[0] === "feature-stats"` branch to `getNavigationLevel` would silently drop the Platform nav (guarded).
- **`SidebarSection` / `SidebarLink` / `SidebarItem` are EXPORTED from `context-sidebar.tsx` for this.** Two sidebar columns on screen at once cannot each own their row markup, or they read as two products. Same reason every shared helper moved out of the page: `lib/feature-stats-format.ts` (the one money format, the null-sinking comparator, the sort toggle, the trend derivations), `lib/feature-stats-objectives.ts` (the outcome catalogue) and `components/feature-stats/primitives.tsx` (`SortableTh` / `GrowthBadge` / `Sparkline` / `OutcomeCard` / `TrendTooltip`). The guard fails if any page redeclares one.
- **Query keys are unchanged across the split**, so the per-objective trend fetched on Economics is the same entry Cost details reads, and Workflow's `positiveReply` / `websiteVisit` cost reads dedupe with the Cost details selector. Splitting a page must not multiply its network.
- **Every Workflow cell is a SERVED field — the join is a display lookup, not a metric.** `buildWorkflowPerfRows` (alias-free, real unit tests) merges three cross-org reads on `workflowDynastySlug`: `workflow-cost-per-outcome?objective=positiveReply` (positive replies + CPPR + spend), the same route at `objective=websiteVisit` (website visits + CPWV) and `public/features/ranked?groupBy=workflow` (outreach). Two calls are needed because that endpoint prices ONE objective at a time. Verified equal on the shared fields in prod (Azalea: `$209.88 / 1 click / 1 positive reply` on both), so the row cannot contradict itself. The rows are the UNION of the three sources — a workflow one source has not caught up on still shows its money, with `—` where a source is silent.
- **⚠️ `completedRuns` is NOT an outreach volume.** A workflow that contacted 638 people logs ~15,000 runs, so labelling the run count "Outreach" overstates it ~23×. The column reads `recipientsContacted`, which is what "outreach" means on every other surface in the fleet (the customer Leads page's Outreach tab is contacted leads). `getCrossOrgWorkflowOutreach` states this and the guard fails if `completedRuns` reaches the page.
- **`limit` on the ranked read is a ceiling, not a page size** — that endpoint defaults to the top 3, and the Workflow page needs every workflow, not a leaderboard.
- The table reveals on SETTLE (`isPending && !isError` per query), so one slow cross-org read cannot skeleton the page forever. Guards: `tests/feature-stats-subnav.test.ts`.
- **The Workflow page EDITS its workflows — `Edit with AI` mounts the SAME `WorkflowChat` the org-scoped editor runs, in a right-side overlay (`components/feature-stats/workflow-ai-panel.tsx`). Never a second chat implementation.** Two things differ, and both follow from this page having no workflow in its URL. (1) The context carries a **name → UUID catalogue** (`lib/feature-stats-workflow-catalogue.ts`, alias-free so it carries real unit tests) instead of one `workflowId`, because the user names the workflow in the sentence ("duplicate Ballad but swap the LLM"). (2) The instructions route a duplication through `get_workflow_details` + `fork_workflow` **explicitly**, because the platform prompt reads an ABSENT `workflowId` as "this feature has no workflow yet, generate one from scratch" — which would discard the very pipeline the user asked to copy. No DAG is inlined (the context is re-sent every turn; same rule as the single-workflow editor). The panel stays MOUNTED while closed — hidden, not unmounted — or closing it to look at the table aborts a streaming answer and loses the thread.
- **The CATALOGUE is the FOURTH row source, and that is what makes a just-created workflow visible.** All three cross-org stats reads are keyed on spend and outcomes, so a workflow that has NEVER RUN is absent from every one of them — without the catalogue in the union, a workflow created from this page would be invisible on it until its first billed run and the page would read as if the creation had not happened. It lands with every earned column blank, which is the honest reading. Its query key is `["workflows", FEATURE_SLUG]` on purpose: `WorkflowChat.onFinish` already invalidates the `workflows` root, so the new row appears with zero extra wiring — do NOT add a bespoke post-chat refresh. Guards: `tests/feature-stats-workflow-ai.test.ts`.
- ⚠️ The chat proxy bills the LLM spend to the staff member's **currently-active Clerk org** — on a platform page that is whatever customer org was last god-moded into. Same as every admin chat surface today; a platform-billed chat route is a separate backend ask.

**The same scorecard exists one grain down, at `.../brands/:id/features/:slug/workflow-stats` — and there EVERY column is THIS brand's own realized figure. There is no fleet half.** Eleven columns: ROI, % CAC, $ CAC, Revenue, Positive replies, CPPR, Website visits, CPWV, Outreach, $ Invested — all features-service fields for this brand.

- **The cross-brand benchmark used to occupy the right six columns of this same table, and taking it out is the fix, not a simplification.** Both halves carried the SAME six labels, so one label meant this brand on one side and every brand on the other, and reading a row across compared one brand against the fleet — two different questions under one heading. The fleet answer keeps its own page (`/feature-stats/<slug>/workflows`), where every row IS the fleet, so nothing was lost. `tests/brand-workflow-scorecard.test.ts` fails if `All client brands`, any `getCrossOrgWorkflow*` read or `buildWorkflowPerfRows` reappears here. The cross-org readers stay — the fleet page still uses them.
- **`$ Invested` is `costEconomics.committedCostUsd` — billed spend PLUS the open provisioned holds, never the billed-only sibling.** ROI and % CAC divide by that same number, so a spend column showing anything else would make one row contradict its own ROI. This line said the exact opposite until features-service#779: ROI rode billed-only while the `spend` block rode committed, so ONE payload answered "how much did this cost" two ways and a brand with a single campaign read **$202** on its Overview beside **$191** here. There is now exactly one basis and it is COMMITTED. The producer still serves `actualCostUsd` (honestly billed-only, so consumers had a gap-free path); nothing in this repo reads it and nothing may fall back to it.
- **Positive replies / CPPR / Website visits / CPWV / Outreach have no per-workflow answer from features-service yet** — it serves them for the WHOLE brand on the un-grouped read. They are declared `.nullish()` under the producer's own established names (`recipientsRepliesPositive`, `recipientsClicked`, `recipientsContacted`, `spend.cpprCents`, `spend.totalCpcCents`) so the columns fill in on their own when it ships, and the page STATES why they are blank rather than leaving five unexplained columns of dashes. Do NOT derive them here: a group is a dynasty folding several versioned slugs, so it would mean summing and re-dividing in the browser.
- **The brand half CANNOT be rolled up from the per-campaign grouping.** `/features/:slug/revenue?groupBy=campaignId` is the only breakdown that existed, and a workflow routinely carries several campaigns (prod brand `75d7e3e8`: 20 campaigns over ~15 workflows, `bronze-2` alone on four). Summing pipeline and re-dividing ROI/CAC in the browser is the compute-a-stat-in-the-browser bug AND would contradict the Overview. features-service owns it: **`groupBy=workflow`** (features-service #772 → v0.130.0), one lean group per workflow. A group is a DYNASTY, keyed `workflowDynastySlug` with `workflowSlugs` listing the versions folded in — the same key the cross-org benchmark uses, so the two halves join directly. Both legs read the producer that FROZE the attribution (runs-service spend by `workflowSlug`, and the `workflowSlug` lead-service froze on each `leads_campaigns` row), never the campaign row's CURRENT workflow: campaign-service switches a live campaign's workflow while keeping its id, so that would mis-attribute everything spent before the switch. Groups deliberately do NOT sum to the brand — a lead served under two workflows belongs to both, and the engine's per-organisation combination is not additive across partitions (the per-campaign grain has the same property). The api-service gateway already forwards `groupBy`, so no proxy PR — but note its whitelist is a fixed list (`brandId, campaignId, workflowSlug, groupBy, lens, pricing`) and `funnel` is MISSING from it, which is the gateway-strips-a-field bug waiting to bite a future reader.
- **The route is `workflow-stats`, not `workflows`, on purpose**: `getNavigationLevel` sends `segments[6] === "workflows"` to the featureSettings level, so a `/workflows/...` sub-route would draw the Settings sidebar instead of the feature one. Guarded.
- **The nav entry reads `Workflows`; the alpha workflow EDITOR one level down under Feature Settings was relabelled `Workflow editor`.** One nav tree with two entries reading the same word for a scorecard and an editor is a nav contradicting itself. The app-level `/features/:id/workflows` entry keeps `Workflows` — different tree, no collision.
- **`buildBrandWorkflowRows` reads the workflow key under either spelling** (dynasty preferred, versioned accepted) because the producer owns how it identifies a workflow; a group carrying neither is DROPPED rather than rendered under a fabricated name. Guards: `tests/brand-workflow-scorecard.test.ts`.

⚠️ **A field the reader's schema never declared can also make a fix look CROSS-REPO when it is one line of frontend — so probe the payload the consumer ALREADY fetches before asking a producer for anything.** The too-narrow-schema rule below is written as a rendering bug (a field reads `undefined`, a cell shows `—`), and its more expensive face is a PLANNING one: a figure the producer serves and the reader strips is invisible to `git grep`, so it reads as absent from the wire and the obvious next move is a producer PR, a deploy gate and a wave. The cheap check is one authenticated call to the exact endpoint the surface already polls, read field by field — from inside the producer's own container if Cloudflare 1010s a scripted request. Cost 2026-08-29 (#3770, the funnel leg row's price): I offered the owner two options, one needing a features-service change for a per-campaign cost, and `?groupBy=campaignId` had been serving `outcomes.cpprCents` all along — declaring two fields turned it into a frontend-only fix with no new request. Generalise: **"the producer does not serve X" is a claim about a payload, so verify it against a payload, never against a reader.**

**A field a producer RENAMED, left declared under its old name and `.nullish()`, reads `undefined` forever and nothing anywhere goes red.** This is the quiet twin of the too-narrow-schema rule above (a narrow `z.object()` STRIPS a new field): here the schema is wide enough, the name is simply dead. `safeParse` passes (optional-and-absent is valid), `tsc` passes (the property exists on the inferred type), and every guard passes — the only symptom is a cell that is always `—`, which is indistinguishable from "the producer could not measure it". The brand workflow reader carried `costEconomics.totalCostUsd` for however long after features-service#402 renamed it to `actualCostUsd`, and it surfaced only because a new column finally needed to render the value. So when a producer's own docs describe a field as *renamed from X*, grep your readers for X in the same breath, and prefer reading BOTH (`new ?? old`) so an older body still fills the column. Corollary for a renamed REQUIRED field: declaring it `.nullish()` is what hides it — a field the producer marks required should be required in the reader too, so a rename fails the parse loudly instead of going blank.

**Third face, the LOUD one: a parser shared across GRAINS must accept every grain's BODY, and a field that is required at one grain is routinely absent at another by construction.** The two rules above are silent — a stripped or dead field reads `—`, indistinguishable from "the producer could not measure it". This one throws, and the failure lands somewhere you would not think to look: `safeParse` fails, the reader throws, the query errors, and reveal-on-settle paints the section as HEADINGS WITH NOTHING UNDER THEM. No skeleton, no error UI, nothing on screen saying it broke — so it gets reported as "the stats display wrong" rather than as a crash, and the diagnosis starts at the producer instead of at the parse. The tell is a whole band of labels blank at once while the wire carries real numbers.

Sharing the parser is still right — the money block a consumer renders is identical at all three grains, so a second parser is a second place for it to drift. What has to change with it is the SCHEMA: reread which fields survive a grain change before pointing a second reader at it. Here the field is whatever names the answer's subject, and a subject name almost never survives a widening. `featureSlug` was required for as long as the only revenue read was per-feature; #3468 pointed the brand and offer Overviews at reads whose bodies carry `brandId` / `offerId` and a `channels` breakdown INSTEAD, because a feature IS an acquisition channel here and a brand runs several. Every brand and offer Overview threw for two days on `$7,000` pipeline, `2.62×` ROI and `$953` CAC that were on the wire the whole time.

**Guard it with a REAL PARSE of each grain's body, never a source-substring assertion about the call sites.** `overview-reveal-on-settle.test.ts` shipped in the same PR and stayed green: it pinned the query keys, the shared parser, `pricing=net` and the absence of browser-side summing — every claim about how the readers are CALLED, none about what the parser ACCEPTS. `tsc` is equally blind, since the property exists on the inferred type either way. `revenue-parse.ts` imports only zod and a type precisely so it can carry real unit tests; keep it that way, and add a fixture per grain plus one rotten body that must still throw, so loosening a field cannot quietly turn the parser fail-soft. (#3468 → #3470)

**Fourth face, and it lives in a hop nobody looks at: a MIDDLE service that re-declares a vocabulary an END service owns will reject values the owner accepts, and the refusal names neither of them.** The three faces above are all about a CONSUMER's schema against a PRODUCER's body. This one is a validator sitting between them. A customer clicks a control, the request is refused with a generic `Invalid request`, and both ends are innocent: the dashboard sends a value the producer's deployed openapi lists, the producer would have taken it, and a passthrough in the middle answers first from a copy of the enum it made when it was written. Nothing rots loudly — the values that were in the list when the copy was taken keep working, so the control is broken for SOME of its options and fine for the rest, which reads as a flaky feature rather than a stale schema.

Diagnose by walking EVERY hop's deployed contract, not just the two ends: `get_endpoint_details` on the producer AND on each gateway between. The tell is one service's enum being a strict subset of the other's, usually with a couple of values the owner's own description calls retired. The fix belongs in the middle hop and is a DELETION — it forwards the field and lets the owner refuse, exactly as api-service already does on the same route (`status: string.min(1)`, "instantly-service owns this vocabulary and is the only place it is authoritative"). Do NOT add the missing values to the copy: that ships the same bug with a later expiry date.

Cost 2026-08-27 (lead panel reply kind): three of the nine reply kinds the picker offers (`lead_referral`, `lead_info_requested`, `lead_meeting_requested`) were refused with `Invalid request` because email-gateway validated against its own copy of instantly-service's list, taken before v0.74.0 widened it. instantly-service and api-service both accepted them.

**Fifth face, the one-word one: `.optional()` and `.nullable()` are DIFFERENT, and a producer that declares a field REQUIRED AND NULLABLE is telling you `null` is a value it means to send.** `.optional()` accepts an ABSENT field and REFUSES a null one, so reading a required-nullable field that way parses every body except the one the producer wrote the null for. It is the loud face (the parse throws, the query errors, reveal-on-settle paints headings with nothing under them) but it reads as a producer outage rather than a reader bug, because the endpoint answers 200 with a body that is valid against its own contract. Check the openapi's `required` list and `nullable` flag together before picking the modifier, and reach for `.nullish()` whenever both are true and the consumer already spells the field `x?.y`.

It gets worse in a FAN-OUT, which is where it actually bites: a surface that reads one endpoint per member of a set (an offer's channels, a brand's funnels) throws the WHOLE read on the first member whose body carries the null, so one channel nobody was looking at blanks every row. So when a schema-mismatch is reported on a page that fans out, probe EVERY member, not the one the page is about — the failing member is routinely the one that looks least relevant. Cost 2026-08-27 (#3614, `getFeatureRevenueByCampaign`): features-service serves the per-campaign `outcomes` block required-and-nullable (null = "no funnel is wired for this channel and the leads were never read"); the reader had `.optional()`. `sales-cold-email-outreach` and `feedback-request-cold-email-outreach` both answered with a full block, and `pr-expert-quote-opportunities` — which states sales funnels and has none wired, so the channel catalogue includes it in the fan-out — answered `"outcomes": null` and took the page down.

⚠️ **Probing an internal service route as an org means the INTERNAL org UUID, never the `org_...` Clerk id the URL carries** — api-service resolves external → internal at the gateway, so a hand-probe that skips the gateway and pastes the id out of the browser address bar hands a `text` where a `uuid` column is expected. It surfaces one hop DOWNSTREAM of what you are probing (features-service `502 Failed to compute feature revenue`, whose log names a brand-service `500` on a `select … where org_brands.org_id = $1`), so it reads exactly like the real bug you came to find and sends you off diagnosing a healthy service. Resolve it first: `docker exec -i distribute-postgres-1 psql -U postgres -d client_service` → `SELECT id FROM orgs WHERE external_id = 'org_...'` (the table is `orgs`, not `organizations`). Same family as the valid-UUID `x-run-id` rule above, and it cost a full round on this fix.


⚠️ **`new ?? old` is ONLY for a RENAME — the same number under two spellings. It is FORBIDDEN the moment the two names mean different things, and a producer that keeps the old name POPULATED beside the new one is usually telling you they differ.** A rename retires the old name (it stops being served, so the fallback can only ever fire on an older body). Two live fields are a MIGRATION between two concepts, and `??` there renders one concept under the other's label on exactly the window where it matters — silently, since both parse and both are numbers. Read the producer's description before writing the `??`: "TRANSITIONAL AND REPORTED ONLY… nothing divides by it" is not a spelling, it is a second answer. Kevin, on `committedCostUsd ?? actualCostUsd`: *"ACTUAL CA VEUT DIRE ACTUAL, COMMITED VEUT DIRE COMMITED, LES DEUX CONCEPTS ONT DU SENS, NE LES MELANGE PAS."* Read the new name alone; absent → null → render `—`, which is the honest "we have no figure". (features-service#779)

## "Queued today" on the Instantly audit is the number the SEND SELECTOR decides on — a step count compared against a daily cap is a wrong number, not a rounding

`GET /internal/audit/account-health` serves two different granularities for the same queue and they are one letter apart: `queuedFirstUnsent` counts every REMAINING STEP of a lead nobody has contacted yet (a 3-step sequence contributes 3), `queuedFirstUnsentSequences` counts the LEADS. A never-started lead owes exactly ONE email today, its first; the other steps are weeks out. So the honest due-today figure — and the exact quantity instantly-service's send selection counts toward an account's daily load — is **`queuedFirstUnsentSequences + queuedNextToday`**, and that is what `queuedTodayFor()` in `audit/instantly/page.tsx` returns.

The step field is not wrong, it is a different question: it is the correct input to the partition that reconciles against `queueSize` (`queueSize === queuedFirstUnsent + queuedNextToday + queuedNextTomorrow + queuedNextLater`), which is why both survive. Mixing them is what breaks.

- **Everything that displays, sorts, buckets or colors "queued today" reads the one helper.** The column, `compareRows`, the `QUEUE_BINS` histogram and the row panel all call it — a table that shows one number and orders by another reads as broken in a new way.
- **The row panel keeps the two totals under SEPARATE headings** (`Due today` / `Queue (all remaining steps)`). They differ on purpose and must never be read as one number.
- **The two tiles above the chart count STEPS** (`queueSize`) and say so; do not relabel them as emails.
- Guard: `apps/admin/tests/instantly-queued-today.test.ts` forbids the old summation and pins a single definition of the value.

Why it mattered: summing the step field put `tatiana@fuseconnectio.com` at 102 against a 45/day cap (227% saturated) while the selector saw ~32 of 45 and correctly kept assigning it leads. Fleet-wide 29 accounts read over cap where 12 are. Every account looking saturated is why a real starvation bug (25 of 39 live mailboxes sending nothing for days) was hard to read off this page. (#3190, #3191, instantly-service#547)

## A sending DOMAIN is the unit that bills and the unit you cancel — the Instantly audit grades it above the accounts table

`DomainHealthCard` (`components/audit/domain-health-card.tsx`, model in `lib/domain-health.ts`) sits above "Sending accounts" and answers what the per-account table cannot: which domains to turn off this month. Mailboxes are sold per domain and a spent one keeps billing, so the delete decision is per domain, not per account. Both read the SAME `["instantlyAccountHealth"]` query, so they share one poll and cannot disagree about the rows.

- **A mailbox must clear `HEALTH_BAR` (95) on BOTH scores** — Instantly's Health Score AND inbox placement. Either one alone answers the wrong question: prod carries mailboxes at `98/0` (perfect health, zero inbox). Below the bar it is **dead** with an empty queue and **dying** while it still owes emails, because deleting a draining mailbox drops queued sends. **The bar between ~85 and ~95 reclassifies nothing** — the scores are bimodal, and measured over all 256 prod accounts on 2026-08-02 the two produce byte-identical verdicts. Do not re-litigate it without re-measuring.
- **The domain roll-up is ORDERED and EXHAUSTIVE** (`domainHealthState`), guarded by a test over every combination of up to three mailboxes: all dead → `To delete now`; all healthy → `Healthy`; **any healthy beside any non-healthy → `Mix state`**; otherwise → `To delete soon`. Mix is deliberately wider than "one dead + one healthy", which left `dead + dying` and `healthy + dying` matching no rule at all. `To delete soon` therefore means NOT ONE mailbox clears the bar — a domain still landing in inboxes must never sit in a tab whose whole promise is that it is on its way out (prod `axionmilestone.com`: four dying mailboxes beside one at 100/100 with 29 queued).
- **An absent score is `ungraded`, never a pass or a fail.** Ungraded mailboxes are excluded from the roll-up rather than counted either way; a domain where nothing could be graded reads `Not graded`, and **that tab renders only when it has rows** so an empty one never advertises a state the fleet is not in. Both scores are non-null for every prod account today — this is the defensive branch, and the honest answer for it is its own word, not a default.
- **Cost is MAILBOX-ONLY, and that is the point**: a registered domain is not refunded when you cancel, so the recurring mailbox subscription is exactly what deleting saves. `MAILBOX_MONTHLY_USD` carries published list prices (google $7 Workspace Business Starter annual, microsoft $6 M365 Business Basic, imap $3 Mailforge, read 2026-08-02). **A provider with no rate returns `null` for the whole domain, never a partial sum** — a partial reads as the real cost while understating it. The tab header states its own total the same way.
- **Colour is keyed on the VERDICT, never on a raw score band** (guarded): a chip coloured by score beside a pill stating the verdict is one row contradicting itself. All tints are in the `html.dark` closed set.
- Rows lead with the most expensive domain (the card exists to answer "what do I turn off"), unstateable costs last. One column per mailbox slot, sized to the widest domain in the ACTIVE tab. `lib/domain-health.ts` is alias-free apart from a type-only import, so it carries real unit tests — keep it that way. Guard: `tests/domain-health.test.ts`.

## Conversion rates and lifetime revenue belong to a SALES FUNNEL — the dashboard writes NO brand-level economics, anywhere

`brand_sales_economics` is the model the funnel model replaced: ONE set of rates and ONE lifetime revenue for every path a brand sells through, plus an `optimizationGoal` that could not tell the two meeting funnels apart. **features-service no longer reads that goal at all**, and prices its pipeline on the brand's DECLARED funnel — so a dashboard write there persists values nothing consumes. The row survives only as a legacy fallthrough for a brand that declared no funnel, and as the prefill the funnel screens seed from.

**So no dashboard surface writes it. `saveBrandSalesEconomics` has no caller left in `apps/dashboard`** — `buildEconomicsPayload`, `resolveStoredEconomics`'s payload role and `rememberSavedEconomics` are deleted with it, and `tests/onboarding-economics-write.test.ts` fails if any of the three names reappears. (`apps/admin` keeps its own copies; staff fork, deliberately not synced.)

**Why this is a DELETION and not a better restatement.** The write REQUIRED all six core metrics on every call, so a screen editing one had to restate five it never showed — and restating them from client state silently destroys what the user confirmed earlier. Cost 2026-07-29 (brand `EmailToolsHub` / `7604c385-1f02-4016-b42f-344565bcd36d`): the rates step showed the hydrated 8.4% visit→signup / 16.2% signup→paid and the user accepted them. While they were at Stripe the pending-checkout snapshot became unparseable, so `readPendingCheckoutLaunch` rebuilt it via `reconstructCheckoutOnboardingState` — which fabricated `rates: { ...DEFAULT_RATES }`. The lifetime-revenue step then PUT the full six-metric set from that rebuilt state, writing `5%` / `10%` over the confirmed values. The same reconstruction seeded a `$2,500` placeholder LTR, and since `roiMultiple = ltrUsd / costPerPaidClientUsd`, the projection printed a flattering ROI that collapsed to `0.6×` the moment the user's real `$100` landed. Restating from the WIRE fixed that instance; not writing at all removes the class.

Rules (`apps/dashboard/src/components/onboarding/onboarding.tsx`):
- **Picking the primary funnel persists NOTHING.** `savePrimaryFunnelAndContinue` is synchronous: it sets the outcome (which orders the detail screens, prices the budget step and resolves the projection) and advances. What the brand sells through was already stated one step back by `saveFunnelsAndContinue`.
- **The best-model step prices the PRIMARY FUNNEL, through the same `getBrandSalesFunnels` → `buildFunnelPatch` → `declareBrandSalesFunnel` path the funnel's own screen and the Settings card use.** Its fields come from `funnelRateFields(def)` plus the lifetime revenue, so a rate reads the same words in all three places and one funnel is priced the same way wherever it is priced. It kept an editor at all because a sub-1x ROI is almost always a small lifetime revenue rather than a bad model — a block showing the quotient and neither operand is unexplainable.
- **The old field set was the retired goal's, and it was incoherent.** `RATE_KEYS_FOR_OUTCOME[outcome]` held the ENTRY legs of DIFFERENT funnels (the meeting goal asked for reply→meeting AND visit→meeting, one from each meeting funnel) rather than the chain of the one funnel being priced. Deleted; do not reintroduce a per-goal rate list.
- **The Update button arms on a LIVE compare** (`economicsSnapshot !== modelEconomicsBaseline`), never a sticky edited latch — typing a value and undoing it must disarm it. The baseline is captured by an effect on entering the step and refreshed on write, so no entry path (funnel Continue, offer Back, a fresh page load resuming at `model`) can forget it.
- **A refusal renders `funnelWriteErrorMessage(err)`**, never `err.message` — that is the whole downstream body verbatim.
- **`DEFAULT_RATES.ltv` is `0` / `""` on purpose — do NOT re-add an invented lifetime revenue.** An LTR placeholder is divided into the fleet cost-per-outcome and rendered as a projected ROI, so it reads as a promise, not a default.
- **`prewarmStoredEconomics` stays.** The post-payment steps run on a FRESH page load (the Stripe return) and never call `hydrateOnboardingInBackground`, so the warm-up is what seeds the lifetime revenue the funnel screens prefill from. `ltvEditedRef` keeps a user-typed LTR from being overwritten by it.
- Guards: `apps/dashboard/tests/onboarding-economics-write.test.ts`. (#3039, retire-brand-level-rates)

**The run-status bar is GONE from the whole customer dashboard, and PAUSING IS DEFUNDING A FUNNEL — `brand-status-control.tsx` is DELETED, not hidden.** It stated three BRAND-level things side by side — the retired optimization goal, the brand pause flag, and the brand's daily budget — and its last home was the campaign Overview, a page scoped to ONE campaign and ONE funnel. Every one of the three was wrong there: the goal word cannot even name which of the two meeting funnels the campaign runs (`sales_meetings` covers both), and the dollar figure is billing's SUM of every funnel's ceiling, not this campaign's. Money is funded per sales funnel on Brand Settings, so **a customer stops a chain by dropping its ceiling to zero** — a brand-wide flag beside per-funnel ceilings is two ways to say one thing, and only one of them is what billing charges. Deleted with it: `setBrandPause` in `lib/api.ts` (there is NO writer left), the `api/brand-status-email` route the mutation fired, the `brand-paused` / `brand-resumed` email templates whose only sender that route was, and the already-orphaned `brand-daily-budget-card.tsx` (the second `setBrandPause` caller). **`getBrandPause` is GONE TOO, and its deletion is the lesson: a flag with no writer does not stay true, it FREEZES.** It survived one round as the first-outcome banner's read-only gate, on the reasoning that a banner must not promise results to a brand that is not running — which is right about the question and wrong about the instrument. With the control deleted nothing wrote it again, so prod held 8 rows, none touched since early August, and the banner inherited the staleness in BOTH directions: a brand marked `paused: true` in July while spending $55.69 that week was told nothing, and every brand with a funded funnel and no campaign at all read `paused: false` and was promised results from a campaign that does not exist. The banner gates on `useRunningDailyBudgetCents` now — money actually standing behind an ongoing campaign — so `BrandPause`, `BrandPauseSchema` and the `brandPause` persist root are deleted with it, and a `src/`-wide guard fails if any of them comes back. campaign-service's endpoint is left alone (its runtime gate-check may still consult it). Generalise: **when you delete the only writer of a piece of state, delete its readers in the same pass or say out loud what will keep it true** — a reader left behind reads a photograph, and it is worse than no reader because it looks like a live signal. Choosing how a brand sells, and funding it, are both the Sales Funnels card on Settings. Consequence accepted: `Restart` exists nowhere, so a brand paused by the old button resumes by funding a funnel. The no-website goal coercion (`noWebsite && storedGoal ? "positive_replies" : storedGoal`) went with the bar and is no longer needed as a display rule — it now holds by construction, since every website-led funnel refuses to be declared for a brand with no domain. Guards: `tests/campaign-funnel-steps.test.ts` + `tests/campaign-budget-once.test.ts`.

**Sibling fix, features-service v0.106.0 (#659): the workflow projection reads live economics on every request and caches only the evidence fan-out.** Before that, `servedCached` keyed the whole body on `(org, brand, objective, pricing)` with a 5s TTL / 60s hard-max and no invalidation on an economics change, so a read right after the write could still return the pre-write `roiMultiple`/`cacPct`. Do NOT re-fold economics-derived fields back into the cached body, and do NOT add a consumer-side cache-bypass param — the split (heavy LTR-independent fan-out cached, cheap LTR division live) is what makes read-your-own-write correct.

## The Sales Funnels section is what a brand STATES about how it sells, and every field on it persists per funnel

`BrandSalesFunnelsCard` (`components/settings/brand-sales-funnels-card.tsx`, model in `lib/sales-funnels.ts`) is **GA** and is the brand Settings page's ONLY sales-economics surface: it REPLACED the flat `Click Destination` and `Sales Economics` sections, whose cards were deleted from `apps/dashboard` with them (`apps/admin` keeps its own separate copies — staff fork, deliberately not synced). A funnel owns the conversion rates, the lifetime revenue and the landing page those two held ONE set of for the whole brand, so a brand selling a $200 self-serve plan and a $20k contract prices each one. It renders its own heading and offers FOUR funnels, multi-select, each with its own conversion rates, its own customer lifetime revenue and its own destinations:

| key | name | chain |
|---|---|---|
| `reply_meeting` | Sales Meeting from Conversation | Positive reply → Meeting booked → Meeting attended → Paid client |
| `visit_meeting` | Sales Meeting from Website | Website visit → Meeting booked → Meeting attended → Paid client |
| `visit_signup` | Website Purchase | Website visit → Signup → Paid client |
| `visit_form` | Form Magnet | Website visit → Form filled → Paid client |

**A funnel's NAME is what it is; its CHAIN is what it does — the two are separate fields and the card renders them on separate lines.** Before #3164 the chain WAS the title, which left the second line with nothing to say and made every card read as a wire diagram. The name leads (`text-sm font-medium text-gray-900`), the chain sits under it quieter (`text-xs text-gray-500`), and a `h-11 w-11` icon tile runs alongside BOTH (44px vs the 42px the two lines occupy — a tile sized to the title alone reads as decoration).

**There is no primary funnel.** Several run at once and none outranks another; which one a campaign optimizes for is a campaign concern, not a settings one. `tests/sales-funnels.test.ts` fails if `primary` / `Primary` / `StarIcon` reappears in the card.

**Choosing a funnel, and dropping one, are decisions about how the brand sells — so NEITHER is a checkbox.** There is no checkbox in the card at all (guarded). A closed card is opened by clicking it ANYWHERE: a `role="button"` div wraps the whole header with `tabIndex` + Enter/Space, deliberately not a `<button>`, because the form it reveals contains its own buttons and a nested button is invalid HTML. The choice itself is then a named button inside the open form — `OK` / `Update` to take the funnel, **`Remove this funnel`** to drop it — with the action row right-aligned on desktop (`sm:flex-row sm:items-center sm:justify-end`) and stacked on mobile. **One card is open at a time** (`openKey`), because the list reorders itself around the selection and several open forms would move under the cursor. Verified by reproduction rather than by reading the classes: `document.elementFromPoint` at six points across a closed card (padding, icon tile, name, chain, empty space, the Selected tag) all resolve inside the clickable container at 1280px and on a Pixel 7, and Enter + Space each fire it.

**The chosen funnels render FIRST, then the rest, as two groups** (`partitionFunnelsBySelection`, pure and unit-tested). A chosen funnel carries a green `Selected` pill on the right (`bg-green-50 text-green-700`); the rest sit under a quiet `Not selected` heading on `bg-gray-50` with a `text-gray-500` name and a dimmed tile, so the two sets read apart at a glance. The heading only renders when both groups exist — labelling a list where nothing is chosen yet is noise. The hover tint differs per group (`hover:bg-gray-50` on a white card, `hover:bg-gray-100` on the gray ones), or an unselected card would show no response to the cursor.

**A funnel that is neither chosen nor open shows its name and its chain, and NO numbers** (`showNumbers = state.declared || isOpen`, guarded). An undeclared funnel's rates and lifetime revenue are a prefill nobody has confirmed, and printing them on a row the brand never picked reads as a claim about how it sells.

**The chain is modelled as `steps` + `legs`, one leg per arrow, every arrow priced.** `legs.length === steps.length - 1` is guarded. `funnelLegPct` prints the rate under its arrow once the funnel is chosen or open, and returns `null` for a rate the brand has not filled — a rate we do not have prints nothing rather than a zero. **`Meeting booked → Meeting attended` is the show-up rate, and brand-service stores it ON THE FUNNEL and nowhere else in the fleet** — so `meetingBookedToAttendedPct` saves like every other leg but is the one `SeedlessFunnelRateKey`: `isSeedableRateKey` sends it down a branch that starts it BLANK on every brand instead of indexing `BrandSalesEconomics` (which has no such column). Rate labels live once in `RATE_FIELDS` and funnels point at them by key, so two funnels sharing `meetingToClosePct` cannot drift into two labels for one stored number.

**The lifetime revenue closes the CHAIN, after `Paid client`** (`funnelLifetimeLabel` → `$2,500 lifetime revenue`), not a line of its own: it is what the last step is worth, so it reads in the same sequence. It is dropped when absent or zero.

**It WRITES, through brand-service's own per-funnel routes and nothing else** (`getBrandSalesFunnels` / `declareBrandSalesFunnel` / `undeclareBrandSalesFunnel` in `lib/api.ts`, proxied by api-service under `/v1/brands/:id/sales-funnels`). `OK` / `Update` declares the funnel and prices it; `Remove this funnel` undeclares it, and its economics go with the declaration. The CTA is `OK` on first declare and `Update` after (`state.declared`), never `Save`. **Do NOT wire `saveBrandSalesEconomics` / `saveBrandClickDestination` here** (guarded): those hold ONE lifetime revenue and ONE destination for the whole brand, so they would take four funnels' values and persist one of each — the per-funnel model collapsing back into the brand-level one.

**The write is a PARTIAL patch diffed against what is stored** (`buildFunnelPatch(def, draft, saved)`), because brand-service leaves an omitted field as stored and CLEARS an explicit `null`. Three things follow, all guarded in `tests/sales-funnels.test.ts`: editing one rate sends only that rate, so a field confirmed elsewhere is never overwritten from a stale copy; emptying a field sends `null` and really clears it, so nothing is validated as "required" client-side any more (`validateFunnelDraft` is shape-only — a blank value is legal everywhere); and the patch can never carry a rate outside the funnel's own chain or a destination the funnel has no use for, both of which brand-service 400s. `sameUrl` compares what two destinations would NORMALIZE to, or every save would re-send an unchanged URL.

**A goal the catalogue prices under a LONGER chain is translated, never read leg by leg** (`orphanGoalSeedFor`). `positive_replies`, `website_visits` and `sales` price ONE end-to-end step; the funnel spells the same journey over several legs. So the brand's rate seeds the LAST leg (the one landing on `Paid client`) and every leg above it seeds at **100%** — the product across the chain then equals exactly what the brand gave us. `positive_replies` → `reply_meeting`, `website_visits` → `visit_signup`, `sales` → BOTH (each from its own rate); `signups` / `sales_meetings` / `form_submissions` / `website_purchase` are absent because a funnel already prices their legs by name. Reading those legs by name CANNOT work: brand-service stores every blended rate NOT NULL with a server default, so a leg the goal never configured reads back a plausible number the brand never stated. Guarded in `tests/sales-funnels.test.ts`, including that the chain multiplies back to the stated rate.

**The prefill is for a person to confirm and is never written on its own.** An UNDECLARED funnel seeds from the brand's blended economics + click destination (`funnelDraftFromBrand`); a DECLARED one shows its own stored values (`funnelDraftFromDeclared`, `null` → blank, never a zero). Because the patch omits any field still equal to what is stored, and an undeclared funnel's stored state is `NOTHING_DECLARED`, a prefilled field the user left alone is never persisted — brand-service has no defaulting layer, so a value it never received reads back `null`, which is how a consumer knows not to rank on it. Turning that into "the brand told us" is worse than the preview was. **The booking link still starts EMPTY** — nothing seeds one, and borrowing the click destination would put a URL on screen the brand never gave us.

**`declared` is read BEFORE `funnels`, and the two empty answers never collapse.** `declared: true` with no funnels means the brand STATED it sells through none; absent-or-false means it has never told us anything, and the card says so in different words. It is `.optional()` in the reader only because an older brand-service serves the set without the flag; an absent flag reads as "never stated". Undeclaring the LAST funnel does not un-state the set (brand-service keeps the marker), so a brand that dropped everything has still answered.

**A refusal from brand-service is shown, not swallowed** (`funnelWriteErrorMessage`): a 400 renders `body.error`, which is a sentence brand-service wrote for a person ("this funnel starts with a click onto the brand's website…"); 403/404 get their own line; anything else gets one generic line. **Never `err.message`** (guarded) — `apiCall` sets it to the whole downstream body verbatim. Do NOT re-implement brand-service's validation as the source of truth; the client checks exist to make typing pleasant and the server's 400 is the answer.

- **A booking link is OPTIONAL wherever a meeting sits in the chain** (`bookingLink: true` on both meeting funnels), and a page destination only where a click onto the site starts it (`pageDestination`). A brand that books over email still runs the funnel, so `validateBookingUrl("")` returns ok; a malformed one is still rejected. These are two independent fields on `FunnelDraft`, not one `kind`-switched destination — `visit_meeting` legitimately has both.
- **The line under the chain is `funnelDestinationChips`: each destination as its own favicon (`BrandLogo`, logo.dev) beside `shortUrl(...)`** rather than the raw link. A destination the brand never gave us is DROPPED, not printed empty. **`shortUrl` cuts everything from the first `?` or `#`** and the chip is width-bounded + `truncate`: a real click destination carries a UTM tail (`…/level-1-free-assessment/?utm_source=landing_page&utm_medium=email&utm_campaign=…`) long enough to fill the row on its own, and none of it identifies the page. The path stays, because that IS what distinguishes one destination from another.
- **Icons are Phosphor duotone** (`@phosphor-icons/react`, imported per-icon from `dist/csr/<Name>` so the 190KB barrel never enters the bundle; the non-deprecated export is `<Name>Icon`). Heroicons is a single-weight utility set and read as flat inside a colored tile; duotone carries a tinted fill under its stroke in the same `currentColor`, so one `text-<tone>-600` class drives both layers. `ChatsCircle` / `CalendarCheck` / `ShoppingCartSimple` / `Magnet`. The four tints (purple / indigo / blue / orange) are all in the `html.dark` closed set, guarded.

- A booking link is a scheduling page on a third-party domain (Cal.com, Calendly), so it is validated as a plain URL (`validateBookingUrl`), NOT through `validateDestination`'s on-brand-domain check. Conflating the two rejects every real booking link.
- `["brandSalesFunnels", brandId]` is in `PERSISTABLE_QUERY_ROOTS` — an unlisted root is default-OFF and the card cold-skeletons on every visit. The declare response carries ONE funnel, so `cacheDeclared` merges it into the cached set in catalogue order; the undeclare response IS the remaining set and is written whole.
- **A funnel carries the MONEY it is funded with, and that money is billing's, not brand-service's.** The card composes two reads — the declaration and its economics from brand-service, the daily ceiling from billing (`getBrandFunnelBudgets`) — and makes two writes on one button. The ceiling stays OUT of `FunnelDraft`: that draft is exactly what brand-service's partial patch reads, and it has no column for money. It is written only when it MOVED, and BEFORE the nothing-changed exit, because a budget edit alone is a real change even when the economics are untouched. The green tag states the funded ceiling rather than merely that the funnel was picked — the money IS the selection now — and a declared funnel at zero reads `Not funded` instead of a green tag claiming it runs. Guards: `tests/sales-funnels.test.ts`.
- **The per-funnel floor governs what a brand may NEWLY state, never what one has already been running — so `funnelBudgetBelowMinimum` reads the STORED ceiling, not the typed value alone.** Per-funnel ceilings shipped after the money did, and billing's single-funnel attribution sweep carried each brand-level ceiling over verbatim rather than raising anyone's spend, so live brands sit under their funnel's floor right now (prod: a reply-to-meeting funnel at $8/day against a $24 floor, charged daily). Checking the typed value alone left those brands two moves — leave it alone, or defund it — and refused the raise, which is the wrong direction to block. It also refused an edit to a **conversion rate** on a funnel whose money nobody was touching, because the gate runs before the whole form saves. A funnel billing funds under its floor is therefore grandfathered: keep it, raise it (even to another figure under the floor), or drop it to zero; lowering it to another funded figure under the floor stays refused, and reaching the floor spends the grandfather through the ordinary rule rather than a branch of its own. The refusal and the tip beside the field (`funnelBudgetFloorMessage` / `funnelBudgetTip`) name what the funnel may actually do instead of a floor it is not allowed to walk down to. **Onboarding passes a stored ceiling of `0` explicitly** — signup is a brand stating its budgets for the FIRST time, so the floor applies in full and no sub-floor ceiling can be created there. billing-service holds the same rule and its 400 is what decides. ⚠️ **The grandfather RATCHETS: it measures against what is stored right now, so a brand that raises $8 to $10 can no longer return to $8, and going through 0 does not help (0 clears the grandfather, so $8 reads as a fresh sub-floor statement). Only a direct DB write gets it back — do not "just test" a raise on a real customer's funnel.** (#3339, billing-service #354 → v0.62.1)

**The funnel key is becoming the fleet's ONE vocabulary, and it is the RICHER word — a surface naming what something buys reads the funnel key, never the goal.** `reply_meeting` and `visit_meeting` both collapse onto `meetingBooked`, so the goal cannot name a chain on its own; the eight-token goal set is being retired into four funnel tokens the owner fixed verbatim: `form_magnet`, `sales_meetings_from_conversation`, `sales_meetings_from_website`, `website_purchases` (renames of `visit_form` / `reply_meeting` / `visit_meeting` / `visit_signup`). `normalizeSalesFunnelKey` (`lib/sales-funnels.ts`) already reads BOTH spellings ahead of brand-service emitting the new ones, same discipline as `CANONICAL_GOALS`. A campaign carries `funnelKey` on the wire and the Campaigns table reads it and **NOTHING else — there is no goal fallback anywhere, and `primaryFunnelForGoal` was deleted rather than left unused** (`lib/campaign-funnel.ts` exports exactly one function, guarded). Deriving a funnel from a goal prints a chain the campaign never stated, and `meetingBooked` proves it: two funnels answer to it.

**Creating a campaign STATES the funnel it sells — `funnelKey` is REQUIRED on both `createCampaign` signatures, and a feature that sells through no sales funnel states an explicit `null`.** campaign-service 400s a sales campaign that states none: the funnel is what the campaign is paced on (its own daily ceiling in billing) and priced on, so a campaign without one is what left customers funding a funnel and never getting a campaign for it. Required-not-optional is the point — it makes a new caller answer the question instead of quietly shipping a null.
- **Onboarding states a funnel the customer just FUNDED** (`lib/launch-funnel.ts` `fundedLaunchFunnelKey`, alias-free so it carries real unit tests), read off the SAME per-funnel budget map billing was written from a few lines above, so the campaign and its ceiling can never name different funnels. The pick never leaves the funded set and is DETERMINISTIC — the primary funnel when it was funded, else the first funded funnel in catalogue order — because a retry that picked differently would create a second campaign for a second funnel.
- **Several funded funnels create ONE campaign here**, not one per funnel: campaign-service provisions the rest, one per funded funnel, on its next tick. Do NOT loop the client over the funded map.
- **Nothing funded STOPS the launch** rather than defaulting. A funnel nobody funded is neither paced nor priced, so inventing one is worse than the error.
- **Staff state it explicitly**: a picker on both admin create paths (`lib/sales-funnel-keys.ts`, the canonical four), required when `isRevenueFeature(featureSlug)` and absent otherwise; it rides the pending-checkout blob because the resume path creates the campaign on a fresh page load. A relaunch carries `campaign.funnelKey` off the row it relaunches.
- **Never derive it from a goal** (guarded) — same reason as the vocabulary rule above. Writes go out in the canonical spelling (`canonicalSalesFunnelKey`) while reads stay tolerant of both.
- The api-service create proxy re-declares the body, so a new field on this call needs the gateway to forward it before it reaches campaign-service. Guards: `apps/dashboard/tests/launch-funnel.test.ts` + `apps/admin/tests/campaign-funnel-key.test.ts`. (#3344, campaign-service #316)

**A brand that has been running outreach for months KEEPS THAT CAMPAIGN when it funds its funnels — campaign-service adopts it into the funnel it was already running instead of parking it and provisioning an empty twin.** The "superseded" state is gone from the fleet, front and back; nothing renders it, nothing computes it, and no campaign is alive-but-never-given-a-turn. It shipped as a 15-minute defer on the keyless pre-funnel campaign plus a new campaign for the same funnel, so the staff table showed two rows on one channel and one funnel: the row holding 100% of the history (11.6×, $1,177) read dead, and the row that read live had produced nothing.
