A grep of `src/` only misses the served HTML (sign-up buttons ×27, hero, `<meta>`/OG/twitter, JSON-LD, animated stat-counter, price-checks). **`/` serves `index-agency.html`, `/pro` serves `index-pro.html`, `/agency` serves `index-agency.html`** — the default homepage is the AGENCY variant, so verify `/` against `index-agency.html` (the variants drift: #2032 found agency's `Organization` JSON-LD missing `sameAs`/`legalName` while pro+index had them). **GEO/AI-search surfaces are part of the sweep too: `apps/landing/public/llms.txt` (curated index) AND the inline JSON-LD (`SoftwareApplication.description`, WebSite description).** Also sweep `apps/docs/src` + `shared/content/src`. When reframing a word/claim across the landing, `grep -rl '<term>' apps/landing/public apps/landing/src apps/docs shared/content`. Watch for a SECOND meaning of the same token. (#1516, #2032)

**Product is COLD EMAIL ONLY — NOT multi-channel.** Do NOT describe distribute as running PR / hiring / VC / journalist / podcaster / influencer / Reddit Ads / Google Ads as product channels. `llms.txt` + JSON-LD `SoftwareApplication.description` drifted multi-channel for months (#2032 de-channelled them). The product is cold email outreach done for you (agency-domain model). The cold-email-vs-linkedin SEO pages discuss "multichannel" as a topic — that's fine content, NOT a product claim. (Kevin 2026-06-22: "We are not multi channel.") **The React LEGAL pages `apps/landing/src/app/terms/page.tsx` + `privacy/page.tsx` are part of the positioning sweep too — they drift independently of the served HTML/llms.txt/JSON-LD and were the multi-channel STRAGGLER (Terms §3 Description, §5 Outreach, §8 third-party still listed PR/VC/hiring/journalist + LinkedIn/X/social months after #2032 de-channelled everything else; de-channelled #2084). Grep them on any positioning reframe.** (#2084)

**The PRODUCT is a productized ACQUISITION AGENCY that sells SALES MEETINGS at a MEASURED cost. Cold email is the CHANNEL, never the category — describing distribute by its channel on any public surface files it under tooling and is a positioning bug.** The rule above ("cold email only, not multi-channel") is about which CHANNELS we claim; this one is about what we SELL. Both hold. Concretely, on every public surface (`meta`, `og`, JSON-LD `SoftwareApplication.description`, FAQ, footer, `llms.txt`): the offer is named **Autonomous Sales Meetings Acquisition**, we are an agency that runs the campaign on the client's behalf, and the deliverable is an interested reply from a buyer. Never "a cold email tool", never "done-for-you cold email outreach" as the self-description. Three facts that must survive any rewrite:
- **The cost per meeting is MEASURED, never QUOTED.** We invest the client's budget through paid APIs, take a margin, and report what each interested reply actually cost. The only legitimate figures are the live cross-client rate (`__CAC_PRICE__`) and the client's own stats. NEVER write "predictable cost", "a cost you know in advance", or any phrasing implying a rate card. The differentiator is that we are the ones who measure it, not that we can promise it.
- **THE COST IS THE CLIENT'S, NEVER OURS. This is the one that gets inverted.** The client authorises a daily budget cap and is charged **the budget the campaign spent, and nothing else** — one number, no breakdown. Our margin (fixed, 75%) sits INSIDE that spend and is invisible to them. So every "$X per interested reply" on any surface is **a price the client pays**, and we have no cost-per-meeting to show a buyer. Banned: "what a meeting costs us", "our cost per reply", "we deliver a reply at $X" (reads as our rate). Correct: "what it cost you", "what our clients are paying", "a positive reply costs $X". Equally banned in the other direction: **"at cost", "pass-through", "no markup", "you only pay for X"** — all false, the margin is in there. "You pay the budget you authorised, nothing else" is true and is the actual selling point (no seat, no retainer, no subscription). Cost 2026-07-29 (#3059 → #3064): shipped the ROI calculator headlined "against what a meeting **costs us**"; Kevin: "non ce n'est pas cost us, c'est cost au client!!! Mais tu me fais peur… reformule le business model." An audit found exactly one offending phrase page-wide (mine) — the rest already said "cost you" — but the inversion was live on the homepage. Guarded by `homepage-offer-story.test.ts` (prose-only: a whole-file `margin` check fires on the inline stylesheet's `margin:` declarations).
- **Do NOT qualify the live rate with "best model" / "best rate" / any model vocabulary.** Every client is served the best model, so the number needs no qualifier — it is simply what it costs. A "best rate" hedge is worse than useless: it invites "so which one will I get?" for nothing, and it leaks the workflow-model detail the landing deliberately hides (Kevin 2026-07-29: "ne rentrons pas dans le détail du meilleur model blabla. On donnera au client le meilleur model de toute façon"). The producer-side rule that the fleet figure is cross-org + BEST workflow (never pooled) is unchanged — it just never surfaces as words on the page.
- **The software is OPTIONAL.** A client who never wants to log in can send their website and we set the whole thing up at the same price, with interested replies arriving in their normal inbox. This is a load-bearing objection-killer; do not let a rewrite make the dashboard sound mandatory.
- **The guarantee is on the MEASUREMENT, not the result** (owner-picked, 2026-07-29). Copy may commit to: the real cost always visible, the whole picture always visible, spend always controlled by a daily cap and an immediate pause, and our own numbers published. It may NOT promise meetings. The FAQ's "results are not guaranteed" stays.
(Kevin 2026-07-29: "on vend du sales meeting. A du cout predictible. On est des experts du CAC" then the correction "les gens ne connaissent pas leur CAC exactement en avance… la seule chose qu'on peut donner ce sont les moyennes des autres clients et leurs propres stats".)

**Homepage (`index-v1.html`) is an OFFER ARGUMENT, one idea and one PRO VISUAL per section, in story order — do NOT re-add a text-only section.** Rebuilt #3051 after an audit against the Hormozi value equation the product itself sells to clients: 5 of the 8 sections had been pushing perceived-likelihood, time-delay had NO section, and the value stack was a list with no prices. Current order and what each section OWNS: hero (dream) · `#testimonials` (the real dream, Doc Dinners **$50 per sales meeting, 10x ROI**) · `#proof` (price anchor vs the $700 market meeting) · `#measure` (per-segment cost of acquisition, dashboard preview) · `#engine` (**the split-and-test tree** — sub-segments by geography/size/role, A/B angles inside each, Hormozi-built templates) · `#how` (30-second setup) · `#speed` (time before the first email) · `#stack` (what's included, priced against documented market rates) · `#pricing` (what you risk + the measurement guarantee). Rules when editing:
- **`#engine` is the section that justifies the price.** It was absent from the site for months while being the entire reason a reply costs what it does. Never delete it to "simplify".
- **A number shown twice on the page must come from ONE token.** The `#measure` dashboard headline reads the same `__CAC_PRICE__` as the hero, so the two cannot diverge. Do not hardcode a second copy.
- **`#measure`'s three segment rows read that same rate, and their BAR LENGTHS are decorative — leave them at `38% / 61% / 92%`.** Each row states its own cost per reply (`__SEG_COST_LOW__` / `_MID__` / `_HIGH__`, built by the unit-tested `segmentCostBand` as the live rate ∓ $5, low leg clamped above zero) — a hardcoded price there would freeze while the headline kept moving, so the same card would end up disagreeing with itself. The bars are NOT a scale for those prices, and making them proportional was considered and rejected (owner-picked): at a ±$5 spread the three would render nearly identical and the row would lose its shape. So this is the case where a length that does not track the number beside it is intentional, NOT the self-contradictory-surface bug — do not "fix" it. Guarded by `segment-cost-band.test.ts`. (#3134)
- **Market-rate figures are only the DOCUMENTED bands** (agency $1,500-5,000/mo + $80-200 per qualified reply, SDR $4,000-7,000/mo + $50-120, $700 per booked meeting, 2-4 weeks DIY setup, 3-6 months SDR ramp). There are no documented per-TOOL prices, so the stack prices the ALTERNATIVES, not the individual tools. Do not invent a price for "a lead database" or "a warmup tool".
- **This page carries its CSS INLINE and links no `css/styles.css`**, so the `?v=N` cache-buster rule does not apply to it (it does apply to its sibling static pages). Guarded by `homepage-offer-story.test.ts`.
- The four-model comparison grid (`#category`) and the post-launch step list were deleted as text-only sections carrying no idea of their own. The nav's "Why distribute.you" now points at `#engine`. (#3051)

**Time-to-first-interested-reply on the landing = `__FIRST_REPLY_LINE__`, an OBSERVED CEILING that REMOVES ITSELF below a sample floor.** Source is the already-proxied public endpoint `GET /v1/public/features/workflow-engagement-latency?featureSlug=&groupBy=workflow` (per-workflow `timeToFirstPositiveReply.{medianMs,sampleSize}`); the pure phrase builder is the exported, unit-tested `firstReplyCeilingPhrase` in `static-html.ts`. Four rules it encodes, all load-bearing:
- It reports the **MAX of the per-workflow medians** phrased as an upper bound ("in under a week"), never an average and never the best model's figure. A single fast model is not a promise; the ceiling is what a buyer can rely on.
- A workflow with `sampleSize: 0` is DROPPED, same husk filter as `fetchBestModelUsd` — a model that produced no interested reply has no latency to report.
- Below the floor (`MIN_FIRST_REPLY_MODELS = 2`, `MIN_FIRST_REPLY_SAMPLE = 5`), or when the fetch fails, the phrase is `null` and **the whole sentence is removed from the page**. Do NOT add a fallback figure: "we could not measure this" and "it took N days" are different statements.
- Fetch bounded `AbortSignal.timeout(8_000)` + gated on the token being present, like every other build-time landing fetch, so a cold endpoint cannot blow the prerender budget.
⚠️ **`timeToFirstLinkClick` is NOT usable as-is**: six workflows report medians within 0.3% of exactly 3.00 days, which is a scheduled-followup artifact rather than organic latency. Do not put click latency on a public surface until that is explained. (#3051)

**OPENS ARE DEPRECATED — distribute no longer MEASURES nor COMMUNICATES open rates.** Apple Mail Privacy Protection made opens meaningless, so open-rate / "opened" / opens-count are DEAD metrics: NEVER show an open rate, an "Opened" funnel step, an opens KPI, or any opens number on ANY surface (landing, dashboard, stats, OG, JSON-LD). The success funnel is Sent → Click → Positive reply; the headline rate is the POSITIVE-REPLY rate. The `__OPEN_RATE__` / `__OPENED_PER_HUNDRED__` tokens + the `openRate*` / `opened*` fields in `apps/landing/src/lib/static-html.ts` are LEGACY — do not add new uses (existing ones are being removed). SEO articles about subject lines/opens must reframe around reply rate, not open rate. (Kevin 2026-07-12: "on ne mesure ni ne communique plus sur les opens".)

**NO INVENTED PEOPLE AND NO INVENTED NUMBERS on any public surface — a landing figure is either a LIVE server-injected token, a sourced third-party benchmark, or a real named customer.** Fabricated testimonials + fake campaign data shipped on the landing for months and were being scraped back by LLMs as if they were real customer results: 6 invented personas (Sara K. / Marcus T. / Priya N. / Tom H. / Alex R. / Julia M.) on the `/performance` proof slider, 3 of the same on `/use-cases` plus per-persona campaign data (a `solo.fm` funnel 47/6/4/3, a Monday→contract agency timeline, freelancer KPIs 4 calls/$25/$6.25, a wholesale grid 2 accounts/$34/3.1%/+72%) and hardcoded stats bands. Removed in waves (archive `/v2` + the freelancer card first, then `/performance` + `/use-cases` in #3030). Rules:
- **The live tokens work on ANY statically-served page**, not just `/performance` — `withLivePerformanceMetrics` (`static-html.ts`) is gated only on the token being PRESENT in the html. Available: `__BEST_POSITIVE_REPLY_COST__` (+ `_NUMERIC__`), `__POSITIVE_REPLY_RATE__` (+ `_NUMERIC__`), `__EMAILS_SENT__` (+ `_NUMERIC__`), `__POSITIVE_REPLIES_PER_HUNDRED__` (+ `_NUMERIC__` / `_RANGE__` / `_BAR_NUMERIC__`), and the CAC family `__CAC_PRICE__` / `__CAC_MULT__` / `__CAC_BOOT_SLOT__`. A new page that needs a number uses these, never a literal.
- **A hardcoded literal that happens to match a live value is still a bug** — the `2.1%` / `1-2` / `14k` literals in the `/performance` funnel were stale copies of the `FALLBACK_LIVE_PERFORMANCE_METRICS` constants, so they froze at whatever the numbers were the day they were written. Wire the token.
- **Competitor / market figures are fine when they are the documented benchmarks** ($4–7 per click, $70–250 per positive reply, agency $80–200, SDR $50–120, $700 market booked meeting) — those are the landing-billing-truth ranges, not our own invented results.
- **The only real testimonials are Katherine Fleishman and Ryan W.D. Parenti (Doc Dinners), with photos under `public/landing/testimonials/`.** Reuse those; do not write new ones.
- A "results / social proof / what people are getting" section with no named, verifiable person is the tell. Delete it rather than soften it. (#3030)
- **The rule targets a figure presented as a RESULT, not a self-evidently illustrative product PREVIEW.** The hero console renders `yourcompany.com` beside a made-up offer, so nobody reads its numbers as a customer's; calling one of them a "no invented numbers" violation is a false positive, and it burns a round (Kevin 2026-07-30: "FAUX c'est illustratif"). It also does not license a visible "illustrative" disclaimer — the landing is not a legal document, readers are not fools, and the hedge is itself the AI-tell ("Pense Apple, pense SIMPLE"). What DOES apply to a preview is internal coherence: see the undated-figure rule below.

**Two numbers can only CONTRADICT each other when they share a period — an undated figure is not multipliable, and calling it incoherent is pedantry that costs a round.** A card reading `$10 / day` beside `126 positive replies` looks like it implies 907 days at the live `$72` a reply, but `126` carried no timeframe, deliberately, so there was nothing to divide and no contradiction to fix. Check the UNITS on both sides before invoking the self-contradictory-surface rule. **The moment you attach a period, the arithmetic becomes checkable and it must actually hold**: the hero states the budget DAILY and the outcome MONTHLY (the house convention, and the Google-Ads asymmetry — `$50/day` feels small, `$1,550/month` is the same money), so the pair is now derived from ONE place, `HERO_DAILY_BUDGET_USD` × `HERO_MONTH_DAYS` ÷ the live cost per positive reply, and it follows the rate down as the rate improves. The asymmetry does not license a false ratio: Google quotes the monthly volume from the real CPA, and so must we. Match the onboarding budget picker's grain too (`$/day = count × unit cost / 31`) so a visitor meets the same two numbers thirty seconds later at signup instead of a different couple. When the rate cannot be graded, `heroMonthlyOutcomes` returns null and the whole row is dropped — "we could not measure this" and "it buys N" are different statements. Guard: `tests/unit/hero-console.test.ts`. (#3112)

**Re-pricing a headline figure is NOT a find-and-replace: every number DERIVED from the old one moves with it, and a derived PAIR routinely conceals a per-unit price that public copy is banned from quoting.** The offer figure appears twice on the landing: as the offer itself (a CTA, a FAQ, a meta description, where the swap really is mechanical) and as an EXAMPLE BUDGET inside an SEO article, where the sentences around it were computed from it. Swapping only the dollar figure leaves *"$400 reaches 350 contacts"*, *"14 replies at 4%"* and an ROI table keyed on *"deals from a $400 campaign"* wrong by exactly the ratio you just applied, and the page contradicts itself in a way no guard catches. Worse, `$25 ÷ 350 contacts` IS our per-contact price, which the billing-truth rule forbids on any public surface, so the honest repair is not to rescale the pair but to **restate it in outcome terms at the documented band** ($4-7 per click, $70-250 per positive reply). That fixes the re-price and the pre-existing violation in the same edit. Two things to check before calling such a sweep done: any table row whose LABEL is a *minimum* ("minimum viable budget", "cost to start") must NOT take the new offer figure (the minimum is `From $1/day` and always was), and a `grep` for the OLD figure will miss the derived numbers entirely because they never contained it. Cost 2026-07-31 (#3194, $25 → $400): ~15 article passages carried derived arithmetic, and one comparison page also priced a positive reply at `$3` seven lines above a table listing `$70-250`. (#3194)

**`sitemap.ts` hardcodes its static entries → it DRIFTS as static pages are added.** `apps/landing/src/app/sitemap.ts` lists static URLs by hand (+ blog rows from DB). Any NEW statically-served page (`src/app/<page>/route.ts` → `staticResponse`) MUST be added to the `STATIC_SEO_PATHS` array there, or it's live + indexable but absent from the sitemap → slow/partial indexing. #2032 found 22 such missing pages (the whole `cold-email-*` cluster + pro/agency/how-it-works/use-cases/privacy/perf-subpages). Keep `STATIC_SEO_PATHS` in lockstep with the `public/landing/**.html` set. (#2032)

**`SITE_NAME` in `apps/landing/src/lib/seo.ts` REWRITES the served Organization JSON-LD of every statically-served page, so the file on disk and the bytes a crawler receives can disagree — and the disk is the one that lies.** `staticResponse()` runs `withCanonicalOrganization`, which parses each page's `ld+json`, DELETES any hand-written `Organization` node and injects `organizationJsonLd()` (built from `SITE_NAME`) in its place. That is the design — one company, stated once — but it means editing the `"name"` inside `public/landing/**.html` accomplishes nothing, and reading it there tells you nothing about what ships. All 22 static pages served `"name":"distribute"` for as long as that constant said so, while every one of those files already read `"distribute.you"`. Same for the root layout's `title.template`, which suffixes every React page title. Generalise: **when a meta or JSON-LD value looks right in the repo and wrong in the wild, look for a request-time rewriter before doubting the crawler** — and verify any change to it by reading the response off a real `next start`, never by grepping the source. (#3603)

**An exclusion written to protect a FILENAME also hides that file's CONTENTS from the sweep, and the tell is that the excluded path is where the thing you are renaming actually lives.** A rename sweep needs a filter — `logo-distribute.svg` is an identifier and must not change, so `grep … | grep -vE 'logo-distribute'` reads as exactly right. It is not: `grep -v` drops the whole LINE, and for a file whose PATH carries the token every line is dropped, so the file is never read at all. #3603 swept `apps/landing` that way and left six SVGs whose `aria-label` still said the old name — three of them under `public/landing/logo/`, i.e. the very directory the filter named. Two habits that catch it: exclude on the MATCH (`(?!\.you|_you)` in the substitution) rather than on the path, so a protected filename survives while its contents are still inspected; and after any sweep, re-grep for the bare token with NO exclusions and read every hit, because the ones a filter removed are the ones nobody looked at. Cost 2026-08-27: a nine-file follow-up (#3611) for a sweep reported complete, three of whose files were byte-pinned across apps and could not be fixed from the landing PR anyway.

**Corollary — a byte-equality pin between two apps makes a one-app fix impossible, so find the pin BEFORE scoping the PR.** `git grep -l "$(basename <file>)"` across `apps/*/tests` costs nothing and tells you whether the file you are about to edit is asserted from somewhere else. `brand-favicon.test.ts` (in `apps/dashboard`, reading `apps/landing`) is the live example; there may be others.


**Static landing assets (`public/landing/css/*.css`, `js/*.js`) are linked with a MANUAL `?v=N` cache-busting query (e.g. `<link href="css/styles.css?v=5">`) — editing the CSS/JS WITHOUT bumping `?v=N` ships nothing visible.** The `?v=N` URL is its own edge + browser cache key with a long-lived `x-vercel-cache: HIT`; a new deploy updates the asset bytes but returning visitors (and the Vercel edge) keep serving the OLD object at the unchanged `?v=N` URL. Tell: a `?cb=<random>` fetch (cache MISS → origin) shows the new bytes while the exact `?v=N` URL the page links shows the old ones — looks like "the fix didn't deploy" but it's stale cache. So ANY edit to `public/landing/**` CSS/JS MUST bump the `?v=N` token on EVERY HTML that links it (grep `styles.css?v=` across `public/landing/*.html` — the token can differ per page; index-agency was `?v=5` while index/index-pro link bare `css/styles.css`). Verify post-deploy that the homepage's linked `?v=N` URL itself serves the new bytes, not a `?cb=` probe. (#2206 shipped the CSS change but forgot the bump → "aucun changement de taille"; #2211 bumped `?v=5`→`?v=6`.)

⚠️ **A TEST pins those tokens, and it lives in ANOTHER APP: `apps/dashboard/tests/welcome-credits-promise.test.ts` asserts the exact `?v=N` on every page that links `main.js` or `pricing-modal-v1.js`, reading them out of `apps/landing/public/landing/`.** So a correct bump turns that file red, in a suite whose name is about signup credits — read the assertion before assuming the bump was wrong, and move it in the SAME commit. That coupling is deliberate (it is what stops a JS edit shipping invisibly), but the two halves are far apart, so a bump done without touching the test reads as a broken guard and a test edited without the bump reads as green. Both move together or neither does. (#3482) **This line said `apps/landing/tests/unit/` until 2026-08-27, and the wrong path is worse than no note: a `grep` of `apps/landing/tests` comes back empty, which reads as "no guard exists" rather than "wrong app", so the bump ships and `test-dashboard` — a CI merge gate — goes red on a landing-only PR (#3603). When a note in this file names a path, the path is an assertion like any other; `git grep <test-name>` across `apps/` before concluding the guard is absent.**

**Each served landing HTML (`public/landing/**.html`) carries a LARGE inline `<style>` block (~8.5KB in index-agency.html) whose page-scoped rules OVERRIDE `css/styles.css` by specificity — so editing `styles.css` can ship ZERO visible change because an inline rule wins the cascade.** index-agency.html's inline `.hero .t-hero { font-size: 4rem }` (specificity 0,2,0) beats `styles.css`'s `.t-hero` (0,1,0) and pinned the agency homepage H1 at 64px on every viewport even after the `styles.css` clamp + `?v` bump landed. So when a landing visual edit "doesn't take", grep the page's OWN inline `<style>` for the selector BEFORE touching `styles.css` — the override usually lives there. **Verify a CSS landing fix by the RENDERED COMPUTED value, never by grepping the CSS file.** A grep of `styles.css` confirms only that ONE source has the rule, not that it WINS — three rounds (#2206/#2211) were burned verifying the file instead of the render. Headless measure (Playwright is a devDependency of `apps/dashboard`/`apps/admin`): `node` a script that `goto`s the live URL at 390/768/1280px and reads `getComputedStyle(document.querySelector("h1.t-hero")).fontSize`; to find WHICH rule wins, iterate `document.styleSheets` rules + `h.matches(rule.selectorText)`. That surfaces an inline-`<style>` or higher-specificity override the file-grep can't. (#2217: `.hero .t-hero` inline rule was the real culprit; clamp only worked once the INLINE rule was switched to `clamp(2.25rem, 8vw, 4rem)`.)

**Landing nav + footer are injected via `apps/landing/public/landing/js/components.js` (raw JS), which BYPASSES the `static-html.ts` `rewriteHtml` pass — so any `href` there MUST be an absolute URL, never a relative `/path`.** `route.ts` → `staticResponse("index.html")` runs `rewriteHtml` only on the HTML; it rewrites the HTML's relative `/sign-in` / `/sign-up` / `/docs` → absolute (`URLS.*`). The nav/footer markup lives in `components.js` `NAV_HTML` + `FOOTER_HTML` and is served as a static `.js` asset → NOT rewritten. A relative `href="/sign-in"` in `components.js` therefore resolves against the LANDING origin (`distribute.you/sign-in` → **404**, landing has no such route), not the dashboard. Auth/cross-app links in `components.js` must be hardcoded absolute (`https://dashboard.distribute.you/sign-in`). When auditing landing auth/CTA links, grep BOTH the HTML (`public/landing/**.html`) AND `public/landing/js/components.js` — the investigator's reflex (React `navbar.tsx` + HTML) MISSES the served nav. (#1569: navbar/footer Sign in + Start free 404'd because the 6 links were relative `/sign-in` `/sign-up` in `components.js`.)

**JS-INJECTED internal links = SEO orphans — the crawler-link-graph twin of the components.js absolute-URL rule.** Ahrefs / GPTBot / ClaudeBot parse RAW HTML only, so any internal `<a href>` that exists only after JS runs is INVISIBLE to the link graph → the target reads as an orphan page (0 incoming internal links) even though it's linked in-browser. TWO such injectors on the landing: (1) `js/main.js` "Related articles" grid — every article page ships `<div class="post-related-grid"></div>` **empty** and JS fills it with a **random 3**; (2) `js/components.js` nav/footer (`/privacy`, `/pro` links). So a NEW cluster spoke page under `public/landing/**` is BORN ORPHANED — its only inbound links are the JS-random grid. Fix pattern (KevinLourd/landing-orphan-internal-links): bake **static `<a class="post-related-card">` cards into the raw HTML** grid (hubs → all their spokes, spokes → same-cluster siblings) + guard main.js `if (!grid || grid.children.length) return;` so JS only fills EMPTY grids (static links win, progressive-enhancement); add any nav/footer-only orphan (`/pro`, `/privacy`) a static inlink in served HTML (a "More from distribute" strip in `index-agency.html`, the served `/`). RULE: any new statically-served article/cluster page MUST get a static in-HTML internal link from its hub (and vice-versa) in the SAME PR — do NOT rely on the JS related-grid for internal linking. Verify with `curl -sA GPTBot <url> | grep -c 'href="/<slug>"'`, never a browser render. Copy in new cards is user-facing → em-dash-free. (Ahrefs orphan report, #2350)

**`/pricing` is served STATICALLY** (`public/landing/pricing.html` + `src/app/pricing/route.ts` → `staticResponse`), NOT a React page — same as `/performance` and `/how-it-works`. Do NOT add a `page.tsx` under `src/app/pricing/` (it collides with `route.ts` → build error). The budget calculator's JS is in the shared `public/landing/js/main.js`, its CSS inline in `pricing.html`. `lib/pricing/fetch-prices` + `components/pricing/provider-tables-async.tsx` are orphaned, kept for a possible live-cost re-add (`pricing-group-format.test.ts` still covers the lib). The landing source of truth is `apps/landing`; the orphan-history `origin/website` branch should be retired. (#1543, #1574)

**Google Ads conversion tracking is split across FOUR injection points + TWO fire sites — touch the wrong subset and you ship attribution that silently undercounts.** Tag `AW-18233267088`. The LANDING is a pure **gclid carrier** (loads the tag + auto conversion-linker so the `_gcl_aw` cookie lands on the registrable domain `.distribute.you`, shared cross-subdomain) and fires **NO conversion event** — a CTA click is not a conversion. The real conversions fire in the DASHBOARD where the events actually happen. The tag must be present on all SEVEN+ landing surfaces AND the dashboard, so it lives in FOUR places: (1) `apps/landing/src/lib/static-html.ts` `analyticsHead()` — covers every statically-served SEO page (home + the cold-email cluster) in ONE spot; (2) `apps/landing/src/app/layout.tsx` — the React landing pages; (3) `apps/dashboard/src/app/layout.tsx` — alongside GA4. Conversions fire from: (4a) `apps/dashboard/src/components/posthog-auth-tracker.tsx` → `manual_event_SIGNUP` on the same once-per-user signal as PostHog `signup_completed`; (4b) `apps/dashboard/src/components/ads-purchase-tracker.tsx` → `manual_event_PURCHASE` on Stripe Checkout return (`?success=true` = card added / top-up paid, from any launch surface). The purchase tracker is **fire-only** — the launch pages (`brands/[brandId]/launch`, `billing`) already read `pending_topup` + strip the return params to arm auto-topup, so stripping in the tracker would RACE that logic; dedup against refresh via `sessionStorage`, never by stripping the param. `manual_event_*` is Google Ads' auto-generated event-name convention — the string is arbitrary but MUST be byte-equal to the conversion action's event name in the Ads UI (a code-only change does nothing until the matching Ads conversion action exists). Do NOT re-add a click→conversion on the landing (false signal, double-counts the real card-add). GTM is intentionally NOT used (the old `GTM-TBD2WC2P` was a never-configured placeholder, removed). (#1617)
  - **Conversion VALUE — the campaign bids "Maximize conversion value", so every value-bearing conversion MUST send `value` + `currency` or bidding optimizes on COUNT against a flat default, not real revenue.** PURCHASE value = the **1-day budget the user picked** (recurring per-day commitment), NOT the one-off charge: the onboarding launch `success_url` carries `daily_budget` (dollars) and the billing top-up `success_url` carries `paid_amount` (cents). `ads-purchase-tracker.tsx` sends `value` = `daily_budget` (dollars) else `paid_amount/100`. **It fires ONLY when a positive payment value is present (= only after a real payment succeeded); a $0 card-add / setup return does NOT fire.** Both params exist only on a payment-mode checkout whose `success_url` was reached, so their presence proves payment went through; billing-guard card capture is an EMBEDDED checkout (inline `onComplete`, no `?success=true` redirect) so it never reaches this tracker. (The prior design read `pending_topup` and fired VALUELESS on the onboarding launch — that success_url never set `pending_topup` — so the top conversion sent no value; fixed KevinLourd/ads-purchase-daily-budget #2643.) SIGNUP has no instant amount → stays an upper-funnel conversion with a FIXED value set in the Ads UI (no code value). **Code-only is not enough: each Ads conversion action sending a value MUST be set to "Use different values for each conversion" in the Ads UI, else the sent `value` is ignored** (mirror of the byte-equal-event-name rule — the Ads UI is the other half). Both trackers originally fired valueless for months → max-conv-value bidding had nothing to optimize on. (#2002)

**PostHog runs through the first-party reverse proxy `e.distribute.you` to beat ad-blockers (`*.posthog.com` blocklists drop events + session recordings).** It's PostHog's FREE **Managed Reverse Proxy** (CNAME `e.distribute.you` on Gandi LiveDNS → `…cf-prod-eu-proxy.europehog.com`, SSL auto-issued by PostHog — NO Cloudflare Worker, NO Vercel egress). `NEXT_PUBLIC_POSTHOG_HOST=https://e.distribute.you` on ALL FOUR apps (dashboard/admin/landing/sales) → `instrumentation-client.ts` `api_host` reads it; each `posthog.init` ALSO sets `ui_host: "https://eu.posthog.com"` so PostHog UI/toolbar links still resolve while `api_host` points at the proxy. **A NEW app that loads posthog-js MUST set the same env + `ui_host`** or it sends to a bare host that ad-blockers nuke. Ops are 100% API: proxy via the posthog MCP (`proxy-create`/`proxy-get`/`proxy-list`); DNS = Gandi LiveDNS (distribute.you is owned by `GANDI_ORG2_TOKEN`, Bearer auth); app env in `/root/distribute/env/<app>.env` on the box. **`NEXT_PUBLIC_*` is build-inlined → it lives in `env/<app>.build.env` and only a REBUILD picks it up (`./deploy.sh <app> --force`); recreating the container does nothing.** `outcome-digest.ts` (server-side) also reads the same env and rides the proxy (forward is fine; no ad-blocker server-side). Verify the proxy with `curl https://e.distribute.you/static/array.js` (recorder, 200) + `…/flags/?v=2` (ingestion, 200), not by grepping config. (KevinLourd/posthog-reverse-proxy, #2316)

**Partnero affiliate referral tracking (program `KHV3KEHI`) is SERVER-TO-SERVER, split across landing + dashboard — NOT the cookie / Stripe `client_reference_id` method.** Signup happens on `dashboard.distribute.you`, a DIFFERENT subdomain than the `distribute.you` referral landing, and Partnero has no cross-subdomain cookie (their own docs prescribe S2S for a separate signup domain), so the front-end `po('customers','signup')` cookie path would silently attribute 0. The wired design:
- **Landing carries + forwards the partner key** — `apps/landing/src/lib/static-html.ts` `partneroHead()` (static SEO pages, injected via `analyticsHead()`) + `apps/landing/src/app/layout.tsx` (React pages) BOTH load PartneroJS (records the click, keeps the key in the `partnero_partner` cookie on the landing domain) AND a delegated capture-phase click listener that appends `?via=<key>` to every `dashboard.distribute.you`-bound link (the cross-subdomain carrier). Key read from the `via` URL param OR the `partnero_partner` cookie. A tracking/analytics sweep that strips landing `<head>` scripts MUST keep both or referred signups stop crediting.
- **Dashboard registers the customer S2S** — `apps/dashboard/src/components/partnero-via-capture.tsx` (mounted in root `layout.tsx` body) persists `?via=` → `partnero_via` cookie (survives the Clerk OAuth redirect); `posthog-auth-tracker.tsx` fires ONCE on signup (same once-per-user guard as the GA `manual_event_SIGNUP` / PostHog `signup_completed` fires) → POST `/api/partnero/customer` → `apps/dashboard/src/app/api/partnero/customer/route.ts` calls Partnero `POST /v1/customers` with `{partner:{key:via}, key:userId, email, name}` using server-only `PARTNERO_API_TOKEN` (Vercel `distribute-dashboard`, sensitive, preview+production). Fail-loud: missing token → 500, missing fields → 400, Partnero reject → 502; fired fire-and-forget so it never blocks signup.
- **Commission = 30% direct + 20% level-2 lifetime** (Partnero multi-level, program settings). Stripe is connected INSIDE Partnero → recurring purchases credit the partner automatically = LTR. There is NO `stripe-service` change and NO `client_reference_id` (that's Partnero's Payment-Links method; distribute uses custom Clerk checkout, so attribution is signup-registration + Stripe-email match, not link rewriting). Do NOT re-add PartneroJS to the dashboard nor a client-side `po('customers','signup')` — attribution is server-side only. Partner portal: `partners.distribute.you`. (#2665)

### Missing Backend Fields

If the dashboard needs a field/endpoint/capability the backend doesn't provide, NEVER work around it client-side (regex, slugify, name-derivation, aggregation heuristics). Draft a message for Kevin to forward to the backend team and block on the change.

**A DISPLAYED STAT is features-service-owned — the dashboard RENDERS a server field, it NEVER computes a user-facing metric in the browser.** Any cost-per-X (CPC/CPS/CPSM/CPPR/CAC/cost-per-close), total/sum, share/percentage, rate, ROI, or expected-outcome shown to the user MUST arrive as a ready field from features-service (the `/audience-stats` `metrics.*Cents` + the `/revenue` `spend` block / `costEconomics.roiMultiple` are the canonical pattern — null-safe, never a false $0). Client-side `reduce()` of a cost breakdown, `cost/clicks`, `100/cacPct`, `cents/total*100`, `daily*30`, `budget/unitCost` are ALL bugs even when they "look right": two browser-computed numbers on one card routinely diverge (the reported incident — CPC `systemStats.totalCostInUsdCents/clicks` = $5.25 while Total spent = runs ACTUAL spend $359, so $359/73 = $4.92; FS reconciled both onto ACTUAL spend in features-service#396). When a card stat has no FS field yet, do NOT compute it client-side — file a features-service feature request (Kevin: "all those stats must be provided by feature service. You should never compute anything from the front."). The ONLY legit client-side join is a pure display lookup (e.g. cost-source name → provider-logo domain via the price catalog), never a metric. Pure formatting (cents/100, toLocaleString) is fine. (KevinLourd/fix-cpc-calculation #2202, features-service#396)

**A cost-per-outcome on an ACCOUNTING surface FLOORS to net committed spend when 0 outcomes — showing "—" there is the bug, and the floor is NOT a "compute-from-front" violation.** features-service returns `cpprCents`/`cpsCents`/… as committed-net-spend ÷ outcomes and NULLs it at 0 outcomes (no denominator, by design). On the ACCOUNTING surfaces (brand Overview outreach cards, top of Leads, Top-3-audiences card, Audiences table) that null renders "—" and HIDES real money already spent (reported: Overview showed "Total spent $94" next to "Cost per positive reply —"). Fix = `lib/cost-so-far-floor.ts` `costSoFarFloorCents(cost, netSpent, count)`: when count==0 && netSpent>0 → display `netSpent` (the SAME net field already shown as "Total spent" = `spend.totalSpentCents` / audience `evidence.totalCostInUsdCents`), else the raw ratio. This is exempt from the "never compute a metric in the browser" rule because at 0 outcomes NO ratio is computed (floor === the net-spend field verbatim); once a real outcome lands features' non-null ratio wins automatically. The floor is coherent with billing because the dashboard already requests `pricing=net` on `/revenue` + `/audience-stats`. **PROJECTION vs ACCOUNTING — do NOT conflate:** the "Your best model" card is a forward PROJECTION (cheapest workflow's floored fleet estimate via `resolved.costPerOutcomeUsd`); it is CORRECT to show the fleet floor ($42) when the ran workflow spent LESS than the fleet floor — do NOT "fix" it to the accounting spend. The stat cards / Overview / Leads / Audiences are ACCOUNTING (real net spend so far). **Reconciling a $ figure the user challenges: ground it against what they SEE (billing net / the "Total spent" tile), not a raw `runs_costs` sum — and `runs_costs.net_cost_in_usd_cents` is NULL for pre-discount grandfather rows, so `SUM(net)` UNDERCOUNTS; net committed = `SUM(COALESCE(net_cost, total_cost)) FILTER (status IN ('actual','provisioned'))`.** (This brand: gross $149 ≠ naive-net $62 ≠ real-net $94 = billing/Overview.) (#2924)

**UPDATE (features-service v0.102.0 → v0.105.0) — the 0-outcome floor lives SERVER-SIDE and is STRICTER than raw net spend.** `audience-stats` floors every per-audience money metric (`cppr`/`cpc`/`cps`/`cpfs`/`cpsale`) at 0 outcomes to **`max(audience net spend so far, the brand's FLEET-BACKED projected cost-per-outcome)`**, so a cost-per-outcome never dips below what the brand realistically pays and all cost columns obey ONE floor. `costSoFarFloorCents` is now effectively a PASSTHROUGH, so the server floor wins on every consumer with zero dashboard change — do NOT re-add a client-side max-with-projection (joining `audience-stats` spend to a `workflow-projection` figure client-side is compute-from-front, banned). **The brand parent in that `max()` MUST be the fleet-backed `workflow-projection.resolved` figure, never a brand-own raw-spend aggregate** — #641 shipped the wrong parent and a brand whose audiences all had 0 of the outcome floored to its own tiny total spend, so the Audiences table read `$0.66` per website visit while Strategy read the `$2.54` fleet benchmark for the SAME audiences (#653). Invariant: at 0 outcomes the Audiences-table cost-per-outcome and the Strategy per-audience projection are the SAME number by construction; if they diverge, the parent is a brand-own aggregate again. (#2952, features-service #641 → #653)

**UPDATE 2 (features-service v0.106.1) — the fleet parent is cross-org + BEST WORKFLOW, and BOTH readers must ask for the SAME `pricing` basis.** Two follow-on bugs on the same invariant, both fixed 2026-07-29:
- **Producer:** `fetchBrandProjectedParents` built the parent by SUMMING the whole fleet's spend over the whole fleet's outcomes — a cross-org **AND cross-workflow POOLED average** (fleet net $4,775 ÷ ~573 clicks = **$8.33**) while Strategy surfaced the best single workflow (**~$2.42**). Same "fleet benchmark" label, 3× apart. Kevin's standing rule: **a fleet-wide estimate is cross-org + BEST workflow, NEVER pooled across workflows** ("on ne fait que du cross org + best workflow pour l'estimation fleet-wide"). features-service #662 → v0.106.1 now collapses version chains via the existing `buildUpgradeChains`/`aggregateAcrossChains` rollup, computes per-dynasty unit costs guarded on `clicks > 0` / `replies > 0` (a 0-outcome dynasty's ratio is meaningless and must not win), and takes the MIN across all five cost columns.
- **Consumer (this repo):** `fetchFeatureAudienceStats` sends `pricing=net` but `getWorkflowProjectionLadder` sent NO `pricing` param → features-service defaulted it to **gross**, so the identical benchmark rendered ~9% apart across the two pages ($2.22 net vs $2.43 gross) even after the producer fix. **Any dashboard reader of a cost/benchmark that another dashboard surface also renders MUST send `pricing=net`** — net is what the org actually pays and what `/revenue` + `/audience-stats` already request. Guarded by `tests/projection-pricing-net.test.ts`.

**The Audiences==Strategy invariant only binds while the audience's OWN spend is BELOW the parent** — the floor is `max(own spend, fleet best)`, so an audience that has burned more than the benchmark with 0 outcomes correctly shows its own spend (ACCOUNTING) while Strategy keeps showing the benchmark (PROJECTION). That divergence is by design; a divergence at LOW spend is the bug. (KevinLourd/projection-pricing-net, features-service #662 + instantly-service #537)

**UPDATE 4 — the rule covers a reader whose output is a COUNT, not only the ones that print a `$`. The Overview forecast bar was the last `pricing=net` straggler and it read as a volume, so it never looked like a money surface.** `getFeaturePipelineActivity` feeds the grey expected-outreach bars, and features-service builds them as `daily budget / cost per outreach`. The budget is money the org really spends — already discounted — so dividing it by a gross divisor promised a 50%-off brand roughly half the sends its budget buys, beside actual bars twice as tall. Prod, brand `6e21bb6c`, $5/day: **15.88 expected against 30 actual**, the ratio being exactly that org's net/gross (its brand-grain spend over 627 contacts is $197.45 gross / $106.35 net → $0.3149 vs $0.1696 per outreach). Tell for this class: the reader itself renders no currency, but a money value sits somewhere in the chain that produced it. **`tests/projection-pricing-net.test.ts` now pins the whole inventory** — `getFeatureRevenue`, `fetchFeatureAudienceStats`, `getWorkflowProjectionLadder`, `getFeaturePipelineActivity` — so a new reader cannot quietly default back to gross; add yours to the list.

Two producer-side invariants a future change must not break (features-service v0.110.0, api-service v0.93.0):
- **Both legs of the forecast's `max(fleet benchmark, brand's own cost)` read the SAME basis.** Comparing a gross figure against a net one makes the `max()` meaningless and can pick the wrong side, which is a silent wrong number rather than an error.
- **`pricing` is part of the pipeline-activity cache key**, so a net request can never be served a gross body from the SWR snapshot.
- The **daily budget itself is never discounted** — a configured ceiling is not a charge (the same carve-out as the per-org usage discount above). Only money-derived values move; counts and rates are identical either way, and a non-discounted org's frozen net equals its gross (verified in prod: gross and net both 260.49 for a non-discounted brand).

The api-service side was a passthrough bug, not a missing param: the gateway's `pipeline-activity` and `stats` proxies re-declared a closed whitelist of query params, so `pricing` was dropped with no error anywhere. Both now forward every param verbatim, like the `workflow-projection` route in the same file always did. (KevinLourd/forecast-pricing-net #3144, features-service #696, api-service #771)

**UPDATE 5 — the inventory is every reader of a money endpoint, WHEREVER it lives, and the one that hid longest was the one that never touches the browser api client. `outcome-digest.ts` builds its own URL server-side on the cron, so it was invisible to a sweep that read `lib/api.ts`.** It omitted `pricing` entirely, so features-service defaulted it to GROSS: a brand on a 50% usage discount was told by email at 08:00 that it was *"now returning 2.3× per dollar"* while its own Overview read **3.9×** at that moment (prod: **$373.91 gross against $206.81 net**, and `Total spent $206` on the card proves the page is net). Both numbers correct, one word, two places — the self-contradictory-surface bug delivered to an inbox. Tell for this class: a reader that is not a React hook. The sweep that catches it is `grep -rl 'apiUrl}/v1'` over `src/lib` + `src/app/api` (server-side URL builders), not a scan of the api client; `staff-digest.ts` is the only other hit and reads no money endpoint. `tests/projection-pricing-net.test.ts` now carries a second describe block reading `outcome-digest.ts` directly. (KevinLourd/digest-pricing-net)

**UPDATE 3 (features-service v0.108.0) — the zero-outcome floor now covers the BRAND/CAMPAIGN AGGREGATE too, so `costSoFarFloorCents` is GONE from `outreach-stat-cards.tsx`. Do NOT re-add a client fallback to total spend on ANY cost tile.** The floor had only ever been lifted to the per-AUDIENCE grain; the `/revenue` `spend` block still returned `null` at 0 outcomes, and the dashboard's own `costSoFarFloorCents` answered that null with the brand's committed spend. Prod, brand `b97440f6`, goal `positive_replies`: the Overview printed **"Cost per positive reply $29"** one card above **"Total spent $29"** while the Strategy page priced the same brand at **$63** — the same number under two labels, and neither matching Strategy. features-service `revenue.ts` now runs the SAME two engines as `audience-stats-compute.ts` (`flooredCostPerOutcome` for the raw click/reply columns = `max(own committed spend, parent)`; `derivedCostPerOutcome` for the funnel columns, because answering "cost per signup" with a raw dollar total is a units error) against parents from the SAME `fetchBrandProjectedParents` argmin (`audienceIds: []` → zero extra fan-out). Three consequences for this repo:
- **Every cost tile on the stat row renders the server field VERBATIM** (`formatCostCents(spend?.cpprCents)`). `costSoFarFloorCents` survives ONLY as a passthrough guard on the per-audience surfaces (`top-audiences-card`, `customer-audiences-page`).
- **The producer's projection read is deliberately FAIL-SOFT** — a blip returns `null`, meaning "we could not estimate this". A consumer-side `?? spend` fallback therefore re-prints total spend on exactly that branch: the original bug, one layer down, on the case no fixture covers. The honest render for an unestimable cost is `—`. Guard: `tests/outreach-stat-cards.test.ts` fails if `costSoFarFloorCents(` or `totalSpentCents` reappears in the component.
- **Copy carries the honesty, not a hedge label on the value:** ONE `EXPECTED_COST_NOTE` constant behind all four cost tooltips on the row (the guard pins exactly 4 interpolations) so they cannot drift into describing two rules. No "Expected"/provenance pill.

**UPDATE 6 — a cost with fewer than TEN outcomes behind it states `Learning`, not a number, and the bar lives in ONE alias-free lib.** The floor above makes the figure honest about its BASIS; it cannot make it STABLE. A cost per outcome is spend divided by a count, so at one or two outcomes it is decided by whichever one happened to land: it moves by tens of dollars on the next reply, and a customer reads a number on a card as a price we are quoting. Prod: `Cost per positive reply $72` on a campaign with a handful of replies, above a Top-3 card ranking audiences at `$24 cppr / 20x` on one or two replies each. `lib/learning-threshold.ts` (`LEARNING_MIN_OUTCOMES` = 10, `isLearning`, `LEARNING_NOTE`) is the single bar and is alias-free so it carries real unit tests — keep it that way. `components/learning-tag.tsx` renders the pill through `ScoreCard`'s `action` slot, i.e. IN PLACE OF the value: printing a dollar figure beside a caveat reads as a price with a footnote, and the whole point is that there is no figure yet.

- **An ABSENT count is learning, never enough.** "We have no count" is not evidence that we have ten of them, so a producer that does not measure an outcome cannot earn a stated price by omission.
- **Gate EVERY cost on the row, not the reported one.** `Cost per website visit` on the visits, BOTH render sites of `Cost per positive reply` on the replies, and the goal's own outcome cost on its own count — a row where two costs say `Learning` and a third states a price is the self-contradictory-surface bug inside one component.
- **The COUNT cards are untouched.** Nothing is hidden: the card beside it still shows the real number, which is also what the reader needs to see the bar approaching.
- **A live-tracker CTA outranks the tag.** A tracker that is not set up is WHY the count is thin, so "set this up" is the actionable answer; the tag only speaks where there is nothing left to set up.
- **Campaign scope by CONSTRUCTION, not by a flag.** Those pairs render behind `showFunnelMetrics`, which only a campaign passes, and the Top-3 gate needs a `metric`, which brand level does not have (it states the return and nothing funnel-scoped). Do not add a scope prop to re-derive what the existing gates already decide.
- **A learning Top-3 row drops its `$25 cppr` subtitle ENTIRELY** rather than keeping a cost beside a tag that exists to say there is no cost. Its rows carry no `(i)` of their own — each sits inside a `Link`, and the header's existing tooltip carries the reason when any listed row is learning.
- **The tag wears the charter's TERTIARY, and every OTHER accent on a campaign's pages wears it too — one colour per surface, owner-decided.** It shipped amber, was reported as *"scary"* (amber is a warning colour and the tag says nothing went wrong), moved to the secondary, and then to the tertiary when the owner asked for one dominant accent across the campaign surfaces: *"dans toutes les pages de campaign il faut que la couleur dominante utilisée soit la couleur tertiaire de la marque"*. Tints are `bg-orange-50` / `text-orange-600` / `border-orange-200`, full-perimeter 1px border. **It carries `tone-tile`, so it rotates to the BRAND's tertiary** — a customer's dashboard says "learning" in THEIR colour, not ours (*"always make our colors the colors of the brand"*). What never rotates is a VERDICT (semantic green and red, and the paused grey), not an accent. ⚠️ **Orange was only HALF-remapped when this landed** — `bg-orange-50` had both a dark rule and a rotation while the weights beside it had neither, which is the third time that exact gap has appeared (purple, then green/red, now orange). `html.dark` for `text-orange-600` / `text-orange-700` / `border-orange-200` / `bg-orange-200`, plus rotation for `.tone-tile.border-orange-200` and the descendant `.text-orange-700` / `.bg-orange-200` / `.bg-orange-600`, were all added with it; `bg-orange-600` stays unremapped in dark, exactly like `bg-purple-600`, because a saturated mid tone reads correctly on the dark surface as-is. **Two siblings moved in the SAME pass and must stay with it**: the `Learning: about N days left` band (`learning-progress-callout.tsx`, which carries MORE layers than the tag — a 700-weight heading and both halves of a bar) and the campaign **Outcome chart**, which had been drawing the PRIMARY (`text-brand-600`). That chart is campaign-scoped by construction (the brand and offer Overviews draw `RoiTrendCard` instead) and carries `tone-tile` on its chart WRAPPER, not the card: an SVG `stroke` is reached by no utility remap, so the colour rides `currentColor` off a class and that class only rotates under a `tone-tile` ancestor. ⚠️ Consequence accepted: `LearningTag` is ONE shared component, so it is tertiary at brand and offer grain too — one tag reading two colours for one meaning would be worse than the wider blast radius. Verified by REPRODUCTION (compiled Tailwind off the app's own `globals.css` + Playwright), not by grepping the classes: every layer moves by the same delta in all four states (light/dark, tinted/untinted). Guards: `tests/learning-threshold.test.ts` + `tests/learning-progress.test.ts` + `tests/pipeline-activity-chart.test.ts`. (#3547, #3551, #3716, #3726)

**UPDATE — which accent the tag wears is a property of the SURFACE, carried by `LearningToneProvider` (`components/learning-tag.tsx`), and the default is the tertiary so every page that states nothing is unchanged.** The consequence accepted above (one shared component, so one colour everywhere) held only while every surface wanted the same accent. The funnel Overview does not: it states the funnel an OFFER sells rather than one campaign's surface, so it reads in the brand's **PRIMARY**. **Every OFFER-grain surface reads the primary too** (owner-decided, #3763) — its Overview, its Audiences, its Leads and its Funnels list; the BRAND root reads it as well (#3778), and only the campaign surfaces stay tertiary. Five things are load-bearing:
- **A CONTEXT, not a prop.** The tags on that page are rendered by three SHARED components (`OutreachStatCards`, `RoiTrendCard`, `TopAudiencesCard`), none of which has any business knowing which page mounted it — threading a tone through all three is how one of them ends up missing it and a single page renders two colours for one meaning.
- **The Campaigns / leg table states NO tone — the SURFACE it is mounted on decides** (`CampaignsTable` is a bare pass-through to `CampaignsTableInner`). It used to pin itself back to the tertiary wherever it was mounted, on the reasoning that it states campaigns and so should read in the campaign accent everywhere; that put an orange table under a primary header on every page stating its own accent, so one screen carried two colours for one meaning. The tone is a property of the PAGE, and the pin was the one thing stopping a page from being one colour. Consequence, and it is the point: the table reads PRIMARY on the brand Overview, on the offer Overview and on the offer's Funnels page, and keeps the default tertiary on the campaign-grain surfaces, which state none.
- **The primary reads the `--color-brand-*` ramp** (`border-brand-200` / `bg-brand-50` / `text-brand-600`), NEVER a charter hex: `:root[data-brand-tint]` re-declares that ramp at the brand hue, so all three layers rotate together and an arbitrary-value literal would be the one control that stays blue on a tinted dashboard. It carries **no `tone-tile`** — that rotation exists for the categorical purple/indigo/blue/orange set, and adding it here would read as load-bearing. All three classes are remapped in `html.dark` AND `html.dark[data-brand-tint]`, so neither theme paints a light block.
- **`paused` ignores the tone entirely.** A verdict never rotates with the brand hue and never takes a page's accent.
- **The BRAND Overview reads the primary too, and it states that tone IN the component** (owner-decided, #3778: *"sur cette page brand, tous les tags learning doivent etre en primary"*). There is no route wrapper for the brand grain — `brands/[brandId]/page.tsx` IS the page — so the tone cannot be stated anywhere else, and the offer Overview, which renders that very component, INHERITS it rather than restating it (`offers/[offerId]/page.tsx` states none now). The offer's other three routes still state theirs on the ROUTE: `CustomerAudiencesPage` / `EngagedLeadsPage` / `OfferFunnelsPage` are shared with the brand and campaign grains, so a tone written inside any of them repaints grains nobody asked to change. Guards: the tone block in `tests/learning-threshold.test.ts` pins the brand page's own tone, the three offer routes, the absence of one in the two shared components AND in the offer Overview route, and that the campaigns table pins nothing; `tests/offer-level.test.ts` pins the import-and-render shape.
Verified by REPRODUCTION (compiled Tailwind off the app's own `globals.css` + Playwright), not by grepping the classes: under a +40 hue tint the primary's text, fill and border all move 258 -> 298 in light and in dark, while the tertiary and the paused grey are byte-identical to before in all four states. Guards pin the default, the class string, both dark remaps, the table's own pin, and that no OTHER page states a tone. (#3749)

**A PAUSED campaign says `Paused`, not `Learning`, and its countdown band is HIDDEN.** The tag says a figure is withheld because too few outcomes have landed; on a stopped campaign nothing is landing, so it promises a number that cannot arrive until the customer restarts it, and the `Learning: about N days left` band prices days against a daily spend that is not happening. `LearningTag`'s `paused` branch reads the word in the pause grey (`bg-gray-100 text-gray-500 border-gray-200`, the SAME tint the status pill and `ROLLUP_STYLE.paused` use, so one campaign is never described two ways on one screen) and carries NO `tone-tile` - a verdict never rotates with the brand hue. ⚠️ **WHICH figures are withheld does not change**: that stays keyed on the outcome counts, so restarting restores exactly the tags and the band it had. The flag is derived ONCE per surface off the campaign query that page ALREADY polls (`isRunningStatus(campaign.status)` - no second read) and threaded to the tags, so a component that handles `paused` while no page passes it is the feature entirely absent with the component perfectly correct: the guards pin the CALL SITES, not only the component. Scope is the campaign that OWNS the surface - its Overview stat row, its return chart, its Top-3 card, its Audiences table, its row in the Campaigns table. Brand and offer grain stay untouched: several campaigns sit under one heading there and no single status answers for them.

**The `max()` semantic is deliberate at EVERY grain, and spend-wins-above-the-benchmark is INTENDED — do not "improve" it to a strict replace.** Kevin: *"If 0 outcome and > expected from the model, then it shows the total spent, exactly the way it is returned by Feature Service. What we have for each audience, I want the same logic for the aggregated."* Generalised: when a surface is asked to reach PARITY with a sibling surface, mirror that sibling's semantic exactly; a semantic upgrade bundled into a parity fix is a second, unrequested change. (#3090 / #3099, features-service #689 → v0.108.0 / #692)

**A COST metric's change/delta indicator is COST-SEMANTIC, never stock-ticker — a FALLING cost is green (good), a RISING cost is red (bad).** Any cost-per-X / CAC / spend change badge (▲/▼ arrow shows direction; the COLOR encodes goodness) colors a decrease green and an increase red — the INVERSE of a stock price. The `ScoreCard` `deltaInverted` prop already encodes this; a bespoke cost-delta badge (e.g. the cross-org Expected economics cards, admin feature-stats) must invert too. Do NOT default to stock-style (up=green) for a cost — Kevin corrected exactly this. (admin Expected economics 7-day change, #2514)

**A brand's "status" (active / paused / inactive) is a COMPOSITE of TWO services — pause + budget — never one.** The `paused` flag lives in **campaign-service** (`brand_pause` table `{brandId, orgId, paused}`, read via `GET /brands/:brandId/pause`, api-service `brand-pause.ts` forwards there — explicitly *NOT* brand-service, *NOT* billing). The `$/day` daily budget lives in **billing-service** (`brand_daily_budgets`, `GET /internal/brands/:brandId/daily-budget`; `dailyBudgetCents: 0` = explicit pause-via-budget, `null` = never configured). The dashboard's own Brand-status pill (`brand/brand-status-control.tsx`) reads BOTH (`getBrandPause` + `getBrandDailyBudget`) — so any status/active surface MUST read both. A brand is paused (campaigns HELD, budget retained, NOT spending) independently of its budget value: reading budget alone counts a paused brand as active and inflates any revenue/MRR sum. Precedence: **paused → active (budget>0 && orgBalance>budget) → inactive**; paused/inactive are excluded from spend/MRR/ARR. Cost 2026-07-01 (Audit → Accounts, #2340→#2342 + features-service #427/#436): the accounts endpoint first read billing budget only, so 16 paused brands showed active and MRR read $3,090 instead of $1,110 (~3× inflation) until pause was factored in from campaign-service. brand-service holds identity only (name/domain/profile), never active/paused. (#2342)

**⚠️ The TABLE NAMES in the rule above are stale, and each stale one fails in the direction that looks like an answer.** Queried 2026-08-25 on the box: campaign-service has **no `brand_pause` table** — the pause state lives in **`brand_pause_transitions`** (`brand_id`, `paused`, `transitioned_at`; take `DISTINCT ON (brand_id) … ORDER BY brand_id, transitioned_at DESC` for the current state, there is no `created_at`). And billing-service's **`brand_daily_budgets` is EMPTY** — money moved to per-funnel rows, so the brand total is `SUM(daily_budget_cents)` over **`brand_funnel_daily_budgets`** grouped by brand. A join against `brand_daily_budgets` therefore returns **0 rows for every brand**, which reads as "nobody has a budget" rather than as a wrong table, so the composite silently collapses to "nothing is active".

**Concretely, on prod that day: 19 brands had an `ongoing` campaign, 4 of those were paused, and only 7 had a funded funnel.** So `status='ongoing'` overstates the live client base by ~3x — do NOT use it as a proxy for "active clients" when sizing a backfill, a quota spend, or a customer-facing count. The full query is: distinct `ongoing` brand_ids, minus the ones whose latest pause transition is `true`, kept only where the per-funnel budget sum is `> 0`. The affordability leg (org balance > budget) is NOT a column — `billing_accounts` has no balance field, it is composed from Stripe topups minus the runs ledger — so it needs a billing-service call, and it can only shrink the set further.

**A detail / right-panel that needs richer per-entity data should FETCH IT ON-DEMAND from the OWNING service, keyed off an id already on the clicked row — do NOT widen the list/aggregation endpoint that populates the table.** When a table row is clicked open a panel that wants more than the row carries (profile, audience, "why relevant"), the reflex is to add the fields to the endpoint that feeds the table. That's wrong altitude when the table is fed by an AGGREGATION endpoint (e.g. features-service `/revenue`, which exists to compute pipeline $, not to serve profiles): the panel data belongs to whichever service OWNS it, fetched lazily on panel-open (gated `enabled` on selection, cached). The only thing the table endpoint must carry is a JOIN KEY already native to it (`leadId`), not the foreign profile/audience fields. Concrete (KevinLourd/remove-database-add-lead-panel, #2095): the overview lead panel's "why relevant" pulls audience from **human-service** (`getAudienceMembershipStats` → `listAudiences`) + profile from **lead-service** (`listBrandLeads`, cached) on click — `/revenue` was left untouched (first instinct to add `email` to it was correctly rejected by Kevin: "revenue seems different than a right panel you can fetch dynamically"). Per-service ownership > widening an aggregation contract. (#2095) **BUT the inverse also holds — do NOT build a client-side JOIN for a PRIMARY TABLE COLUMN when the table's OWN feed already serves the field on the row.** The distinction is table-column vs detail-panel: #2095 is about a right-PANEL wanting RICHER data than the row carries. A column rendered on EVERY row is different — if the table's owning endpoint already carries it, read it straight off the row. The Audiences→Leads table computed its **Audience** column via `getAudienceMembershipStats` (email→name) + `listAudiences` (id→avatar) joined by email, but lead-service's `GET /orgs/leads` (which feeds the table via `listBrandLeads`) ALREADY serves `audience {id,name,avatarUrl}` + `audienceId` on the leads_campaigns row (forwarded untouched by the api-service `/v1/leads` pass-through, kept at runtime by `LeadDeliverySchema.passthrough()` — only the `Lead` TYPE lacked the field, so the wire value was present but untyped). Fix = add the field to the `Lead` type + read `lead.audience`; drop the two queries + the email-join. Reading off the row is also MORE accurate (exact stored attribution vs email match). Before adding a membership/lookup join for a table column, check the table's primary list endpoint's openapi for the field first. (KevinLourd/leads-audience-from-backend, #2358)

### Verify backend shape before writing client types (wire-shape rot)

**Deployed openapi is ground truth. Before declaring ANY typed helper (`listX`/`getX`/response interface/Zod schema) for a backend endpoint, fetch the real shape via the `api-registry` / `api-registry-staging` MCP.** Never invent from "what feels right" or an aspirational PR description. **Live (MCP `call_api`) > source > local clone** — local clones (incl. a subagent's) go stale; verify a subagent's reported wire shape via the live MCP. Want a richer shape → file a backend request and block. Long-lived types rot silently when an upstream endpoint is refactored — re-check periodically, not just at creation. (#1079, #1094)

**A backend-contract migration on `apps/dashboard/src/lib/api.ts` MUST migrate the byte-equal reader in `apps/admin/src/lib/api.ts` in LOCKSTEP — the two apps share the same `getX`/`listX` Zod readers and DRIFT.** admin is a dashboard fork with its own copy of `api.ts`; a reshape fixed in only one app leaves the other's `safeParse` throwing on every consumer of that endpoint (loud console `response shape mismatch` + broken page). This has bitten in BOTH directions: dashboard-straggler on the ahref DR/traffic bulk readers (#2070), and admin-straggler on the `getWorkflowProjection` `{workflows[]}`→`{rows[]}` ladder reshape (dashboard fixed #2445/#2446, admin still legacy → #2451). When you migrate a reader for a reshaped/renamed backend contract, `grep` the SAME symbol in the sibling app's `api.ts` and port it in the same PR (or spawn the sibling straggler); prefer keeping the two readers byte-equal, diverging only where a support type is genuinely absent in one app. (#2451)

**Both `apps/dashboard` AND `apps/admin` register email templates at boot (`src/instrumentation.ts` → `PUT /internal/emails/templates`) into ONE SHARED store keyed by template `name` — so a template that exists in BOTH apps is LAST-WRITER-WINS, and whichever app redeploys last overwrites the other's copy. If the two copies DRIFT, the digest/email silently renders the STALE app's template against the sending app's metadata → blank `{{tokens}}`.** The box's crontab has the `outcome-digest` cron (01:00 UTC) that SENDS it — it was `apps/dashboard/vercel.json` until the Vercel cutover; `apps/admin` had NO cron but still registered a stale aggregate copy of the same template → every admin deploy clobbered the dashboard's correct per-brand template, and the cron's per-brand metadata rendered against admin's old `{{orgName}}`/`{{totalExpectedRevenueUsd}}` tokens → "digest for ." + "worth  in expected pipeline revenue" blanks. **Rule: a given email-template `name` has ONE owner app — the one whose cron/route SENDS it. Do NOT register the same template name in a sibling app "just in case"; a non-sending app registering it is dead code that only clobbers. When editing a template, `grep -rn '"<template-name>"' apps/*/src/instrumentation.ts` — if two apps declare it, either delete the non-sender's copy (preferred) or keep them byte-equal.** admin's whole digest surface (lib + cron route + template + tests) was deleted for exactly this. (#2676)

**A boot seed that UPSERTS BY NAME cannot express a RENAME — the old row survives, and the deploy that renames may not even come up.** Every registrar in the fleet works the same way (email templates by `name`, platform keys by `provider`, features-service's feature seed by `slug`): it walks its own list and upserts each entry, so an entry whose key CHANGED is inserted under the new key while the row under the old key is untouched. Two consequences, and the second is the one that bites: (a) consumers listing the store see BOTH, so a renamed thing appears twice; (b) if anything about the store is unique-per-concept — a partial index, a display invariant, a health check that counts — the boot can FAIL, and on the Hetzner box a failed boot is `UNHEALTHY after 60s, rolling back`, so the rename silently never ships while the old code keeps serving happily. Renaming a seeded key therefore needs the seed to PRUNE rows absent from its own list before upserting, in the SAME ship as the rename. Cost 2026-08-18 (features-service, `sales-feedback-request-cold-email-outreach` → `feedback-request-cold-email-outreach`): the rename hotfix rolled back three times before a follow-up hotfix added prune-before-upsert; `deploy.log` showed only `UNHEALTHY … rolling back` with no cause, and the container logs of the *rolled-back* attempt were the only place it was visible. **Diagnostic corollary: the last `deploy.log` line for a service is NOT its current state** — the log is append-only and a later successful deploy of a DIFFERENT sha may follow the rollback you are reading. Confirm with the clone's own HEAD (`git -C /root/distribute/repos/<svc> log --oneline -1`) plus the container's health, and settle "did the data change land?" by reading the destination store, never the log.

**That `apps/*/src/instrumentation.ts` grep is NOT the whole registrar set — BACKEND services register into the same shared store, so an ownership audit that only greps this repo finds a false "nobody else owns this name".** The store is transactional-email-service's `email_templates` table, keyed by `name`, upserted by any caller of its `PUT /templates` / `PUT /platform-templates`. Known backend registrar: **billing-service owns `credits-reload-failed` + `brand_daily_budget_changed`** (its `src/instrument.ts`, called from `src/index.ts` after `app.listen()`); **this repo owns the six dunning templates** (`credit-depleted`, its two follow-ups, and their three `-blocked` variants — distribute.you#1420) and billing deliberately does NOT re-register them. So the one-owner rule spans repos: the owner is whichever service SENDS the `eventType`, wherever it lives. Two consequences: (a) before adding a template here, `git grep '"<name>"' origin/main` in the backend repos that send email, not just `apps/*`; (b) **verify a registration landed by reading the DESTINATION store, never by reading the registration code** — billing's registration module was imported by nothing for months, so its declared templates were absent from prod (36 rows, zero from billing) while the code looked correct, and `credits-reload-failed` failed on every fire. The authoritative check is a `SELECT name FROM email_templates WHERE name = '<name>'` against the transactional-email prod branch. (billing-service v0.57.1)

**These two apps are also the fleet's PLATFORM-KEY REGISTRAR — key-service is where every shared platform secret lives, and the `PLATFORM_KEYS` array in each app's `src/instrumentation.ts` is what puts it there (`POST /platform-keys` on every cold start, idempotent upsert).** A backend that needs a shared secret resolves it from key-service (`GET /keys/platform/<provider>/decrypt`), so a secret sitting in a service's own env file is a gap, not a design: the fix is to add `{ provider, envVar }` here from whichever app already holds that env var, NOT to set it on the consumer. Three things a future edit must respect: (1) **the loop SKIPS a missing env var with only a `console.warn`** — a provider whose env var is absent from that app's env registers nothing and fails silently, so confirm presence (`grep '^<VAR>=' /root/distribute/env/<app>.env` on the box) before assuming a registration landed; (2) **registering the same provider from BOTH apps is fine only while the two values are byte-equal** — the upsert is last-writer-wins, so if the values ever diverge, whichever app redeploys last wins (same hazard as the email-template store above, without the ownership rule, because a platform secret has no "sender"); (3) both `tests/platform-config-startup.test.ts` pin the expected list AND an exact count, so a new entry means editing both apps' tests in the same commit. `clerk` (the Clerk backend secret, consumed by client-service — the only backend that calls the Clerk API) is registered from both apps this way. (#3084)

**An `x-external-user-id` a platform JOB sends CREATES A USER ROW — so a synthetic caller identity is keyed on the JOB, never on the org, and it lives in the `system-` namespace (`lib/service-identity.ts`, byte-equal twins in both apps).** The api-service admin path resolves `x-external-org-id` + `x-external-user-id` through client-service `/internal/resolve`, which UPSERTS `users` on whatever external id it is handed — correct for a person signing in, a row-per-call-site for a cron. Three surfaces here have no person behind them (the nightly outcome-digest scan, the no-login public report proxy, the read-only share-link proxy) and each interpolated the ORG into its id (`outcome-digest:org_ABC`), so prod reached **89 phantom rows against 64 real Clerk users**, one per org per job, growing forever. Two things made it worse than table noise: **client-service's public user count — the figure the landing `/investors` page prints — excludes `system-%` and two test ids and nothing else, so it read 153 users for a platform with 64**, an invented number on a public surface; and one row was the literal `report-public:%7BorgId%7D`, an id whose template never interpolated, which nothing would ever have surfaced. Dropping the org from the id loses nothing — the org travels on its own header and is stored on the run (`runs.organization_id`, 13,159 of them referencing these 89 principals), and the gateway reads the org from the ORG upsert, never from the user row; `users.orgId` does churn on the shared row as a scan walks orgs, harmlessly. Generalise: **before sending an identity header for a caller that is not a person, ask what the receiver PERSISTS on it** — the header reads like metadata and is a write. Guards: `apps/{dashboard,admin}/tests/service-identity.test.ts` (the catalogue is alias-free, so those are real unit tests, and the admin copy pins the two files byte-equal). (KevinLourd/service-identity-namespace)

Sub-classes (all invisible to a "page loads" smoke test):
- **Data existing in SOME backend service ≠ the dashboard can reach it — `api-service` (the gateway) must PROXY the path first; confirm before scoping a consumer-only PR.** The dashboard only talks to `api-service` via `/api/v1`; a `/orgs/*` route owned by another service (e.g. a freshly-imported `ahref-service`) is unreachable until the gateway adds a transparent-proxy route. The registry `api` service lists ZERO endpoints (passthrough — the index doesn't enumerate its proxy table) and full-text search doesn't index downstream paths, so neither tells you whether the gateway forwards it. 30-sec probe — a live gateway call `mcp__api-registry__call_api(service:"api", GET "/v1/<path>")`: **404 "Not found" = route ABSENT** (CROSS-REPO: needs a new `api-service` proxy route + likely new `<SVC>_SERVICE_URL`/`_API_KEY` Railway vars before the dashboard can consume it) · **401 "Missing authentication" = route PRESENT** (reachable). Confirm via `git -C ~/conductor/repos/api-service grep "orgs/<thing>" origin/main`. Treating producer-shipped as dashboard-reachable turns a 1-PR task into a 3-part rollout. (#1299, DIS-200/212) **⚠️ A 404 on the DOWNSTREAM path is NOT proof the gateway doesn't proxy it — the gateway can RENAME the path segment. Before concluding "proxy missing" (and spawning an api-service workspace), list the gateway's OWN routes at the relevant prefix: `mcp__api-registry__list_service_endpoints(service:"api", pathPrefix:"/v1/public")`. The features-service cross-org public stats live downstream at `/public/stats/*` but the gateway exposes them REMAPPED as `/v1/public/features/*` (cost-projection/ranked/best/revenue/workflow-engagement-latency) — so `GET /v1/public/stats/cost-projection` 404s while `GET /v1/public/features/cost-projection` is live. Probing only the downstream literal gave a false "absent" and triggered a redundant api-service proxy spawn (the routes already shipped in api-service #404/#542). Always cross-check the gateway's endpoint listing before scoping a proxy add. (admin Sales-Cold-Email cross-org stats page, #2486) **Corollary — the gateway PRESERVES the downstream service's own `/orgs` prefix under `/v1`, so a `/orgs/<thing>` downstream route is proxied at `/v1/orgs/<thing>`, NOT `/v1/<thing>`. Probe `/v1/orgs/contacts` not `/v1/contacts` — the dashboard/admin readers already encode this (`apiCall("/orgs/contacts")` → `/api/v1/orgs/contacts`). Dropping the `/orgs` segment in the probe gives a false 404 → a wrong "needs api-service proxy PR" conclusion. crm-service `/orgs/contacts*` is live at `/v1/orgs/contacts*` (api-service #740); the correct probe is `list_service_endpoints(api, pathPrefix:"/v1/orgs/contacts")`. (admin brand-level CRM surface, #2962)**
- **Audit ALL of a service's endpoints when migrating a consumer**, not just the ones the migration touches — adjacent endpoints rot in the same refactor window. (#1213)
- **`safeParse` every list/get `apiCall<…>` wrapper** that talks to an external service (per-field schemas in the same file) — converts wire-rot from a render-crash into a caught fetch-error. **Per-VERB schema:** a write response DTO is often NARROWER than its read sibling (omits computed fields like `isDefault`/`updatedAt`/counts) — reusing one schema turns every successful write into a false "invalid response shape". Too-loose → bad data renders + crashes (#1213); too-strict shared schema → false error on success (#1221, `.omit({ isDefault: true })`).
- **A too-narrow consumer `z.object()` SILENTLY STRIPS a newly-added backend field** (Zod drops undeclared keys) → `field` reads `undefined` at the call site though it's on the wire. Adding the TS type is not enough — add it to the Zod schema too. (#1282)
