**This is the dashboard's named architecture: a LOCAL-FIRST, stale-while-revalidate (SWR) cache.** Local-first = the on-disk cache is the source the UI paints FIRST, network is secondary. SWR = show the cached (stale) data instantly, refetch in the background, swap fresh in silently (TkDodo: *"stale data is better than no data, because no data means a loading spinner = perceived slow"*). It is the 4th "don't revert resolved state on a transient" layer and the only one that survives cache EVICTION/RELOAD (the first three keep warm-in-memory state across refetch only): 1. `keepPreviousData` (data) · 2. `useCoordinatedReveal` (reveal) · 3. `useMonotonicStatuses` (row bucket).

**Mechanism (`src/lib/query-provider.tsx` + `src/lib/persist-cache.ts`, unit-tested):** the **PER-QUERY persister** `experimental_createQueryPersister` (from `@tanstack/react-query-persist-client`), wired as a default query option (`queries.persister = persister.persisterFn`) on a plain `QueryClientProvider`. Storage = **IndexedDB** via `idb-keyval`. NOT the old whole-client `PersistQueryClientProvider` + `createSyncStoragePersister` (localStorage).

**Why the migration (2026-06-25, KevinLourd/dashboard-instant-cache) — the 2026-06-23 "persist-everything into localStorage" policy was SELF-DEFEATING, causing the exact two symptoms it meant to fix ("30s skeletons all the time" + "site lourd/lent"):**
- **localStorage's hard ~5 MB per-origin cap** → a heavy brand's `brandLeads`/`brandEmails` blew the cap → `removeOldestQuery` evicted the small overview queries (cached first) → the overview cold-skeletoned on the slow Neon chain. The persist-all policy evicted the very page it was meant to keep.
- **Whole-client `dehydrate()` re-serializes the ENTIRE cache on EVERY mutation** (TanStack #9775). ~12 overview queries polling 5s → constant multi-MB `JSON.stringify` + sync write on the MAIN THREAD → jank/"lourd".
- The per-query persister fixes BOTH: each query is written **separately, keyed by its query hash, only when IT changes** (no whole-cache re-serialize) AND IndexedDB has **no 5 MB cap + async off-main-thread writes**. Verified against TanStack `createPersister` docs + TkDodo #5169.

**`admin` ≠ `dashboard`: separate Vercel apps = separate ORIGINS = separate storage.** `admin.distribute.you`'s heavy Outlets/Journalists/Articles cache NEVER touches the `dashboard.distribute.you` cache. Don't diagnose dashboard slowness via admin's big lists; the dashboard customer never even loads those (alpha/staff-gated). Admin is allowed to stay heavy.

**`apps/admin` runs the SAME per-query IndexedDB SWR persister (ported from the dashboard, #2779) BUT with a DENYLIST, not the dashboard's curated allowlist — do NOT re-introduce a `PERSISTABLE_QUERY_ROOTS` allowlist in admin, and do NOT re-sync admin's `persist-cache.ts` to the dashboard's.** admin is staff-only on a separate origin ("allowed to stay heavy"), so `isPersistableQueryKey` persists EVERY successful root except `SENSITIVE_QUERY_ROOTS` (`apiKeys`/`byokKeys`/`keySources` — key material only). This is deliberate: any new admin page auto-SWRs with zero allowlist maintenance (a new dashboard page still opts in via the allowlist). Two more admin-specific divergences from the dashboard's `query-provider.tsx`: (1) the cache **bucket = `urlOrgId ?? "platform"`** (`bucketForPath`) — admin has TWO page classes: `/orgs/[orgId]/…` god-mode customer pages (bucket = URL org id, DIS-143 isolation preserved) AND cross-org FLEET pages (`/metrics`, `/audit`, `/feature-stats`, `/workflows`, `/features` — platform-global data owned by no org, bucket = fixed `"platform"` so they're SWR regardless of the staff user's Clerk active org); (2) the nav reseed + remount key derive from that URL bucket (no monotonic active-org latch — the URL is stable, never blinks). Storage key namespace is `distribute-admin-cache:{bucket}` (vs `distribute-dashboard-cache:`). (#2779)

**Load-bearing rules:**
- **`maxAge` is a LIE without `navigator.storage.persist()` — request persistent storage at boot, or the browser EVICTS the whole IndexedDB store after a few idle days and every `useAuthQuery` surface cold-skeletons again.** Best-effort IndexedDB (the default) is evictable under disk pressure / prolonged non-use, so the "instant across days" the per-query persister promises silently decays — this is the recurring "instant right after a deploy, infinite skeleton a few days later" report on audiences/leads. `QueryProvider` calls `requestPersistentStorage()` once at mount (feature-detected, idempotent via `storage.persisted()`, never throws). Do NOT remove it; a rebrand/refactor of `query-provider.tsx` must keep the boot call. Pairs with the org-gate latch below — eviction UNMASKS the disabled-query hang. (KevinLourd/leads-skeleton-persist-orggate)
- **Disk retention (`maxAge`) and memory (`gcTime`) are DECOUPLED, and BOTH are finite.** The decoupling is why per-query beats whole-client: a heap-GC'd query **stays on disk**, so staying instant across sessions needs NO `gcTime: Infinity` → no heap blowup. (The whole-client persister mirrors disk⇆memory, so `maxAge: Infinity` there would force `gcTime: Infinity` = the #1273 OOM.) `PERSIST_MAX_AGE_MS = 30 days`, `PERSIST_GC_TIME_MS = 30min`. The old "`gcTime` MUST equal `maxAge`" rule (TanStack #5169) applies ONLY to the whole-client persister.
- **A read of the persisted cache is SCOPED TO ONE BUCKET, and the store is SWEPT — `maxAge` was `Infinity` and nothing ever deleted anything, so opening any page loaded every response the app had ever cached into the heap.** The store is one flat key space shared by every bucket (each god-mode org, plus admin's `platform`), and the persister's own `restoreQueries` / `persisterGc` call `storage.entries()` and only THEN filter on `key.startsWith(prefix)` — so a whole-store `entries()` decodes every org's cache to keep one page's. It ran twice per mount (the mount `restoreQueries` plus the nav reseed, whose effect also fires on mount) and again on every navigation. Reported as a single admin tab holding **3.5 GB**. Three parts, all in `lib/idb-bucket.ts` + `lib/persist-cache.ts`, byte-equal across both apps: (a) `bucketEntries(prefix)` reads the bucket's CONTIGUOUS key range with a bounded `getAll`, and is what the storage adapter's `entries()` returns — the persister then finds its prefix filter already satisfied; (b) the mount `restoreQueries` is GONE (the nav reseed is a superset AND cold-guarded, while `restoreQueries` calls `setQueryData` unconditionally and can stomp fresher memory) — do NOT add it back; (c) `sweepStaleEntries` walks a CURSOR at boot, in constant memory, across every bucket, deleting what `PERSIST_MAX_AGE_MS` and the buster say is past. Do NOT reach for the persister's own `persisterGc()`: it calls `entries()`, so garbage collecting would re-create the allocation this exists to avoid, and it only ever looks at the current bucket. `reclaimLegacyStore` clears the pre-bounded store once ever (the sweep cannot reach it — most of those bytes are recent enough not to be stale), guarded by `RECLAIM_MARKER_KEY`, which the sweep must never delete or the reclaim runs on every boot. Guards: `tests/persist-bucket-scope.test.ts`.
- **Org-scope via the persister `prefix`** = `persisterStorageKey(orgId)` (`distribute-dashboard-cache:{orgId}`); each query's storage key is `${prefix}-${queryHash}`, so org A's keys never collide with org B (DIS-143). **`storage: undefined` while `orgId` is null OR on the server** (`persistEnabled = typeof window !== "undefined" && !!orgId`) — no anon-bucket cross-org bleed. The keyed remount (`key={orgKey}`) still resets the in-memory cache on switch.
- **`buster` = MANUAL version constant** (`persistCacheVersion()` → `PERSIST_CACHE_VERSION`, currently `"1"`), bumped BY HAND only on an incompatible shape change — NEVER the git commit SHA (the SHA flips every deploy, ~12×/day → wiped the disk cache on ~every visit, defeating persistence). Expiry is a coarse safety net at 30 days, so `buster` is the practical forced invalidation; cross-deploy shape drift is otherwise tolerated by `safeParse`/`z.coerce` on list readers + keep-last-good `structuralSharing`. Bump when you rename/remove a field on a response type consumed straight from cache with no safeParse guard (additive optional fields do NOT need a bump).
- **Persist filter = success + non-sensitive + ALLOWLISTED** via the persister `filters.predicate` → `shouldPersistQuery` (reused from the old `shouldDehydrateQuery`). `PERSISTABLE_QUERY_ROOTS` is the INVENTORY of every live non-sensitive root (big lists included); default-OFF for an unlisted root. Secrets EXCLUDED (`apiKeys`/`byokKeys`/`keySources` via `SENSITIVE_QUERY_ROOTS` — key material never on disk).
  - **A root nobody listed is a page that cold-skeletons on every visit, and NOTHING goes red — so a repo-wide guard now scans for it instead of a rule nobody re-reads.** `tsc` passes, every source-substring guard passes, the reveal-on-settle gates pass; the surface renders correctly and simply feels slow beside its neighbours, so it is reported as a loading-spinner complaint rather than a bug and the first instinct is to look at the gate, which is fine. `persist-query-cache.test.ts` walks `src` for every `useAuthQuery(["root", ...])` and fails on any root that is neither in `PERSISTABLE_QUERY_ROOTS` nor in `SENSITIVE_QUERY_ROOTS` — so adding a query and forgetting the allowlist is now a red test, not a slow page. Four roots had rotted that way by the time it was written (`offerFunnels`, `offerFunnelRevenue`, `offerFunnelPipelineActivity`, `leadReplyKind`). When it goes red, decide the root deliberately: allowlist it, or add it to the sensitive set if it carries key material. (#3659)

**A local-first cache is only local-first if READING it is cheaper than the network — the nav reseed reads KEYS, then only the values it will actually seed.** The store is one flat key space and the reseed treated it as a unit: `bucketEntries` pulled every value in the org's bucket over the IndexedDB boundary and `coldRestorablePairs` `JSON.parse`d each one, on EVERY navigation, then discarded the ones whose query was already warm in memory. That is the whole of "le dashboard est lent": measured on a 12.3MB bucket (40 ordinary snapshots plus one 60k-row leads list) it is **83.6ms of main-thread parse per page change, against 0.2ms for the same result** — and a heavy brand's leads list is nearer 100MB. The query key is recoverable from the STORAGE key (`${prefix}-${JSON.stringify(queryKey)}`), so `queryKeyFromStorageKey` + `coldStorageKeys` run the cold-guard on a string comparison and `valuesForKeys` reads only the survivors. Do NOT reintroduce a whole-bucket `entries()` read on a hot path; the storage adapter still exposes one because the persister's own API demands it. The cold-guard runs TWICE — once on the key, once after the value round-trip — because a query can resolve in between and seeding it then would stomp fresher data with an older snapshot.

**Nothing over `MAX_PERSISTED_ENTRY_BYTES` (2MB) goes to disk, and an entry that outgrows the cap is DELETED rather than left behind.** There was no cap, so one oversized snapshot taxed every page in the org. The offender is `brandLeads`: unpaginated by design (the revenue engine and the leads page both want the whole population), ~100MB of slim `view=basic` rows on a heavy brand. Refusing it is not a broken page — the query keeps `keepPreviousData` in memory and the per-query persister still restores it lazily on its own fetch; the only cost is that a genuinely enormous list cold-loads once per session instead of painting from disk, which is the right trade because reading it from disk was never fast either.

**The mount disk read is kicked in the `useState` INITIALIZER, not in an effect.** An effect runs after the children have mounted and rendered, so the first page of a session paints its skeleton and only then asks IndexedDB what it should have shown. `mountReadKicked` latches the nav effect's first run, or the `pathname` dep makes every mount read the bucket twice — the exact duplicate the removed `restoreQueries` used to cause.

**`gcTime` stays at 30 minutes. Do NOT raise it to keep navigation memory-warm** — that was considered and rejected: it applies to the un-persisted mega-lists too, so it would pin ~100MB of leads in the heap for the whole window, which is the #1273 OOM lever pointed at the problem it is meant to fix.

**A dashboard route is DYNAMIC, so Next's default prefetch fetches only its LAYOUTS — the page's own payload waits for the click, and what fills the wait is the nearest `loading.tsx`.** There are four boundaries for 31 routes, so for every funnel / leg / campaign sub-route the nearest one is `offers/[offerId]/loading.tsx`: drilling into a funnel blanked the WHOLE offer area to a full-page skeleton. Two fixes, one per navigation shape. A `<Link>` takes `prefetch` (the sidebar's `SidebarLink`). A row that navigates with `router.push` — which is every drill-down table, because a row carries its own controls and cannot be an anchor — warms its href on `onMouseEnter` AND `onFocus` through `useRoutePrefetch` (`lib/use-route-prefetch.ts`, one fetch per href per mount). Hover rather than a blanket prefetch on every row: only rows a human points at are fetched, so a long table does not fire one request per visible row. A NEW drill-down row needs the same two handlers or it reintroduces the skeleton on its own leg of the path.

**The poll is 5s, and the one thing it may NOT do is make somebody wait longer than that.** The instant paint is still the point — the cache shows the previous answer before any request goes out — but the freshness traded for it is bounded: owner-decided, *"on peut attendre pour le refresh… si ce n'est que je ne veux pas qu'on attende plus de 5s"*. It was raised to 60s on the belief that the producer's cache made a faster interval expensive; that belief was wrong twice over. **features-service's view-cache TTL is 5s, not 30s** (`src/lib/view-cache.ts` `DEFAULT_TTL_MS = 5_000`, no `FEATURE_VIEW_SNAPSHOT_TTL_MS` on the box), and inside the TTL a request is served from the snapshot with NO fan-out while past it the snapshot is served anyway plus one background refresh — so **the expensive cross-service work runs at most once per TTL however often this polls.** The interval buys freshness on screen; it does not multiply upstream work. A number is therefore at most ~10s old (5s snapshot age + 5s to the next poll). Generalise: a "lowering this is dangerous" comment naming another service's TTL is a point-in-time assertion like any other — read the producer before repeating it.

**The one slower tier is `LEADS_POLL_INTERVAL` (15s), and it is about PAYLOAD, never freshness.** `brandLeads` / `campaignLeads` are unpaginated by design (the revenue engine and the leads page both want the whole population) and run to ~100MB of slim rows on a heavy brand, per open tab — at 5s that is 12x the transfer of the old cadence, continuously, for a list whose contents only move when the BACKEND sends. Every user action that can change it invalidates it explicitly, so the interval is only the backstop for somebody else's sending. The revenue read on the same page stays on the fast tier: it is small, and it is exactly what a reader watches after stating an outcome. A NEW read joins the 5s default unless its payload is in that class.

**A USER'S OWN write re-reads EVERY grain the fact is served under — `lib/write-invalidation.ts` is the one list, and the poll is not the answer to it.** The trap is that ONE fact lives under SEVERAL query roots: the same money is asked for per channel, per campaign, per offer, per funnel and per brand, each its own answer from features-service, so a mutation that invalidates the root it happens to know about leaves every sibling grain on the pre-write figure — and the person who just clicked is looking at exactly those figures. A lead statement invalidated `featureRevenue` alone, so the campaign's cards moved while the brand Overview, the offer money, the funnel walk, the per-campaign rows and the audience costs did not. Two sets, and both are exhaustive by construction: `LEAD_OUTCOME_ROOTS` (the six money grains + `featureAudienceStats` + `featureStats` + both activity grains) and `CAMPAIGN_MONEY_ROOTS` (`campaigns`, `campaign`, `brandDailyBudget`, `brandSpendableBudget`, `offerFunnels`). Four things are load-bearing: **`brandSpendableBudget` is the one every budget writer forgot** — it is the join of billing's ceilings to campaign-service's statuses, so it moves on a pause exactly as on a budget edit and it is what the header money reads; **`workflowProjection` is deliberately ABSENT** from the lead set (a cross-org fleet benchmark one lead cannot move, and one of the app's most expensive reads); **`brandFunnelBudgets` is deliberately ABSENT** from the budget set (both writers already `setQueryData` billing's own answer into it, and a re-read that fails would fall back to the pre-save figure); and the module **imports nothing at all** (the client is taken structurally) so it carries real unit tests — keep it that way. React Query's default `refetchType: "active"` means this refetches what is on screen and marks the rest stale, which is the shape wanted. Guards: `tests/write-invalidation.test.ts` + `tests/poll-interval.test.ts`, and they pin the CALL SITES, not only the helper.

⚠️ **The money still lands in ~5s, not instantly, and that is the producer's cache — do not paper over it.** A refetch fired the instant a write returns can still be served the pre-write snapshot, and the honest fix is features-service invalidating its own view-cache on write, not a browser-side recomputation (which is the compute-a-stat-in-the-browser bug). What IS held locally is the statement the person just made (`statedReply`, `statedReplyKinds`) — their own words over the round trip, dropped the moment the producer answers.

**`isLoading` is `isPending && isFetching`, so a query the org-consistency gate has DISABLED reports `isLoading: false` while still unresolved** — the terminal branch fires and the surface flashes its empty / not-found state before the read runs. Every live surface reads `isPending`; `workflow-detail-panel.tsx` is the one place a disabled query's eternal `isPending` is correct (its `enabled: !!workflow` cannot stay false on a settled page, and the panel above is already a skeleton meanwhile). Guards: `tests/instant-paint.test.ts`, plus `tests/persist-query-cache.test.ts`, whose root scan now reads the whole KEY ARGUMENT — nine call sites pick their key with a ternary, and a pattern anchored on `useAuthQuery(["` saw neither branch.

**Irreducible:** the genuine FIRST-EVER load on an empty cache still waits on the cold Neon backend (~20-30s, features/api/runs scale-to-zero 0.25 CU). Killing that needs backend warm-up (other repos), NOT a dashboard cache change.

**Backend-DOWN must degrade to STALE, never to a skeleton — and the persister has TWO disk-restore paths, each with a hole that a down backend un-masks.** The per-query persister self-restores a query from disk ONLY when that query actually FETCHES (i.e. `enabled`); the org-consistency gate (`use-auth-query.ts`) disables every `useAuthQuery` while Clerk's active-org is still settling, so during that window the disabled queries never fetch → never self-restore. The other path — the mount seed `persister.restoreQueries` (query-provider.tsx) — is ONE-SHOT per provider mount. So a page reached while the gate is momentarily closed, OR a sub-page whose memory was GC'd (30-min `gcTime`), cold-skeletons even though its stale snapshot sits on disk. Backend-HEALTHY hides this (the network answers within seconds); backend-DOWN turns the transient into a STUCK multi-minute skeleton on a page the user opens 10×/day — the local-first cache silently not painting. Diagnose by reading the disk directly in the browser console: `(await indexedDB.databases())` + a `keyval-store` getAllKeys — if `distribute-dashboard-cache:<org>-["<root>",…]` entries EXIST for the affected org/brand while the page skeletons, the data is on disk and NOT being painted (a restore-path bug, NOT eviction; eviction = the store/entries are absent, a different failure fixed by `requestPersistentStorage` #2336). **Fix (#2574): `reseedColdQueriesFromDisk` re-seeds COLD queries from this org's on-disk snapshot on EVERY org-scoped navigation, decoupled from `enabled`/network** — pure guarded helper `coldRestorablePairs` (org-prefixed = DIS-143, buster-checked = never paint stale-shaped data, COLD-GUARDED = `getQueryData!==undefined` skip so a reseed never stomps fresher live memory), read-only `setQueryData`, zero network. Any new org-scoped restore/reveal work must keep the invariant: painting a present disk snapshot is decoupled from the gate + the network, so a down backend always shows stale. (#2574, sibling of the #2336/#2337 eviction+org-gate fix — distinct: there the disk was empty, here it's present-but-unpainted.)

**Rule:** persistence is GLOBAL — one per-query persister wired as a default query option, never a second provider / per-page persister. `experimental_createQueryPersister` is `experimental_`-prefixed (stable behavior, API may shift — pin to the installed `@tanstack/react-query-persist-client` version). Keep `PERSISTABLE_QUERY_ROOTS` in lockstep with live queries. (#1273 small-allowlist origin; #1278/DIS-170 30min+allowlist; 2026-06-23 persist-all-localStorage regression; 2026-06-25 KevinLourd/dashboard-instant-cache = per-query IndexedDB; 2026-08-02 investor-update-ram-leak = bucket-scoped reads + a bounded store, the current design.)

### keep-last-good (`structuralSharing`, cache-write boundary) — a SUCCESSFUL refetch returning VALID-but-degenerate data must not collapse the UI

5th "don't revert resolved state on a transient" layer — and the ONLY one for a **successful** refetch that legitimately returns **worse** data. The other four all assume the new data is good: 1. `keepPreviousData` (key-change / error→undefined) · 2. `useCoordinatedReveal` (reveal decision) · 3. `useMonotonicStatuses` (status downgrade *ordering* on a poll) · 4. persisted cache (eviction). **The gap:** a 200 whose nullable field flips non-null→null (a cold Neon chain — api→features→workflow/runs/email-gateway/brand, all scale-to-zero — answers a poll/refocus refetch with zeroed unit costs or fewer rows while half-warming). `keepPreviousData` can NOT help — the null IS the new valid query data, so React Query installs it and any UI **derived** off it (budget cards, a `disabled` gate, a badge, a $ amount) collapses mid-session.

**Industry-aligned fix (researched: TkDodo / TanStack docs / SWR all agree there is NO built-in for successful-but-worse — you intercept at the cache-write boundary).** TanStack's hook is `structuralSharing(prev, next)` (SWR's is `compare`, Apollo's is a `merge` field policy). Helpers in `src/lib/keep-last-good.ts`: `keepLastGoodFields(prev, next, fields, label)` (per-field coalesce: keep prev's non-null when next nulls it) + `keepLastGoodList(prev, next, {keyFn, fields, label})` (keyed merge: per-item coalesce + RETAIN a prev item that vanished from next). Both `console.error` the suppressed downgrade (fail-loud — a persistent real downgrade still logs; this is keep-resolved, NOT a silent fallback). Compose per query (e.g. `keepLastGoodWorkflowProjection` in `api.ts`, co-located with the type) and pass it as `structuralSharing` on the `useAuthQuery` — protects EVERY consumer of that query at ONE place, not per-component (a per-component latch is opt-in-and-forgettable, which is exactly how the collapse shipped).

**Five load-bearing rules:**
- **OPT-IN per query, NEVER a global default.** A null can legitimately mean "deleted/gone"; blanket last-good would mask real deletions. Apply ONLY where a null means "transient/not-ready" (cold compute, in-flight aggregation), not "removed".
- **Put it at the QUERY (`structuralSharing`), not at each consumer.** One merge → cards + button + badges all inherit it. The per-component `useLastGoodValue` latch is strictly weaker (forgettable).
- **List ONLY the fields whose null = transient.** Fields outside the allowlist let next win (incl. when null) — so a genuinely-cleared field still clears.
- **CUT the *steady-state* poll on econ-INDEPENDENT slow-changing queries — but keep a BOUNDED cold-start warm-up poll.** The workflow-projection unit costs are "fetched ONCE" by design (#1528/#1536) — a perpetual 5s `...pollOptions` on them only re-hits the cold Neon chain for zero steady-state benefit (cards update from the client `econLive` recompute). BUT "fetch-once + `refetchOnWindowFocus`" alone is NOT correct: **keep-last-good cannot heal the FIRST fetch** (no prior good value to keep), and the cold Neon chain most often returns its degenerate-200 (null unit costs / empty workflows) on exactly that first request — so a cold load STICKS on the empty-state ("No workflow has website-visit cost data yet") until a manual window-refocus. The right shape is a **conditional `refetchInterval`**: poll (~4s) ONLY while the payload has no usable data (e.g. no workflow with non-null `costPerCloseUsd`), capped at N tries (so a genuinely-empty brand stops), `false` the instant it warms. This self-heals cold-start without perpetual polling. (#1557-regression: #1542 dropped the poll entirely calling it "pointless"; it was the cold-start self-heal — keep-last-good #1548 only covers a later bad refetch of an already-good cache, never the first fetch.)
- **TanStack types `structuralSharing` params as `unknown`** — pass `(prev, next) => keepLastGoodX(prev as T | undefined, next as T)`.

**Rule:** any **polled / refetch-prone** query with **nullable fields that GATE or compute UI** (disabled-button, empty-state, $ amount, recommended pick) MUST pass a keep-last-good `structuralSharing`. Pure read-display surfaces that already degrade to "—" on null are lower-risk (phased-sweep, not a hotfix). (#1542-regression: the launch-page budget cards + Launch button — then `campaigns/new`, now `brands/[brandId]/launch` — collapsed when the polled `workflowProjection` caught a valid-but-degenerate 200 mid-session; `econLive`/§2 inputs were NOT the cause — they're latched. The poll predated #1542; #1542 widened it by recomputing cpc client-side. Fix: keep-last-good `structuralSharing` on the query + drop the pointless 5s poll. Other projection/revenue surfaces — conversions/workflows/brand-overview/report + top-workflows card — are the phased follow-up; the campaign-LEVEL variants (`campaigns/[id]/overview`, top-campaigns card) were removed in #1768.)

### A form seeded from a query must RE-SEED when the payload changes — a once-per-mount latch reads the DISK snapshot and ignores the server (7th "don't get stuck" layer)

The six layers above all keep RESOLVED state from being reverted. This one is the mirror image: local component state copied OUT of a query, which then never hears about the fresher answer. Any editable surface does this — it cannot bind inputs straight to `data`, so it seeds a `useState` from the query and lets the user type.

**The trap is that the FIRST payload to settle is the on-disk one.** With the local-first per-query IndexedDB cache, a restored snapshot resolves before the network does, so a `hydrated.current` boolean latch seeds the form from the PREVIOUS VISIT's copy and the fresh server payload that lands a moment later updates the cache and nothing else. Compounding it: **`setQueryData` after a mutation does NOT go through the query function, so it is never persisted** — the disk keeps the pre-write copy, and the write the user just made is the thing that gets overwritten on the next mount.

The symptom is the worst kind: the value IS saved, the server has it, and the screen shows blank. It reads as the product dropping the save, so the bug gets reported against the write path where there is nothing to find.

- **Re-seed when the payload is a DIFFERENT OBJECT than the one the form was built from** (keep the last-seeded reference in a ref). Identity, not deep equality: React Query returns the same reference when nothing changed (structural sharing), so an unchanged refetch costs nothing and cannot loop.
- **Two things outrank the server: a field the user has EDITED, and the card/row they have OPEN.** A form that rewrites itself mid-edit is worse than a stale one. The mutation's own `onSuccess` seeds those from its response, which is also what guarantees the card shows exactly what persisted.
- The `hydrated` flag stays, but only to answer "has this ever been seeded" for the settle gate — never as "seeding is finished".

Reference impl: `brand-sales-funnels-card.tsx` (`seededFrom` ref + the `touched || openKey === def.key` guard). Guards in `tests/sales-funnels.test.ts`. (#3342 — a saved `meetingBookedToAttendedPct` rendered blank on the customer's Settings page while brand-service had held the value for hours.)

### Reveal on SETTLE, never success-only — an ERRORED gate query must not eternal-skeleton (6th "don't get stuck" layer)

**A `useCoordinatedReveal` / skeleton barrier gated on `data !== undefined` (success-only) SKELETONS FOREVER when its query ERRORS — this is the #1 cause of the recurring "page still shows skeleton" reports, and it is INVISIBLE to every cache/persist/reseed fix.** The five layers above (keepPreviousData, coordinated-reveal, monotonic-status, persisted cache, keep-last-good) all assume the query eventually SUCCEEDS. None cover the case where the gate query keeps FAILING: a cold Neon chain (features→downstream, all scale-to-zero) intermittently returns an aborted fetch / 5xx / `ECONNRESET`, React Query exhausts `retry:1`, `data` stays `undefined` forever, and any barrier keyed on `data !== undefined` never latches → permanent skeleton, no error UI, no recovery. The fast sibling cards are held hostage if they AND the failing query.

**Rule — reveal on SETTLE (resolved OR errored):** destructure `isError` from every `useAuthQuery` that feeds a reveal barrier, and gate on `data !== undefined || isError`, never `data !== undefined` alone. On error the region paints its null-safe "—"/stale content (a DISPLAY fail-soft — the error still logs loud via React Query + the reader's safeParse throw, this is keep-resolved not a swallowed fetch); the monotonic latch keeps it revealed while the next poll recovers real data. **This is ORTHOGONAL to the org-gate + disk-reseed work (#2336/#2574):** a query DISABLED by the org-consistency gate has `isError:false` → NOT settled → still (correctly) waits for reseed-from-disk; reveal-on-settle covers the DIFFERENT case where the query is enabled, fires, and FAILS.

**Two co-defects that turn one failing query into a whole-page skeleton (both must be fixed together):**
- **Single load-bearing gate.** Do NOT gate heterogeneous cards on ONE query's reveal (the "ONE barrier PER card" rule). The brand Overview gated the entire section + cost card + the fast `featureStats` outreach cards on the slow `/revenue` reveal — so `/revenue` failing skeletoned everything. Gate each card on ITS OWN query's settle.
- **Defensive `!data` RE-GUARD in the child component.** A `const loading = pending || !data` inside the presentational component (`revenue-overview-section.tsx`) RE-LOCKS the skeleton even after the page forces reveal-on-error (page passes `pending=false` but `data` is undefined → `!data` wins). Drop the `!data` re-guard; make sub-fields null-safe (`data?.x` → "—"). The child must trust the page's `pending` flag, never re-derive loading from `!data`. **A component carries ONE gate per query, so fixing the reported one and leaving its siblings ships the bug with a smaller blast radius — grep the whole file for `pending || !` and fix every hit in the same commit.** #2650 fixed `revenueLoading` and left `activityLoading = activityPending || !pipelineActivity` two lines under it, so the identical eternal skeleton reappeared 2026-08-08 the moment `pipeline-activity` 502'd. **Where a gate genuinely cannot take an absent payload** (`PipelineActivityChart` reads `data.days`), the answer is a branch STATING the reason, never a skeleton — and inline the `!data` check at the render site, since a derived `const` boolean does not narrow the prop for TypeScript.

**And check each region against the query that actually FEEDS it — a region sharing a neighbour's gate goes blank on an outage of an endpoint it never reads.** The Outcome card's cumulative line is `pipelineActualSeries` off `/revenue`, which was serving fine; only its dashed forward projection comes from pipeline-activity, and that degrades to nothing. It was grey for 20 minutes purely by association.

~100 prior "skeleton" PRs all touched cache/reseed (#2074/#2107/#2129/#2336/#2574); none gave the gate query a failure exit. Guarded by `overview-reveal-on-settle.test.ts`. (#2650, #3303)

### Public marketing pages — SEO + AI-scraper rendering

Public landing/marketing/docs (`apps/landing/`, `apps/sales-cold-emails-landing/`, `apps/docs/`): default **ISR + `unstable_cache` + edge cache**, NEVER Suspense streaming on indexable content. AI scrapers (GPTBot, ClaudeBot, PerplexityBot, Bingbot AI) parse raw HTML only — a Suspense fallback means they index the skeleton, not the data.
1. No `<Suspense>` on indexable content (hero, h1/h2, body, JSON-LD, leaderboards, tables, blog cards, stats — SSR-sync). Suspense only for user-specific non-indexable widgets.
2. Never combine `force-dynamic` with `revalidate` (force-dynamic wins, ISR silently off). Prefer ISR (`export const revalidate = N`) alone.
3. `headers()`/`cookies()` in a page forces dynamic rendering — drop them, resolve hostname from `NEXT_PUBLIC_*`.
4. Wrap every fetch/DB call in `unstable_cache` with explicit tags.
5. Invalidate via `revalidateTag(tag, profile)` — Next 16.2+ requires the 2nd arg (`"default"` for ordinary invalidations).
6. `unstable_cache` at module top level breaks vitest — mock `next/cache` globally in `tests/setup.ts` (identity pass-through; `revalidate*` → `vi.fn()`), declare `setupFiles` in `vitest.config.ts`.
7. Validate scraper output after any caching change: `apps/landing/scripts/seo-snapshot.sh` + `measure-ttfb.sh`; pre/post snapshots in the PR. (#1153)
8. **Skeleton-leak check (seo-snapshot does NOT catch this).** `seo-snapshot.sh` counts h1/h2/article/JSON-LD — it does NOT count `animate-pulse`, so a page that ships data behind `<Suspense>` passes the snapshot while serving skeletons to LLMs. Add `curl -sA "GPTBot" https://distribute.you/<page> | grep -c animate-pulse` — **>0 = the data is behind Suspense/client-fetch and invisible to AI scrapers**, fix it. Canonical fix = `/investors` + `/pricing` (#1520): both were ISR + `<Suspense fallback={Skeleton}>` around async server components → 314/102 skeletons in raw HTML. Removing the Suspense (the async sections await straight into the static prerender, mirroring `/benchmarks`) dropped both to 0. **A page that's already `revalidate=N` ISR gains nothing from `<Suspense>` on its own data — drop it.** When de-Suspensing, make the data fetch **fail-soft** (try/catch → zeros) so a build-time fetch throw can't abort the prerender (`data-sections.tsx` `EMPTY_INVESTOR_METRICS`). Guarded by `investors-page-ssr.test.ts`. **Measuring page byte-composition: never `grep -oE 'ld+json.*'` on minified single-line HTML — `.*` grabs the whole doc, not the block; use a real parser (python non-greedy per-`<script>`).** (A grep artifact once mis-reported a 4 KB JSON-LD block as "2.27 MB"; the real bloat was the 2.2 MB RSC flight payload `self.__next_f`, which scrapers ignore.)

**Fixing an Ahrefs / external site-audit: target the SERVED HTML, never reconstruct from source metadata exports.** The audit overview gives issue COUNTS, not per-URL data. Pull the per-URL row export (Ahrefs MCP `site-audit-issues` / API / UI CSV) OR crawl the live served HTML — do NOT reverse-engineer which page is flagged from the Next `metadata` source. Source reconstruction produces false positives because: (a) `title` templates compound (`page title "X — distribute"` + layout `template "%s | distribute Y"` = doubled brand + over-length — the served `<title>`, not the raw string, is what Ahrefs measures); (b) Next metadata **inherits** down the layout tree (a child page with no `openGraph`/`twitter` is NOT missing them — it inherits the root layout's); (c) a layout `title.default` is **overridden** by any child `page.tsx` `title` (the default may never be served); (d) `description` constants (`PAGE_DESCRIPTION`/`SITE_DESCRIPTION`) and JSON-LD `description` fields are NOT the HTML meta description — a 166-char `Service.description` in `application/ld+json` is structured data, not the `<meta name="description">`; (e) a `<html>` literal inside a JSDoc comment fools a naive `grep '<html'`. Incident 2026-06-04 (#1310 / DIS-69): 4 such false positives caught only by direct served-HTML / template-aware verification. Ship ONLY directly-verified served defects; defer count-only issues (OG-incomplete, structured-data, redirects) to DIS-69 pending the per-URL export. The recurring SEO-audit home is **DIS-69** (Ahrefs project 9700515) — update it, don't open a new issue.

### Public pages — no horizontal overflow on mobile

Every public app ships **`html { overflow-x: clip }`** in `globals.css` — never `overflow-x: hidden` (`hidden` makes html/body a scroll container, breaks `position: sticky`; `clip` doesn't). It's a safety net, NOT the fix — real spills are fixed at the element level. Wide legit content (code, perf tables) scrolls internally via its own `overflow-x-auto` (else `clip` HIDES it). Decorative `absolute -inset-N` glows must stay in-viewport: a full-bleed component whose `max-w-*` box reaches the edge bleeds the glow past it → body scroll; keep every home-page component inside the standard `max-w-* mx-auto px-4 sm:px-6 lg:px-8` container. Verify with `apps/landing/scripts/overflow-audit.mjs [BASE_URL] [--app=landing|sales|docs]` (headless Playwright at 360/390/414px, separates escaping spills from contained); subagent/static analysis is NOT reliable for overflow. (#1284) **The audit auto-discovers React routes ONLY — it SKIPS the static-served landing pages (`src/app/<page>/route.ts` → `staticResponse("….html")`: `/pro`, `/agency`, `/use-cases`, `/how-it-works`, the whole `cold-email-*` cluster).** A "mobile review" that trusts the audit's pass count misses those pages — pass the explicit static routes too, or audit `public/landing/**.html` directly. Two real overflows hid there for months (`/cold-email-cost-guide` `.guide-channels` stayed 2-col at ≤640px → 2×202px spill; `/cold-email-for-saas-founders` inline `repeat(4,1fr)` stat grid couldn't collapse → 538px spill) because auto-discovery never loaded them. Distinguish REAL overflow (body `scrollWidth` > viewport) from contained element-spill (`scrollWidth == vw`, masked by `clip`) — only the former is a bug. (#1745)

### Landing voice — sell scale, not solitude

The ICP ("The Serial Builder", `/investors`) is a solo / 1–3-person team running 3–10 products — keep "solo founder" as an SEO keyword + persona. **But never celebrate the solo lifestyle in copy** ("Stay solo. Go big.", "$0→$1M MRR solo", "you don't need a team" demotivate a founder who wants to grow + undersell the platform). The dream we sell = variable CAC + measurable channels per product across a portfolio. The investors dream quote stays "whether I stay 1 person or grow to 10." (#1162)

### Landing billing-truth invariants — the model is GOOGLE ADS, so "No subscription" is TRUE; sell RESULTS not the per-email price

- **The billing model is Google Ads: the customer sets a daily budget, is charged for what the campaign spent, and the balance tops itself up at a threshold so the campaign can keep running.** Nobody calls Google Ads a subscription, and neither do we. So **"No subscription", "No seats, no retainer", "Pay-as-you-go", "Cancel anytime", "Pause anytime" and "No monthly fee" are all TRUE and shippable** — there is no seat, no retainer, no term, no recurring fee, and stopping is a budget you set to zero. What stays banned is denying the **RECHARGE**, which is a different fact and is genuinely mandatory on a recurring campaign: never write "Credits never auto-renew", "no auto-renew", or "no surprise charges", because the balance really does top itself up. **Subscription ≠ auto-recharge.** The previous version of this rule conflated the two ("the product is a per-day SUBSCRIPTION with mandatory auto-topup → never write no subscription") and that conflation stripped a true claim off the homepage twice — #1739, then #3434, reverted the same day it shipped. Kevin 2026-08-18: *"il n'y a aucune subscription!!! On est comme Google Ads!!! Et oui ils peuvent cancel anytime."* The auto-topup fact that started this is a DASHBOARD launch-gate rule (recurring → auto-topup mandatory) and says nothing about what to call the model on the landing. Note the two halves of this file already agreed: the business-model section above calls "no seat, no retainer, no subscription" the actual selling point. (#1738, #3434)
- **Sell the RESULT, not the means.** Do NOT quote distribute's per-email/per-contact price ($0.07/contact, $1.75/reply). Quote industry-benchmarked **result economics: $4–$7 per click, $70–$250 per positive reply (= a buyer conversation worth engaging)**. Keep COMPETITOR figures untouched (LinkedIn $8–15/click, InMail $3–5/msg, agency $3–10k, SDR, DIY $0.65). Consequence accepted: distribute is no longer visually "cheapest per contact" — comparison visuals position it as mid-market done-for-you, not 1000× cheaper. The OFFER is **"$25 free credits"** = a $1-per-$1 match up to the first $25 spent (NOT a no-card free trial — a match needs a card). (#1732, #1728) **Same principle for the MECHANISM: landing copy never exposes internal model/workflow vocabulary ("best-performing playbook", "best model", "cheapest workflow that delivers", "cross-org workflow") — a visitor does not care which model produced the number. State the practical live price a user gets ("what a positive reply for a sales meeting costs right now, live"), not how we produce it. Kevin 2026-07-19: "on ne veut pas rentrer dans le détail technique de quel est notre model… c'est ce que ça donne en user en pratique." (#2810/#2811)

### Landing logo discipline — borrow trust from providers

distribute = "Stripe of Distribution" (thin wrapper over Apollo, Anthropic, Resend, LinkedIn, Muck Rack, Featured, Adobe, Gartner…). Wherever a provider/source/tool is named on a public page, show its logo via `apps/landing/src/components/provider-avatar.tsx` (`logo.dev` + `NEXT_PUBLIC_LOGO_DEV_TOKEN`, initial-letter fallback). NEVER hand-roll a provider SVG, NEVER ship the initial fallback as the intended UI — set the token in every env. New Channels-grid mappings in `apps/landing/src/data/feature-providers.ts`; stat/study mappings via `SourcedStat.providerDomain` / `ExternalStudy.providerDomain`.

### There is NO alpha gating in the dashboard — a `useFeatureFlag` gate there DELETES a surface, it does not stage one

`useFeatureFlag` returned `false` unconditionally in `apps/dashboard` from the 2026-06-14 admin/dashboard split onward, so every gated entry rendered for NOBODY — staff included — and its page was reachable only by typing the URL. That reads in a diff as a staged alpha and behaves as orphaned code, which is why it survives: nobody looks at it, so nothing about it gets fixed and its contents drift out of date unnoticed. Three whole surfaces sat that way for months and were deleted in one pass:

| Deleted from `apps/dashboard` | Lives in |
|---|---|
| `orgs/[orgId]/brands/[brandId]/brand-info` | `apps/admin` |
| `orgs/[orgId]/brands/[brandId]/workflows` (+ `/new`), `(dashboard)/workflows`, `components/workflows/*`, `api/v1/workflows/[id]/required-providers` | `apps/admin` |
| `orgs/[orgId]/services/crm` (+ `_components/*`) and its `services/crm/oauth/callback` | `apps/admin` |

Deleted with them, because they had no other reader: `lib/use-feature-flag.ts`, the whole `FEATURE_GATES` registry (`lib/feature-gates.ts` now exports only `Maturity`, `MATURITY_STYLES` and `GA_BRAND_FEATURES`), `lib/workflow-display-name.ts`, the six workflow readers in `lib/api.ts` (`listWorkflows` / `getWorkflow` / `getWorkflowSummary` / `getWorkflowKeyStatus` / `fetchGlobalRankedWorkflows` / `createWorkflow`) plus `triggerFeatureRun`, the breadcrumb's workflow crumb, and 24 persist roots the allowlist still listed.

- **A dashboard surface that needs a limited audience uses the EMAIL allowlist** (`lib/beta-allowlist.ts` + `useIsBetaUser`), which actually evaluates, and carries a visible `<MaturityBadge level="beta" />`. `MaturityBadge` stays — the beta Profile surfaces render it.
- **`GA_BRAND_FEATURES` stays and is live** (`sole-feature.ts` resolves the one feature a brand's surfaces are about). A feature graduates by being added to it.
- ⚠️ **`PERSISTABLE_QUERY_ROOTS` is an INVENTORY, so a root outlives its reader silently** — a page removal leaves its roots behind and nothing goes red. The dead set here was 24 entries, most orphaned by the earlier entity-page removals (#2593) rather than by this one. When you delete a surface, diff the allowlist against the live `useAuthQuery` roots (the scan in `persist-query-cache.test.ts` does the forward direction; the reverse is a grep for each listed root's literal across `src`).
- Guard: `tests/feature-gates.test.ts` fails if the hook file returns, if anything under `src` CALLS it, or if any of the deleted directories reappears. It matches the CALL (`useFeatureFlag(`), not the bare name, because several files legitimately EXPLAIN this history in a comment and a guard that trips on its own rationale is the source-substring trap this repo keeps recording.

**`apps/admin` keeps the real PostHog-driven gating and is unaffected** — the rest of this section describes THAT app.

### Feature maturity gating (alpha / beta / ga) — admin
Immature features stay in prod but are hidden from non-staff via **PostHog feature flags**, NOT `NODE_ENV`. `alpha` → staff only (flag on person-property `email = kevin.lourd@gmail.com`) · `beta` → opt-in cohort · `ga` → everyone, NO flag.
- Single source: `src/lib/feature-gates.ts` `FEATURE_GATES` (surface key → `{ flag, maturity }`). Flag name `<maturity>-<surface>`. **Each distinct gated surface gets its OWN flag — do NOT reuse an adjacent surface's flag for convenience** (couples graduation; the `<maturity>-<surface>` convention is one-flag-per-surface). When gating a new surface, create a dedicated PostHog flag mirroring an existing alpha flag's targeting (e.g. `alpha-brand-database` = Outlets/Journalists/Articles Database rows, staff-only, ≠ `alpha-brand-features`). (#1343: recommended reusing `alpha-brand-features`; Kevin picked "go dedicated".)
- `useFeatureFlag(flag): boolean` (`src/lib/use-feature-flag.ts`) — **default-hidden** (false until PostHog loads → no flash); subscribes `posthog.onFeatureFlags` to re-resolve after `identify`.
- `<MaturityBadge level>` (`src/components/maturity-badge.tsx`) — amber alpha / violet beta.
- Pattern: call `useFeatureFlag` at component top (hooks rule), gate the render, attach the badge:
```tsx
const ok = useFeatureFlag(FEATURE_GATES["services-crm"].flag);
{ok && <SidebarLink item={{ ..., maturity: FEATURE_GATES["services-crm"].maturity }} />}
```
- Graduation = widen flag targeting in the PostHog UI (no redeploy). Flags created/edited via PostHog MCP (`mcp__posthog__exec` → `create/update-feature-flag`). "Disappear for everyone" = hard JSX removal, NOT a flag defaulted-off.
- **EVERY surface that LISTS features must apply the brand-features filter `features.filter((f) => GA_BRAND_FEATURES.has(f.slug) || featuresAlphaOk)` — not just the sidebar.** There are THREE such surfaces and they must stay in lockstep: `BrandLevelSidebar` (sidebar), `brands/[brandId]/page.tsx` (overview grid), AND the `breadcrumb-nav.tsx` feature switcher (BOTH the app-level `appFeature` dropdown AND the brand-level `feature` dropdown render `visibleFeatures.map`, not `features.map`). A new feature-listing UI defaults to leaking alpha to non-staff if it maps the raw `useFeatures().features` — gate it. (#1410: breadcrumb switcher shipped without the filter; sidebar+overview had it, breadcrumb was the forgotten third surface.)
- **Deleting/renaming a flag the CURRENTLY-DEPLOYED prod code still reads HIDES the gated surface for EVERYONE (incl. staff) during the deploy gap.** `useFeatureFlag(<deleted>)` resolves false. So when a rename ships (`alpha-X` → `alpha-Y`, or an alpha→GA that drops a gate), delete the OLD flag only AFTER the new code is live in prod (Vercel deploy READY), never during the PR. Order: ship code → confirm prod READY → delete old flag. Deleting early = the gated entry vanishes until the new build lands. (#1338: deleted `alpha-feature-settings` while #1337 prod still gated the Feature Settings entry on it → entry disappeared for everyone for ~2 min until #1338 deployed.)
- **In the DASHBOARD app `useFeatureFlag()` is HARD-DISABLED — it returns `false` for everyone (alpha/beta live only in the admin app since the 2026-06-14 split). So a NEW dashboard surface that must ship to a small set of people (a beta) CANNOT gate on `useFeatureFlag` — it would be invisible to everyone, silently.** Gate on the email allowlist instead: `src/lib/beta-allowlist.ts` (`BETA_ALLOWED_EMAILS` + `isBetaEmail`) + `useIsBetaUser()` (reads the Clerk `useUser()` email), mirroring `apps/admin/src/lib/admin-allowlist.ts`. The gate applies to BOTH the sidebar entry AND the page body (page returns the "not available" card for non-beta). Drop the gate (not widen the list) to graduate to GA. Reference: the Signups / Booked Meetings / Sales outcome pages (#1654; the current cohort is in `BETA_ALLOWED_EMAILS` — Kevin + pressbeat). (The `FEATURE_GATES`/PostHog path below is admin-app-only now.)
- **The mirror of that rule, and the one that rots silently: an EXISTING dashboard surface still gated on `useFeatureFlag` is not "staff-only", it is DEAD — invisible to everyone, reachable only by typing its URL.** The hook returns `false` unconditionally, so `{flagEnabled && <SidebarLink/>}` renders for nobody. That reads in the diff as a staged alpha and behaves as an orphaned page, which is why it survives: nobody looks at it, so nothing about it gets fixed, and its contents drift out of date unnoticed. So when a task touches a dashboard surface, `grep` its nav entry for `useFeatureFlag` FIRST — if it is gated, the surface has been showing to no one since 2026-06-14 and "graduate it" means DELETING the gate (plus its `FEATURE_GATES` entry, which is then dead), never widening a PostHog flag. Treat everything on that page as unverified: it was last exercised by whoever wrote it. Cost 2026-08-16 (#3400, the org API-key page): gated on `alpha-keys`, so the owner did not know the page existed; three of its four install snippets had rotted into commands that fail on paste (see below).
- **A surface that prints a COMMAND or a PACKAGE NAME for a person to paste must have that command run before it ships — `npm view <pkg>` is five seconds and the failure is total.** This is the "prove the artifact WORKS through the real consumer" rule (see the investor-update markdown case) applied to install instructions: rendering the snippet proves nothing, because the consumer is a shell on someone else's machine. The org API-key page advertised `npx @distribute/mcp` and `@distribute/api-client`; **neither exists on npm** (the MCP repo is `@mcpfactory/mcp-service`, `private: true`, retired brand), and its curl authenticated with `X-API-Key`, which is api-service's separate ADMIN path — an org key sent that way is answered `401 Invalid admin key`. A user key is a Bearer token (`src/middleware/auth.ts`, path 2). Shipping a GA page whose commands 404 is worse than shipping no page, so the honest move is to delete every snippet that does not resolve and restore it when the package is real. (#3400)
- **The org API key page (`/orgs/:orgId/api-keys`) is GA and hands out exactly ONE thing: a Distribute key.** The second section, where an org pasted its own third-party credentials, was REMOVED — we run every vendor ourselves, so `listByokKeys` / `setByokKey` / `deleteByokKey` / `listKeySources` / `setKeySource` must not come back to this page (guarded: `tests/org-api-keys-ga.test.ts`; those readers survive in `lib/api.ts` with no caller). A key authenticates as its org through key-service and then reaches every route the dashboard does, with **no scopes and no expiry** — revoking is the only limit, accepted deliberately as of #3400. `/provider-keys` stays a redirect so old links resolve.
- **STAFF/GOD-MODE preview gating (distinct from beta) = `useIsAdminUser()` (`src/lib/use-admin-user.ts`, reads Clerk email → `isAdminEmail`), mirroring `useIsBetaUser`.** Use it (not the beta allowlist) for a surface only STAFF should preview. Gate BOTH the nav entry and the page body; badge rides the nav entry (per the whole-page-gate corollary below). Since the dashboard has NO edge allowlist, a page gated only on this hook is a UI-visibility gate over the viewer's OWN org data, never a data-security boundary. **The campaign-centered v2 Campaigns surface is the case this rule used to name, and it is now GA — every gate and every beta badge is GONE from it** (`campaignsOk = isRevenueFeature(featureSlug)` in `context-sidebar.tsx`, both sidebar levels badge-free, and neither `campaigns-page.tsx` nor `campaign-overview-page.tsx` reads `useIsAdminUser`; guarded by `tests/campaigns-page.test.ts` + `tests/context-sidebar.test.ts`, which now fail if the hook reappears in the sidebar or either page). **Campaign re-intro is MULTI-STEP (owner-approved, #2761/#2762) — the campaign concept is being progressively re-added despite the "#1768 removed campaigns" notes elsewhere in this file; step 1 is READ-ONLY (list + per-campaign pipeline/$CAC/ROI/%CAC from features-service `getFeatureRevenueByCampaign` groupBy=campaignId + right panel + global header). Today = 1 campaign/brand so the list shows 1 row until multi-campaign creation lands. Before editing the brand sidebar or "campaign removed" surfaces, know this surface exists — extend it, don't re-invent or delete it.** (#2761, GA'd 2026-08-02)
  - **Campaign v2 step 2 (#2771) — per-campaign OVERVIEW + LEADS drill-down + a campaign SIDEBAR level.** `.../campaigns/[id]` renders `components/campaigns/campaign-overview-page.tsx`, a PARALLEL of the brand Overview rather than a shared body, to keep the GA page clear of staff-preview risk and its source-substring guards green. Revenue + `/stats` scope to `campaignId`; pipeline-activity / audiences / economics / pause stay brand-level (features-service serves those brand-scoped by design). `getNavigationLevel` gains a `"campaign"` level (`segments[4]==="campaigns" && segments[5]`) → `CampaignLevelSidebar`; `EngagedLeadsPage` takes an optional `campaignId`. Query keys are byte-equal to `OutreachStatCardsAuto`'s, so the polls dedupe.
  - **Campaign v2 step 3 — the per-campaign budget is DEPRECATED as a concept: money is funded per SALES FUNNEL and a campaign runs one funnel, so the ceiling belongs to the funnel.** campaign-service keeps a per-campaign figure only as a MIRROR of that funnel ceiling, because `gate-check` is the first node of every run and cannot call billing hot on a scale-to-zero compute. The campaign Overview keeps the brand-level read for the monthly outcome forecast, which asks a different question ("what does the money buy per month") and is answered with the SUM of every funnel's ceiling — a number that is right for the forecast and wrong under one campaign's name. Audiences + sales-economics stay BRAND-WIDE; per-campaign audience stats via `fetchFeatureAudienceStats(campaignId)`. ⚠️ This line used to end "so there is no per-campaign pause" and that was FALSE the whole time: `PATCH /campaigns/:id` has always taken `{status: "activate" | "stop"}`, proxied by api-service, and step 6 below now uses it. A note in this file saying a capability does not exist is a point-in-time assertion like any other — check the deployed contract (`api-registry`) before building around it, because the reflex when one is missing is to design a workaround, and here the workaround (defund the campaign) was destructive.

⚠️ **What stays deprecated is campaign-service's `maxBudgetDailyUsd` MIRROR, which nothing edits and nothing displays — not the idea of a per-campaign figure.** A campaign is (offer × funnel × channel) and billing keys a ceiling on exactly that triple (#3455), so the campaign's own money exists and four surfaces now state it: Campaign Settings EDITS it (#3456, the only writer), the Campaigns table states it per row, **the cost card states it as the DENOMINATOR beside today's spend** (#3710 — `dailyBudgetCents={campaignBudgetCentsValue}`, so the card reads `$50 / $50` the way the brand card does; the brand and offer Overviews state none for the opposite reason, since billing answers a brand with the SUM of every funnel and that is a wider scope than the numerator beside it, and a campaign with no funnel or channel has no billing row keyed to it so it states the reason through `budgetNote` rather than leaving a bare figure a reader takes for a denominator that failed to load), and **the campaign Overview states it read-only at its top right, beside the campaign's own status pill** (#3462) — a line, not a heading: the page states no campaign identity of its own (the top bar names the open campaign, once per screen), so this says only whether it is running and what it may spend while it does. That figure is NOT the run-status bar coming back: the bar printed the BRAND's sum next to a retired goal word, this is the campaign's own row at the campaign's own grain, and the pill and the whole-dollar formatter are the ones every other campaign surface uses so a campaign reads the same way in the list and once it is open. Several readers, one shared narrowing — so they cannot diverge. Guard: `tests/campaign-budget-once.test.ts`.

**The FUNNEL Overview states the same two answers, in the same place, through the same trigger (#3738).** A funnel is sold leg by leg and funded per (funnel, channel, offer), so it carries a status and a ceiling exactly as its offer does; its page stated neither, with the ceiling visible only as a denominator inside the cost card. `CampaignControlsTrigger` now rides its `headerAction` slot, narrowed by BOTH `offerId` and `funnelKey` — billing keys a ceiling on the triple, so a bare funnel spans every offer selling it and would list a sibling offer's campaigns under this one's name. The money is the SERVED running figure the cost card below already divides by, passed as `totalCentsOverride` rather than summed from the trigger's own rows: one number on one screen, so the header and the card cannot disagree. `buildControlRows`' funnel filter normalizes ONCE (the wire carries two spellings of every funnel) and an unmapped key narrows to nothing rather than throwing; a campaign that predates the funnels names none, so it belongs to no funnel's list rather than to whichever one the reader is looking at. Guards: `tests/campaign-controls.test.ts` + `tests/campaign-controls-surface.test.ts`.
  - **Campaign v2 step 6 — PAUSING is a STATUS, not a budget of zero, and the same modal states and changes it at all three grains.** A campaign carries TWO independent answers to "is this running, and how hard": campaign-service's own `status`, and billing's `(offer x funnel x channel)` ceiling. Collapsing them (pause = set the ceiling to 0) is what this replaces, and it was never merely inelegant: zero throws the amount away, and billing's per-funnel floor lets a funnel funded UNDER its minimum be kept or raised and nothing else, so a grandfathered campaign stopped that way could never be restarted at the figure it was running. Nothing needed building for it — `PATCH /v1/campaigns/{id}` with `{status: "activate" | "stop"}` has been live all along (campaign-service maps them to `ongoing` / `stopped` and stamps `stopReason: manual`, which its own code documents as NOT auto-resumable — a person stopped it, a person restarts it), so this is one `setCampaignStatus` wrapper and no backend PR.
    - **ONE modal, three entry points, and its rows are always CAMPAIGNS.** `CampaignControlsModal` mounted through `CampaignControlsTrigger`, filtered by `{}` at brand grain, `{offerId}` at offer grain, `{campaignId}` on a campaign's own Overview. A brand and an offer are SCOPES, not things billing or campaign-service fund, so an editable figure at either grain would have to be split back across the campaigns and no split the customer did not state is honest. One campaign is a one-row modal, not a branch.
    - **A ROW is one campaign as the CUSTOMER knows it — the `(funnel x channel x offer)` identity — never one campaign-service row, and this is what makes the total honest.** campaign-service mints a FRESH row every time a campaign's workflow changes and keeps only the newest `ongoing`, so a campaign that has run for months is stored as dozens of rows: one live and the rest its own history. billing keys exactly ONE ceiling on that triple. So a list per stored row shows the same campaign dozens of times, each offering to edit the one ceiling they all share, and `scopeTotalCents` adds that ceiling up once per row. Measured in prod (brand `75d7e3e8` / offer `d5ecba00`): 46 stored rows of a single cold-email campaign, 45 stopped, against a real $50 ceiling — the offer read **$2,310/day** and the modal listed 46 identical lines. `buildControlRows` therefore GROUPS on the triple (a funnel-less campaign, which names no triple and has no ceiling to share, groups by its own id). Two consequences to hold: a row's `campaignId` is the row a RESTART addresses (the live one, else the most recent — an ancestor would resume a workflow the customer replaced), and a PAUSE fans out over `runningCampaignIds` so it cannot leave a second row live. Every piece of modal state — drafts, failures, invalid rows, the React key — is keyed on `rowId`, never a campaign id. Generalise: **before summing a per-entity figure, check the entity is the grain the producer keys that figure on** — several rows sharing one billing row is a double count, and it reads as a plausible number rather than an error. (#3533)
    - **The MONEY on the trigger is read, never recomposed.** Brand grain passes billing's own served total (`budgetData.dailyBudgetCents`, which the Overview already fetches for the cost card); offer grain has no served figure, so `scopeTotalCents` adds up the offer's own campaign ceilings — the same shape as the funnels card's per-offer total, and for the same reason. Do NOT sum the rows to obtain the brand's number: that is how two surfaces come to state one number two ways.
    - **The roll-up is TWO states plus `none`** (`rollupStatus` → `active` when ANY campaign in the scope is running, else `paused`; `none` for a scope with no campaign at all). There is deliberately no third word: `partially-paused` existed, and it read as a fault on a brand doing exactly what it meant to — one funnel live, an older one stopped on purpose. Owner-decided: **if at least one campaign is running, the scope is running.** Which campaigns run is what the rows themselves say, one toggle each; the pill answers the coarser question a reader asked by glancing at it. Do NOT re-add a mixed verdict. `statusLabel` in the Campaigns table now reads `stopped` as **Paused** for the same reason: the row in the list and the pill on that campaign's page must say ONE word about one campaign, and from the customer's side a stopped campaign is one waiting to be turned back on. `isActiveStatus` is untouched, so the colour and the table's sort key are unchanged.
    - **Only what CHANGED is written, and status and budget never restate each other** (`controlsDiff`). The writes are a FAN-OUT — there is no bulk endpoint — so a failure is reported per row, the modal stays open, and it never claims a success it does not have. The floor is checked against the FUNNEL total across every row of that funnel the form is editing at once (`projectedFunnelTotalsUsd`), because this modal can move several of them in one go; billing holds the same rule and its 400 decides.
    - ⚠️ **`activate` FIRES THE WORKFLOW IMMEDIATELY** (`executeCampaignWorkflow` + `wakeScheduler`), so restarting spends now rather than at the next daily tick, and the summary above Confirm says so. `activate` also 400s without `x-brand-id` / `x-feature-slug`, which `setCampaignStatus` therefore sends rather than assumes.
    - The campaign Overview header is now the way IN to this modal, which is a deliberate change to the "editing stays on Campaign Settings" line above: the header still renders no field and holds no mutation, and every window writes through the SAME narrowing in `lib/campaign-budget.ts`. Several windows onto one number are fine; a second NARROWING is not. `lib/campaign-controls.ts` is alias-free so it carries REAL unit tests — keep it that way. Guards: `tests/campaign-controls.test.ts` + `tests/campaign-controls-surface.test.ts`.
  - **Campaign SETTINGS states BOTH answers a campaign carries — is it running, and what may it spend (#3456, #3701)** — `components/settings/campaign-settings-card.tsx`, guard `tests/campaign-settings.test.ts`. It carried the campaign's name, its audiences, its services and its click destination; every one of those is a statement about the OFFER, which has its own Settings page one level up, so the screen was four editable copies of the offer's answer one click below the offer itself. They are gone, and `updateCampaign` / `CampaignSettingsPatch` went with them rather than being left unrendered — there is NO per-campaign settings write. Four things a future edit must hold: it writes `saveBrandFunnelBudget(brand, funnel, cents, channel, offer)` (billing's row, never the mirror); **ZERO is the point of the screen** and is an ordinary value, the way a customer stops one campaign without losing anything they stated about how it sells; the floor binds the FUNNEL total (`projectedFunnelTotalUsd`, computed only to check the form — nothing displayed derives from it, and billing's 400 decides); and a campaign naming no funnel or channel says so rather than pointing at a guessed row. **The narrowing that turns billing's per-pair figure into ONE campaign's money is `offerScopedCents` in `lib/funnel-channels.ts`, read by EVERY budget surface** — Offer Settings through `funnelChannelBudgets` for every channel of a funnel at once, and the three per-campaign surfaces through `lib/campaign-budget.ts` (`campaignBudgetScope` / `campaignSavedCents` / `campaignBudgetCents` / `fmtDailyBudgetUsd`), which Campaign Settings, the Campaigns table and the campaign Overview header all import. A second copy of it is how those pages would start disagreeing about the same campaign's money. `lib/campaign-budget.ts` is alias-free, so it carries REAL unit tests (`tests/campaign-budget.test.ts`) — keep it that way; a runtime `@/…` import there turns them into resolution failures.

    **The STATUS lives here too, and it is what a customer stops a campaign with.** The page stated only the ceiling for as long as it existed, so the only way to stop a campaign from its own settings screen was to ZERO its budget — which throws the amount away, and which billing's per-funnel floor makes irreversible for a funnel funded under its minimum (it may be kept or raised, never re-lowered, and going through 0 clears the grandfather). A pause costs nothing to reverse and leaves the ceiling untouched. Four things a future edit must hold: the flip reads the SAME `isRunningStatus` set and the SAME `setCampaignStatus` the controls modal uses (a second list of running-words is how two surfaces come to disagree about whether one campaign is live); a campaign naming no acquisition channel CANNOT be restarted from here, because campaign-service validates the workflow's seven tracking headers before it flips the row, so the toggle is disabled rather than sending a request we know is refused; **ONE Save commits both**, since a toggle that writes instantly beside a field that does not reads as two rules on one card; and what Save is about to do is STATED above the button rather than reported after — including that restarting **fires the workflow immediately** rather than at the next daily tick. Zero survives as the way to genuinely defund, and the copy now names it as the thing that is NOT the same as pausing.

    **A figure under what the funnel may be funded at is put BACK to the smallest one it may hold, never refused on screen.** Refusing it and leaving the typed value there makes the customer guess what is allowed; naming the floor alone makes them do the subtraction the funnel's other campaigns imply — and neither answers what they were actually trying to do, which is spend less. So the field is restored, the surface says why, and it offers **pause** as the reversible way to stop. `minimumCampaignBudgetUsd` (`lib/campaign-budget.ts`, beside `projectedFunnelTotalUsd`, which moved out of the card so the check and the clamp read ONE projection) derives the bar from the SAME rule `funnelBudgetBelowMinimum` enforces, in the same two branches — the funnel's floor, or the grandfathered figure billing already funds it under — minus what the siblings carry, so **a clamped value can never itself be refused** (a guard walks every fixture asserting exactly that). It runs on BLUR, never per keystroke: typing `1` on the way to `10` must not jump to the floor under the cursor. It also RETURNS the clamped figure as well as setting it, because `setValue` does not land before the tick ends and a Save reading `value` back would write the figure it just refused. **ZERO is never clamped** — defunding is an ordinary state, and the bar governs what may be NEWLY stated, not whether a customer may stop.
  - **The offer's HORMOZI LEVERS are edited on the COLD EMAIL campaign's Settings, and Offer Settings keeps the same editor exactly while no such campaign exists (#3686).** They are the words that channel's emails are written around, so they are stated where that campaign is set up. What does NOT move with them is the storage: brand-service keys the 7 user-fields on `(brand, offer)` and has no per-campaign column, so the campaign page is a **WINDOW** onto the offer's own answer, never a second copy — an offer sold through two cold email funnels states one set of levers and both campaigns show it. Two windows on one value is fine; a second NARROWING is not (same rule as the budget's `offerScopedCents`). Five things a future edit must hold:
    - **Never zero editors.** An offer is born at signup and a campaign is only provisioned once a funnel is funded, so an offer with no cold email campaign is the ORDINARY case for a brand that has not launched — a straight move would have left it with nowhere at all to state what it promises. Offer Settings renders the SAME `BrandOfferCard` while `coldEmailCampaignForOffer` answers null, and a link to that campaign's Settings once it does not. One editable card at a time, and never zero.
    - **The gate is `isColdEmailChannel` over a slug SET (`lib/offer-levers-home.ts`), never a `===` at the call site** — a slug re-version rots every hardcoded comparison, and the siblings that also write emails from these levers (`sales-crm-email-outreach`, `feedback-request-cold-email-outreach`) are one line from joining it **if** their Settings pages start hosting the card. Admitting a slug whose page does not render the card gates the levers onto a page that never shows them.
    - **`coldEmailCampaignForOffer` collapses campaign-service's many stored rows to the ONE a reader is sent to**: a live row wins outright, else the latest by `updatedAt` (ISO-8601 UTC strings, so nothing is parsed). Byte-equal to the Campaigns table's `listedCampaigns` rule, and a guard pins the two running-status sets together. A campaign carrying no offer belongs to none and is left out rather than folded into whichever offer the reader is looking at.
    - **Both pages wait for their read to SETTLE before deciding**, and the campaign gate reads `["campaign", campaignId]` — the key the budget card below it already polls, so naming the channel costs no second request. A card that renders and then vanishes is worse than one arriving a moment late.
    - **A FAILED campaigns read keeps the editor on Offer Settings** (`data` undefined ⟹ null ⟹ the card renders). Pointing at a campaign we could not confirm exists is worse than showing the card twice, and losing the only editor to a blip is worse than both.
    - `lib/offer-levers-home.ts` is alias-free (its only import is type-only and erased at build) so it carries REAL unit tests — keep it that way. Guard: `tests/offer-levers-home.test.ts`.
  - **Campaign v2 step 4 (#3180) — the surface is called CAMPAIGNS again (#2902 had renamed it to Channels), it CREATES nothing, and it names the open campaign in the TOP BAR.** Route back to `.../brands/[brandId]/campaigns`; nav entry, page heading, empty state and breadcrumb crumb all read `Campaigns` (the campaign sidebar's back-link is gone, see the no-back-link rule below). **`NewCampaignModal` is DELETED, not hidden** — a campaign is set up with us, not spun up from a table row, so the create control and the clone-a-campaign modal behind it are gone (guarded: the page must not mention `New channel` / `NewCampaignModal`, and the file must not exist). **`HeaderPageContext` (`components/header-page-context.tsx`) is the ONE exception to "the top bar carries no breadcrumb"**: tenant identity (org, brand) stays in the sidebar switcher, but a campaign has no name anywhere in the sidebar and several sit under one brand, so the bar names the one you drilled into — on `.../campaigns/[id]` and nowhere else, on the `["campaign", id]` key the overview already polls (one deduped poll), with a skeleton rather than a placeholder word while the name lands. Do NOT extend it into an org/brand breadcrumb. **A crumb between the offer and the campaign is the FUNNEL, and it is resolved from the CAMPAIGN, not from the path** — a campaign lives at `/offers/:offerId/campaigns/:id`, off the funnel it was opened from, so a crumb gated on the `/funnels/:funnelKey` segment vanishes exactly one level deeper than the list the campaign was picked in. Since a campaign is named for its LEG (#3664), that left the funnel's own name nowhere on the bar. `funnelKey = route.funnelKey ?? campaign?.funnelKey` — the campaign states it in the same wire vocabulary the funnels route carries, so the crumb reads the same words, draws the same mark and links to the same page whichever way you arrived; a campaign stating none renders NO crumb rather than a guessed one, and the read is the one the bar already makes. Generalise: **when a route level is OPTIONAL in the path, the crumb for it comes off the entity, never off the segment.** (#3693) **And the bar names the DEEPEST thing the path states, which for a funnel LEG page is the arrow itself (#3702).** That page is reached from a `Done by you` row and from nowhere else, so it has no campaign to name and the arrow IS the entity; the crumb stopped at the funnel, which meant every leg of one funnel wore the same bar. It reads the label off `funnelLegs` — the same walk the funnel page lists its rows from and the same vocabulary a campaign is named with, since a campaign is named for its leg — so an arrow reads identically whether you opened it as a leg or as the campaign that performs it (`Sales interest → Meeting booked`). It is where you already are, so it is never a link, it draws the same `FunnelLegMark` the funnel's own rows draw, and a `legKey` naming no arrow of the funnel renders NO crumb rather than a guessed one. The path shape is parsed in `offerRouteFromPath` with the others, never at the render site: that parser exists because the campaign crumb was once keyed on a hardcoded segment index and silently rendered nothing the day campaigns moved under the offer. The table's Channel and Goal columns read the brand-Settings catalogues through the shared marks (see the Acquisition Channels section). (#3180)
  - **Campaign v2 step 5 (⚠️ the STRATEGY half is HISTORICAL — `campaigns/[id]/strategy` no longer exists; the campaign's sub-routes are `audiences`, `leads` and `settings`. The AUDIENCES half is live and the rule below still governs it) — STRATEGY + AUDIENCES live under the campaign, and the rule for that whole level is: show the campaign's own value where campaign-service stores one, else the brand's.** They used to link out to the brand pages (`${basePath}/…`), so drilling into a campaign and clicking Strategy silently left it. Now `campaigns/[id]/strategy` + `campaigns/[id]/audiences` render the SAME `StrategyPage` / `CustomerAudiencesPage` through an optional `campaignId` prop — the `EngagedLeadsPage` precedent, NOT a parallel body: the prop is absent on the brand routes, so the GA path is byte-identical and the existing source-substring guards stay green. `getNavigationLevel` (`segments[4]==="campaigns" && segments[5]`) and `campaignIdFromPath` (`parts[5]`) already ignore depth, so the campaign sidebar and the top-bar campaign name carry over to the sub-routes with no change.
    - **What actually narrows is exactly the three things campaign-service stores per campaign**: the goal (`campaign.goal` → `optimizationGoalForRuntimeGoal`, driving the objective labels, the goal-gated columns and the default sort), the targeted audience subset (`campaign.audienceIds`, a display filter over the rows already fetched — human-service has no per-campaign audience read), and the per-audience stats (`fetchFeatureAudienceStats({campaignId})`, on its own cache key so it never shares an entry with the brand-wide read). All three are `null`-inherit, same semantic as the budget in step 3.
    - **(HISTORICAL, superseded by the levers rule below — the strategy route it describes is deleted.) The Hormozi offer was a PREVIEW on the campaign page and did NOT write.** There is no per-campaign column for the 7 user-fields, so `saveOffer` early-returns on `campaignScoped`, the Save button renders only when `offerDirty && !campaignScoped`, and the card states `Preview only. Nothing here is saved yet.` Inline editing still works as a local draft (with Discard) — that is the "see how it would work" surface Kevin asked for. Wiring Save here would take one campaign's edits and overwrite the BRAND's offer for every other campaign, which is the control-lying-about-what-it-touches bug this repo treats as a bug, not a shortcut. Same posture as the Acquisition Channels card, which states what runs rather than collecting a choice it cannot store.
    - **Brand-wide by backend design, so the campaign page inherits and SAYS so**: sales economics + conversion rates, the offer, and the workflow-projection ladder (best model / fleet benchmark — `/workflow-projection` takes no `campaignId`, verified in api-registry). The audiences header states that creating, pausing or archiving an audience there changes it for every campaign, because those controls genuinely write brand-level rows.

The `["campaign", campaignId ?? "none"]` key is byte-equal to the campaign Overview's and `HeaderPageContext`'s, so all three share one poll. Guard: `tests/campaign-strategy-audiences.test.ts`.
