Diagnostic path when a lead is missing from a tab: call the prod reader directly (`GET lead.distribute.you/orgs/leads?brandId=` with a valid-UUID `x-run-id`) and look at the row's `email` — an EMPTY email is the tell, because the delivery overlay is keyed on the lead's registered email (`lead_contact_methods`), NOT on the provider payload the lead row stores. (#3071, #3072)
- **The Leads page counts PEOPLE and says "Contacted"; the brand Overview counts email SEQUENCES sent and says "Outreach" — two grains that must never share a word.** `sequences.total` (features-service `/revenue`, undeduped by lead, so it tracks the spend charted beside it) against the count of lead rows whose `contacted` is true: on one prod brand, one afternoon, **9,915 against 7,895**. Both correct, and the pair read as one broken number for as long as both cards said "Outreach". The rename covers the base tab (`LEAD_TAB_LABEL.outreach`), the stat card above the table, and the CSV columns (`Contacted` / `First contacted at`) — a page whose export disagrees with its screen is the same bug one file over. The card label is a PROP (`outreachLabel`, defaulting to `"Outreach"`) on the SHARED `OutreachStatCards`, threaded through `OutreachStatCardsAuto`: a second copy of that component is how the two surfaces would drift again. The campaign Leads page renders the same component, so it says "Contacted" too — correctly, it counts people as well. ⚠️ The tab still holds rows badged **Queued** (`contacted` means handed to Instantly, not sent — see the status-label rule above); that gap is stated by the row's own badge, deliberately, rather than by weakening the tab's name. **A campaign-scoped count now answers for the campaign IDENTITY, so on a brand whose campaigns are one identity it EQUALS the brand's — a divergence there IS a bug.** A campaign as the customer knows it is not one stored row: campaign-service used to mint a new row on every workflow switch, so one campaign lives in storage as many (measured brand: 48 rows, 47 `stopped`, and 35 distinct `campaign_id`s over 20,039 `leads_campaigns` rows — all one identity). features-service already totalled the identity; lead-service answered for a single row, so the page showed `$5,745 / 2.7×` beside `5,081 leads` while the brand read 7,895. Fixed in `sales-lead-service#412` → v0.50.0 (identity resolved from campaign-service, delivery evidence read brand-wide then narrowed — passing the single id returns the older members with NO evidence, i.e. "never contacted", which drops them from the tabs and is the same bug inverted — deduped one row per person, loud fallback to the single row if resolution fails; `/stats` had the same gap). Verified in prod: both scopes return 8337 rows / 7895 contacted / 45 clicked / 29 replied. Guard: `tests/leads-contacted-label.test.ts`.

**A positive reply is called a SALES INTEREST everywhere a customer reads it — one vocabulary, and the funnel catalogue is what carries it.** The step in `SALES_FUNNELS` (`reply_meeting`) reads `Sales interest`, so the campaign's own name, the leg in the top bar (`campaignLegLabel`), the Sales Funnels settings card, the lead panel's funnel and the onboarding rate labels all move from ONE array — nothing restates the word. Everything else is a label: the stat cards (`Sales Interests` / `Cost per sales interest`), the Leads tab, the Audiences column and its cost column (which stopped being the acronym `CPPR`, so it reads like its siblings `Cost per signup` / `Cost per sale`), the activity-chart bar, the Strategy best-model card, and the daily digest's noun (`outcomeNounPlural`). ⚠️ **The literal email-reply event keeps the word `reply`, deliberately** — `Replied` on the lead timeline and in the CSV, the reply-KIND picker, and every wire name (`repliedPositive`, `positive_replies`, `cpprCents`, the `positive-replies` tab key) are facts about a message arriving, not about the metric, and flattening the two senses is the vocabulary-sweep trap this repo already records. `apps/admin` keeps its own staff vocabulary and is deliberately NOT synced, with ONE exception the CI gate forces: `admin/src/lib/lead-funnel-stages.ts` carries a copy of the funnel steps that `apps/dashboard/tests/admin-funnel-steps-parity.test.ts` pins byte-for-byte, so the catalogue rename has to land in both.

**The campaign Overview states TWO outreach counts, because a lead is contacted ONCE and outreached several times.** `Leads contacted` is `funnelSteps.contactedRecipients` (distinct people, and the base the funnel's first rung converts from, so the card and the share under it agree by construction); `Outreaches` is `sequences.total`, the undeduped volume that tracks the spend beside it. Both are already on the campaign's own `/revenue` read — no extra request. The second card only carries its explaining tooltip when the first is beside it (`contactedOverride != null`): alone, `Outreach` is the only figure on screen and needs no disambiguation, which is what keeps every other surface byte-identical. **The `X% of contacted` line under Sales Interests is SERVED** — `funnelSteps.steps[0].conversionFromPreviousPct`, read verbatim through the `ScoreCard` `subtitle` slot; dividing the two counts in the browser is the compute-a-stat-in-the-browser bug and would drift from features-service the moment either side changed scope. It is gated on that rung actually being the sales-interest one AND converting `from: "Contacted"`, because a rung deeper in the funnel states a share of the rung before it, which is a different sentence; null means "we could not measure this" and renders no line rather than a fabricated `0%`. `formatSharePct` keeps one decimal under 10% — `0.1%` and `0.9%` are different answers about a campaign and both round to `0%`. Guard: `tests/outreach-stat-cards.test.ts`, which pins the CALL SITE as well as the component.
- **The Leads page offers a BOARD beside its tabs, and the two are different statements about one population.** The tabs are nested subsets (a lead that replied is also in Outreach), which is why the page has to dedupe them into `coveredLeads` to count itself; they answer *how many cleared each bar*. The board is a PARTITION — one card per lead, in the furthest step of the funnel it reached — and answers *where is this lead*. Both are offered rather than one replacing the other: the table owns the search result count, the sort, the dates, the pagination and the CSV export, and a board has nowhere to put any of them.

- **A MOVE IS A STATEMENT, and lead-service refuses one carrying no cost** (`400 cost_required` — a meeting that was run and went nowhere still cost what it cost). So a drop cannot write: it opens the SAME `StageStatementForm` the lead panel uses, exported for this rather than copied, which asks the cost and — on the one step the producer prices — the value. A board that wrote on drop would meet a refusal the person never had a chance to answer.
- **Only some columns take a card.** lead-service accepts a statement on five of the seven steps (`WRITABLE_STAGE_KEYS`); a reply's kind is a fact about a message and a click is measured by the delivery layer. Those columns render and REFUSE, rather than accepting a drop and then failing.
- **Every column states where its evidence comes from** — `measured` (delivery), `tracked` (the customer's own conversion tracker), `stated` (a person). It sits on the COLUMN because it is a property of the step: every lead's booked meeting is stated and every lead's click is measured, so it is said once instead of once per card. This is the half a customer asked for out loud: which of these did we update ourselves.
- **The cards cost NO network.** Which column a lead sits in comes off the two sources the page already holds — the lead row for `contacted`, the `/revenue` join for every step after (`trackedStages` + `leadBoardColumnFor`). A board of 500 cards must never be 500 requests, so do NOT reach for `useLeadStepStatements` per card. Consequence accepted: a `never` statement lives only on the per-lead read, so a lead marked dead sits at its last reached column; the panel is where a `never` is stated and read.
- **The write is row-scoped** (`useSetAnyLeadStepStatement`): the board has no open lead to bind a per-lead hook to, and holding the target in state first would race the submit. It invalidates `["featureRevenue"]`, which is what actually moves the card.
- **It draws only when exactly ONE funnel is in scope.** A brand selling through several has no single order to lay columns out in — the same refusal `leadFunnelStages` already makes. With several funnels the tabs stand alone, whatever the toggle last said.
- **Drag is a mouse affordance**, so every card carries a `Move` control listing the same targets. It is BORDERED rather than a bare grey word: a quiet text control reads as a label and never gets pressed, which is how the verify screen's resend link went unused.
- `lib/lead-board.ts` is alias-free so it carries real unit tests; `CompanyLogo` moved to `components/company-logo.tsx` when the table and the board both started drawing it. Guards: `tests/lead-board.test.ts` + `tests/lead-board-surface.test.ts` (which pins the CALL SITE, not just the component). (#3666)

**UPDATE — the board is a TRIAGE now, four columns, and it answers a different question from the funnel rungs it replaced.** Not "how far down the funnel is this lead" but "is this one still in play, and if not, why not". A funnel rung is stated on the lead's own PANEL, which is where its cost and value are asked for; the board has nowhere to put either. Consequence accepted: there is no drag-to-book-a-meeting any more. The columns are `Contacted` · `Sales interest` · `Disqualified` · `Opt-out`, and they carry no funnel, so the board renders at BRAND level too (the one-funnel gate is gone with the rungs).

Two splits are the whole design, and both come from the sales canon rather than from our data model:

- **Opt-out is not an opinion.** It is the prospect's own act and it is legally binding (CAN-SPAM, GDPR), so it never shares a column with a commercial judgement of ours. It is also the only column a card can neither enter nor LEAVE — `writable: false` stops a card arriving, and `movableColumnsFrom("opt_out")` returning `[]` is what stops a lead who asked us to stop being dragged back into play. That second half was caught by RENDERING the board (the card was `draggable` and carried a Move control), not by reading it, which is the reproduce-it rule earning its keep on a consent decision.
- **A "no" is not a disqualification.** Salesforce and Forrester split the negatives into permanent (this person can never buy: wrong role, they moved on) and temporary (not now, no budget yet, not interested today), and Forrester warns that one bucket holding both quietly loses recyclable pipeline. So `Not interested` stays in **Contacted** — owner-decided, and the reason is that in sales a no is where the conversation starts. Only an OBJECTIVE fact about the person reaches Disqualified. **A BOUNCE stays in Contacted too**: it is a failure of DELIVERY, not an opinion, so it is an address to repair rather than a lead to write off. Neither is guessable from the column names, so the board states both in a footnote and each column carries a one-line blurb.
- **A REFERRAL ("Not them, but points us on") is NOT sales interest** — it is not THIS person's buying interest, which is the distinction the column exists to make, and Outreach's own taxonomy keeps Referral as its own class for the same reason. It reads as Contacted; the new lead it produces arrives as its own row.

⚠️ **`lead_job_change` does not exist yet** — instantly-service owns the fine reply-kind vocabulary (9 values, `/orgs/manual-qualifications` → `replyKind`) and has no bad-timing or job-change value, so Disqualified fills from `lead_wrong_person` alone today. `DISQUALIFYING_REPLY_KINDS` lists `lead_job_change` AHEAD of the producer (typed as bare strings for exactly that) so the column fills with no change here the day it ships; `columnReplyKinds` derives the picker from the CATALOGUE, so a value nothing renders can never reach a button. Same consumer-ready-first discipline as declaring a field `.optional()` ahead of a producer.

**Where the two classifications live, and which wins.** sales-lead-service serves the COARSE `replyClassification` (`positive | negative | neutral`) plus `unsubscribed` and `bounced` on every lead row; instantly-service serves the FINE kind per statement. A stated kind OUTRANKS the coarse one — a person read the message, the classifier guessed at it — and it decides interest AND its absence, so a human saying "not interested" is never overridden by a machine reading the same message as positive. Precedence overall: opt-out, then disqualified, then interest, then contacted.

⚠️ **The BOARD reads the fine kind while the STAT CARDS read the producer's COARSE projection of that same kind — so a rule this repo states about a reply kind is only HALF a rule until the producer's projection agrees, and the half that is missing is the one a customer reads as the number.** `leadBoardColumnFor` places a card off `replyKind`; `recipientsRepliesPositive` (the Sales Interests card, its cost, and the learning gate) is counted by features-service off `replyClassification`, which instantly-service freezes from its own kind→coarse map at write time. Two sources, one screen. Nothing goes red when they disagree: both values are real, both parse, both render — the board simply says one thing about a lead and the card beside it says another, which gets reported as "the number is stuck" rather than as a disagreement. Cost 2026-08-29 (instantly-service #649 → v0.75.2): `lead_referral` was excluded from `INTEREST_REPLY_KINDS` here months ago with the reasoning written out in full, and instantly-service still projected it to `positive`, so a customer who requalified a lead from *interested* to *referral* watched their campaign keep reporting **1 sales interest** for a lead their own board had correctly moved to Contacted. Two rules from it: when you change which column a reply kind lands in, `git grep` that kind in instantly-service's coarse map in the SAME breath; and remember a stored classification is FROZEN at write time, so a producer fix needs a backfill or every existing row keeps the old answer forever.

⚠️ **Corollary, and it is why the producer's fix is a DIVERGENCE rather than a re-sync: "worth READING" and "counts as a BUYING SIGNAL" are two different questions and a referral answers them differently.** instantly-service forwards a referral to the agency inbox (a name to talk to is worth a human's eyes) and reports it `neutral` (it is not this person's interest). Its forwarding set is therefore a deliberate SUPERSET of its coarse map's positive entries, guarded by a test that asserts exactly that relationship. Do not read the two as one concept in either repo.

**UPDATE 2 — at CAMPAIGN grain the board is the WHOLE page, and a card is moved by HOLDING it.** No view switch, no funnel tabs, no table: a campaign sells one funnel and its leads are worked one by one, so "where is this person" is the whole question and a second view of the same rows is a control that only ever answers what the page beside it already answers. `boardOnly = Boolean(campaignId)` gates both. **At BRAND grain the toggle and the table STAY, and that is not an oversight** — with no `campaignId` the reply-kind read is disabled and `canMove` is false, so the board there shows the coarse machine classification and can write nothing; taking the table away would leave a read-only board with no dates, no sort and no pagination.

- **HTML5 `draggable` is GONE, and its two failures are why.** It fires on no touch device at all, and its drag image is a screenshot the browser composites — which is exactly where the square white corners behind a rounded card come from. Replaced by pointer events: `pointerdown` starts a `LONG_PRESS_MS` (350) timer, a wander past `SLOP_PX` (8) before it fires means the finger was SCROLLING and cancels it, and the ghost that follows the pointer is a LIVE element, so its radius, its shadow and its opaque background are the card's own. Measured, not asserted: radius `8px`, background `rgb(255,255,255)`, opacity `1`, `pointer-events: none`.
- **ONE gesture, two actions, told apart by TIME.** A quick press that did not wander opens the lead's panel; a hold picks the card up. `touch-action: none` is set ONLY on the card currently lifted — set on every card it would kill the board's own scrolling on every touch device.
- **EVERY column accepts the drop; the form is where a move is refused** (`columnMoveRefusal`). A target that silently rejects a drag reads as a broken board rather than as a rule. Opt-out therefore takes the card, opens the form, and says why nothing can be written — and that refusal is not a preference: `columnReplyKinds("opt_out")` is empty because instantly-service's vocabulary carries no unsubscribe value, so there is literally nothing to state. A card still cannot be moved OUT of Opt-out, for the consent reason above.
- **The rail SCROLLS ITSELF under a card held at its edge.** Four 256px columns do not fit a phone, so most of the board is off-screen while a card is up, and a target you cannot reach is a target the drag cannot use. A `requestAnimationFrame` loop rather than a scroll-per-move, because a finger parked at the edge emits no further move events.
- **The card is the shape a campaign wears everywhere else** (`CampaignIdentity`): the ORG on top, the person under it, quieter, behind **Via** with their photo (`FullLead.photoUrl`, initial when absent or hotlink-blocked). **Every card wears a tag** — the stated kind when there is one, the COLUMN's own word when there is not, since a tagless card reads as one we know nothing about when we always know at least that we contacted them.
- **The tag row says WHAT the card is and HOW LONG it has been that — and that line replaced the per-card move menu.** A `Move` button first sat under the tag and cost the card a whole row; it became a `⋯` on the tag's own row; it is now GONE, and the space it held states the age (`timeAgo`, `text-gray-400`). A tag with no age is the one thing a triage board cannot be read for. **The TAG is what gives way when the row is tight, never the date** — the longest kind (`Not them, but points us on`) is far longer than any age, and a truncated `3 h…` states nothing at all; so the tag is `min-w-0 truncate` and the date is `shrink-0`. Measured at 1280 and on a Pixel 7 against the app's own compiled Tailwind: one row, zero overflow, centres aligned, card unchanged at 78.5px.
- **A stated kind is dated by the STATEMENT, everything else by the delivery status.** `qualifiedAt` when somebody said it (the delivery event underneath is a different moment), otherwise `leadDateForStatus(lead, getLeadConsolidatedStatus(lead))` — the same one map the leads table's Date column reads, so the two surfaces cannot date one lead two ways. The optimistic held statement carries its own `new Date()`, since that IS when the person said it. **No instant ⇒ the card says nothing**, never a borrowed date.
- ⚠️ **Accepted consequence: a MOVE now needs a pointer.** The `⋯` menu was the only keyboard/no-drag path to move a card; OPENING one is still keyboard-reachable (`role="button"` + `tabIndex={0}` + Enter/Space) and a move is the hold-drag and nothing else. Owner-decided — do not re-add the menu without re-deciding it.
- Verified by REPRODUCTION at 1280 and on a Pixel 7 (esbuild-bundled component + compiled Tailwind off the app's own `globals.css` + Playwright), never by grepping classes: tap opens, hold lifts, a drop writes NOTHING until a kind is picked, an Opt-out drop is refused by name. Guards: `tests/lead-board.test.ts` + `tests/lead-board-surface.test.ts`.

**The fine kinds are read ONCE per campaign and joined by email** (`["campaignReplyKinds", campaignId]` → `listManualQualifications({campaignId, limit: MAX_REPLY_KINDS})`), never per card: a board of 500 leads must not be 500 requests. The producer caps that read at 500 rows newest-first, so a campaign past the cap `console.warn`s rather than letting its older cards fall silently back to the machine's classification. Because the kind is recorded against a CAMPAIGN, a brand-level board shows the triage and cannot write it (`canMove={Boolean(campaignId)}`) — the cards then carry no Move control rather than one that fails on press. A move states a reply kind through the SAME `setManualQualification` the lead panel calls, and it ASKS WHICH: "Sales interest" is three different things a prospect can have said, so a drop opens a picker of that column's own kinds instead of writing a guess. What was just stated is held locally (`statedReplyKinds`, keyed on email) so the card jumps before the re-read lands, and a refusal drops it. Guards: `tests/lead-board.test.ts` + `tests/lead-board-surface.test.ts`.

⚠️ Shipping this closed a THIRD instance of the dark-remap gap: `text-green-700`, `border-green-200`, `text-red-600` and `border-red-200` — the weights the reply-KIND pills read through, already live in the lead panel — had no `html.dark` rule, so a pill legible in the light default carried near-black text and an invisible border on the dark surface. The `-50` fills beside them had been remapped and the weights had not, exactly as with the Learning tag and the stale-build notice. When you add a tinted chip, remap the FILL AND the text/border weights in the same commit.

**The Leads page states TWO outreach counts, like the campaign Overview.** It counts PEOPLE (its tabs bucket the same `listBrandLeads` snapshot the table renders), so that count is `Leads contacted` — passed as `contactedOverride` — and the ACTIONS beside it are read off `/revenue` by `OutreachStatCardsAuto` itself, because a page counting people has no second count to give. The wrapper names that second card `Outreaches` only when both are on screen. **`salesInterestSharePct` (`lib/funnel-share.ts`, alias-free, real unit tests) is the ONE home of the `X% of contacted` line** — the campaign Overview and the Leads page both read it, so they cannot state the same percentage two ways.

**A CAMPAIGN-scoped leads read is BRAND-SIZED, so it is sized like one — both readers send `view=basic`, and neither is a "small" read.** `listCampaignLeads` went without the slim projection for as long as a campaign scope meant one stored campaign row and a handful of leads. `sales-lead-service#412` made a campaign read answer for the whole campaign IDENTITY (see the rule above), which silently turned it into a brand-sized read: measured on one prod brand, `?campaignId=` returned **53,777 rows / 156 MB / 54 s** against **102 MB / 6.6 s** with `view=basic`, and `EngagedLeadsPage` polls it every 30 s per open tab. Both readers now ask for the slim projection, pinned in `tests/campaign-leads-endpoint.test.ts`. Generalise: when a producer widens what a scope RESOLVES TO, every consumer sized for the old scope is now mis-sized, and nothing goes red — the reader still parses, the page still renders, it just moves ten times the bytes.
  - **The failure mode this produced is worth recognising on sight: an infinite skeleton whose cause is the GATEWAY dying, not the page.** api-service buffered lead-service's already-streamed response into JS objects and re-serialized it (`await response.json()` → `res.json()`), so a 156 MB body OOM-killed the process (`FATAL ERROR: Reached heap limit` inside `JsonStringifier`, 5 restarts). An OOM kills every in-flight request for every org, not just the one that triggered it. The page's own reveal-on-settle gates cannot help: the request never returns at all. Diagnose by reading `docker logs distribute-api-service-1` on the box before touching the component. Fixed in api-service v0.98.4 (`pipeExternalService`, raw bytes) — do NOT reintroduce parse-then-re-serialize on that route.
  - **`GET /v1/leads` forwards its query VERBATIM as of api-service v0.98.6** — it used to rebuild the upstream query from a fixed whitelist (`brandId, campaignId, limit, offset, view`), silently dropping anything else, which is the same gateway-strips-a-field bug documented for the features `groupBy`/`funnel` whitelist. So a new lead-service filter reaches it with no gateway PR. (`limit`/`offset` were in that whitelist and `/orgs/leads` implements neither — a gateway's copy of a downstream's vocabulary rots.)
  - **A `skipped` lead is invisible on the CUSTOMER page and rendered on the STAFF one, which is why the two apps ask for different rows.** `skipped` matches no engagement tab, so `coveredLeads` never counted it and the customer page could not display it; lead-service's default therefore excludes it (`sales-lead-service#416`: 53,760 of 65,956 rows for one brand). `apps/admin` DOES render Skipped / Buffered / Claimed tabs, so its leads views send `status=all` (#3411). Keep that asymmetry in mind before "syncing" the two apps' readers — it is deliberate, and dropping admin's `status=all` empties three of its tabs.
- **Entity-count badge MUST mirror its page's data source (`listingFallback`), not a feature-stat** — badge≠page is a DASHBOARD bug even when the empty page traces to an upstream gap. Resolution order: `listingFallback[entity.name]` (the page's count) → `fStats[entity.countKey]`. An entity missing from `listingFallback` falls through to a `countKey` counting a DIFFERENT data layer. Unify BOTH the page render AND both badges on ONE canonical endpoint. (#1231)
- **(REMOVED #2196) The canonical quote-requests / quote-PITCHES readers (`listAllRankedOpportunities`, `listAllQuotePitches`) + their pages and sidebar badges are GONE** — the quote routes were orphaned (no nav) and deleted along with the readers, the `["rankedOpportunities"]`/`["quotePitches"]` query keys, and the `/orgs/{opportunities,quote-pitches}` consumer wrappers in `api.ts`. The public-report quote surface is unaffected (it never imported these authed readers). If quote/HITL is rebuilt, re-add the paged reader (never a hardcoded `limit`) AND a nav entry. (history: #1287, #1367)
- **A feature SLUG re-version (`-opportunities` → `-outreach`, any `X-v1`→`-v2`) rots EVERY hardcoded-slug consumer — gate the FAMILY via a shared helper, never a per-slug `===`.** ~9 slug special-cases across the authed + report trees (campaign HITL queue, `PROMPT_EDITABLE_SLUG`, report draft/reply handlers, report quote-requests page, `REDIRECT_TO_FIRST_ENTITY`, report sidebar, `REPORT_ENABLED_FEATURES`, report header labels). `tsc` + tests stay green on a stale literal. Fix: `grep -rn "<old-slug>" apps/dashboard/src`, replace with ONE helper (`slug.startsWith("<prefix>")`, e.g. `lib/expert-quote-feature.ts` → `isExpertQuoteFeature`) + a unit test over the whole family + a hypothetical `-v2`. Thread the REAL `featureSlug` (not a constant) into per-feature calls (prompt-fork) or the new feature uses the old prompt. (#1292, DIS-197)

### Clerk avatars — `imageUrl` is ALWAYS set; gate real logos on `hasImage`

Clerk's `organization.imageUrl` / `user.imageUrl` is **never null** — when nothing is uploaded Clerk returns a generated gradient-initials avatar hosted on `img.clerk.com`. So `imageUrl ? <img> : <initial>` ALWAYS renders Clerk's default, and an `onError → initial` fallback never fires for it (the default image loads fine). To show a real uploaded logo only, gate on the **`hasImage: boolean`** discriminator (true only for a deliberate upload). For orgs without an upload, prefer a logo.dev logo keyed on the org's domain (onboarding sets org `name` = brand domain → `OrgAvatar.orgDomainFromName` in `breadcrumb-nav.tsx`), else the initial badge — never Clerk's default gradient. (#1321 shipped the default by mistake; #1327 fixed it.)

### Public marketing pages — SEO + AI-scraper rendering

For any public landing/marketing/docs page (anything under `apps/landing/`, `apps/sales-cold-emails-landing/`, `apps/docs/`), the default rendering strategy is **ISR + `unstable_cache` + edge cache**, NEVER Suspense streaming on indexable content.

Why: Next.js streaming SSR injects content into `<template>` blocks resolved by client-side JS. Googlebot (Chromium-headless) executes JS and sees the resolved content, but **AI scrapers (GPTBot, ClaudeBot, PerplexityBot, Bingbot AI, curl-like crawlers) parse raw HTML only** — they receive the skeleton fallback, not the data. Putting leaderboards, benchmarks, blog lists, stats behind `<Suspense>` kills AI Search indexing for the very content that earns AI Search traffic.

Concrete rules:

1. **No `<Suspense>` on indexable content.** Hero copy, h1/h2, body text, JSON-LD blocks, leaderboards, benchmark tables, blog cards, stats — all SSR-sync in the byte stream. Suspense reserved for user-specific, non-indexable widgets (logged-in dashboards, real-time counters, chat).
2. **Never combine `force-dynamic` with `revalidate`.** `force-dynamic` wins and ISR is silently disabled. Prefer ISR (`export const revalidate = N`) alone; let `unstable_cache` + graceful build-time empty-result handle the missing-config case.
3. **Calling `headers()` or `cookies()` in a page forces dynamic rendering** even with `revalidate` set. Drop them if the page can be ISR-prerendered (resolve hostname-dependent URLs from `NEXT_PUBLIC_*` env vars instead).
4. **Wrap every fetch / DB call used by a public page in `unstable_cache`** with explicit tags. Edge cache (`s-maxage / stale-while-revalidate`) provides instant HTML; `unstable_cache` provides instant data on the rebuild after invalidation. Both stack.
5. **Invalidate via `revalidateTag(tag, profile)`** in the publish/update flow (webhooks, admin forms). Next 16.2+ requires the 2nd arg `profile: 'default' | 'seconds' | 'minutes' | 'hours' | 'days' | 'weeks' | 'max' | CacheLifeConfig`. Pass `"default"` for ordinary invalidations.
6. **`unstable_cache` at module top level breaks vitest** because the wrapping runs at import time before `vi.doMock` can intercept. Mock `next/cache` globally via `apps/landing/tests/setup.ts` (`unstable_cache` → pass-through identity, `revalidatePath` / `revalidateTag` → `vi.fn()`); declare `setupFiles: ["tests/setup.ts"]` in `vitest.config.ts`.
7. **Validate scraper output after any caching change** with `apps/landing/scripts/seo-snapshot.sh [BASE_URL]` (counts `<h1>`, `<h2>`, `<article>`, JSON-LD blocks under a `GPTBot` user-agent) and `apps/landing/scripts/measure-ttfb.sh [BASE_URL]` (warm/cold TTFB per route). Both pre- and post-change snapshots in the PR description.

Incident 2026-05-24 (distribute.you#1153): pre-refactor, `/blog` TTFB was 1.5–2.3s because `force-dynamic` overrode `revalidate = 60` and every visit hit Neon directly. `/benchmarks` was dynamic because `headers()` was called for hostname resolution. `/investors` took 6–8s end-to-end because metrics were `cache: "no-store"`. Three loading.tsx files existed but were never reachable (page components weren't async-suspended). Single PR flipped 8+ routes from `ƒ Dynamic` to `○ Static` ISR with `unstable_cache` + tag invalidation; no Suspense or skeleton was added on any indexable content.

### Adding a brand optimization goal touches ~8 dashboard surfaces — wire ALL or it ships half-done

`BrandOptimizationGoal` (the "Maximising X" goal a brand optimizes for) has repeatedly shipped INCOMPLETE — a new goal added to some surfaces but not others, leaving an internally-incoherent UI (goal selectable in Settings but absent from the Overview picker; strategy header defaulting a real goal to "Sales meetings"; unit cost falling back to the meeting cost). #2402 (website_visits/positive_replies), #2424 (form_submissions), #2475 (purchase) each had to backfill gaps a prior goal PR left. When adding/renaming a goal, wire EVERY surface below in ONE PR (grep the goal literal across `apps/dashboard/src` to find stragglers):

1. **`lib/api.ts`** — `BrandOptimizationGoal` union · `normalizeBrandOptimizationGoal` (WIRE value → local; e.g. brand-service snake `combined_sales` ↔ local `sales`, `website_purchase` 1:1, legacy `sales`/`purchase` → `website_purchase`) · `serializeBrandOptimizationGoal` (local → wire; `sales` → `combined_sales`) · `isVisitDrivenGoal` · `SalesObjective` union + the `WorkflowProjectionResponseSchema.objective` z.union · `salesObjectiveForOptimizationGoal` · **AND `RuntimeGoal` — the goal a CAMPAIGN carries. campaign-service forwards the funnel's goal VERBATIM from brand-service, so it is the SAME canonical vocabulary, and `optimizationGoalForRuntimeGoal` therefore DELEGATES to `normalizeBrandOptimizationGoal` rather than re-deciding: a second mapping is a second place to go stale, which is exactly what happened (it was a three-token union ending in a bare `return "sales_meetings"`, so `formSubmission` printed "Sales Meeting from Conversation" on a brand that had declared only Form Magnet — #3253). `runtimeGoalForOptimizationGoal` is DELETED with `NewCampaignModal` (#3180); do not re-add a brand→campaign goal mapping.** The audience-stats row type+Zod also carry per-goal evidence/metrics (`evidence.sales` + `metrics.cpsaleCents` for the sales/website_purchase goals — note lowercase `cpsaleCents`; revenue spend block uses `salesCount`/`cpSaleCents`).
2. **`lib/strategy-model.ts`** — its LOCAL copy of `isVisitDrivenGoal` (vitest has no `@` alias, so it can't import api's) · `goalForOptimizationGoal` (→ `FeatureAudienceStatsGoal`) · `isVisitDrivenStatsGoal` (the stats-goal sort-metric family) · `outcomeNoun` · `objectiveForOptimizationGoal`.
   - **A goal that BORROWS a neighbouring family instead of sending its own native goal is a WRONG NUMBER, not a rendering approximation — features-service ranks workflows BY the goal you send, so borrowing returns a different outcome's winner and then prints THAT workflow's cost for YOUR outcome.** `goalForOptimizationGoal` mapped `positive_replies → meetingBooked` and `website_visits → signup` (a shim from before features shipped the single-step goals). Prod, one brand, one moment: Strategy sent `objective=positive_replies` → Dawn, cheapest per reply, **$61.73**; Audiences + the Overview top-audiences card sent `goal=meetingBooked` → Osprey, cheapest per CLICK, whose cost per reply is **$183.85**. Both labelled "fleet benchmark", 3× apart. `FeatureAudienceStatsGoal` now carries `positiveReply` + `websiteVisit` (features' canonical enum, already served) and every goal maps to itself. When a producer ships a native goal, MIGRATE the borrow — a stale shim is silent and only shows up as two prices on one screen.
   - **All three audience-stats call sites derive the goal from `goalForOptimizationGoal(optimizationGoal)`, never a local ternary** (brand Overview `brands/[brandId]/page.tsx`, `campaigns/campaign-overview-page.tsx`, `audiences/customer-audiences-page.tsx`). Two of them had drifted to a 2-branch copy (`isVisitDrivenGoal(g) ? "signup" : "meetingBooked"`), so `sales` / `form_submissions` / `website_purchase` brands were ALSO asking for the wrong goal there. Sort metric comes from `isVisitDrivenStatsGoal`, not a `=== "signup"` literal.
   - **A goal-gated COLUMN must test the goal FAMILY.** `showMeetingCols === "meetingBooked"` alone hides the Positive-replies + CPPR columns on a `positive_replies` brand — the one whose outcome IS the reply. Guards: `tests/audience-stats-goal.test.ts`. (cppr-mismatch-audiences)
3. **`lib/workflow-projection-choice.ts`** — `serverOutcomeUnitCost` + `workflowOutcomeUnitCostFromRates` need a branch, or the goal silently falls to the meeting-cost `else`. A goal with no dedicated grain field reads `resolved.costPerOutcomeUsd` (captured onto `WorkflowProjectionItem.costPerOutcomeUsd` in `ladderRowToWorkflowItem`) — the native per-goal cost features-service ranks on.
4. **`components/brand/brand-status-control.tsx`** — the Overview modal: `GOAL_LABEL` Record + the `GOAL_OPTIONS` picker array (both keyed on `BrandOptimizationGoal`; `beta: true` gates via `useIsBetaUser`).
5. **`components/settings/brand-sales-economics-card.tsx`** — `OPTIMIZATION_GOALS` array + `PCT_FIELDS[].goals` (which conversion-rate fields show) + `REQUIRED_FIELDS_BY_GOAL` Record. A goal with no dedicated backend rate REUSES an existing pair (purchase reuses `visitToSignupPct × signupToPaidClientPct` — brand-service has no purchase-specific rate).
6. **`components/strategy/strategy-page.tsx`** — the "The plan" header (Objective label + conversion-rate rows) must cover the goal, else it defaults to the sales-meetings branch.
7. **The three `Record<BrandOptimizationGoal, …>`** (GOAL_LABEL, REQUIRED_FIELDS_BY_GOAL, strategy-page OBJECTIVE_LABEL) are exhaustive → `tsc` CATCHES a missing key there, but the ternary/switch surfaces (picker array, unit-cost branches, strategy conversion rows) compile fine while silently mis-defaulting. Those are the ones that ship broken.
8. **Backend is usually already ahead** — verify the goal's enum on brand-service `PUT /orgs/brands/{id}/sales-economics` (`optimizationGoal`) + features-service `GET /features/{slug}/workflow-projection` (`goal`) via api-registry BEFORE assuming a backend gap; these goals were dashboard-only wiring. (#2402, #2424, #2475)
9. **`components/onboarding/onboarding.tsx`** — the sales-goal step is ALSO a goal-listing surface. `Outcome === BrandOptimizationGoal`; `OUTCOMES` (label + unit + `beta`) is the picker, and the restore validators + `ONBOARDING_STATE_VERSION` must accept any new goal key. There is NO per-goal rate list here any more — the old `RATE_KEYS_FOR_OUTCOME` held the entry legs of DIFFERENT funnels, so it asked for numbers belonging to no single path; every rate the flow collects comes from the funnel catalogue's own chain. Beta goals are `useIsBetaUser`-gated in the funnel with a `<MaturityBadge level="beta"/>` (currently only signups/sales_meetings ungated). Google-Ads conversion-category LABELS (Sign-ups / Book appointments / Page views / Contacts / Submit lead forms / Purchases) live here, distinct from the settings card's `# Signups`-style labels. (#2508)
10. **`components/audiences/customer-audiences-page.tsx` + `components/revenue/top-audiences-card.tsx`** — the two per-audience RANKING surfaces. Both lead with the goal's OUTCOME cost, and both read that choice from ONE helper: `strategy-model.ts` `audienceRankMetric(goal, trackerSetUp)` → `cppr | cps | cpfs | cpsale | cpc`, with `AUDIENCE_RANK_METRIC_{LABEL,INFO,OUTCOME_NOUN}` carrying the words. The table also renders a count column beside each cost (`showMeetingCols` / `showSignupCols` / `showFormSubmissionCols` / `showSaleCols`; `isPositiveReplies` hides the visit cols). **The card MUST NOT read the response's `sortMetric`** — features-service classes `websitePurchase` + `sales` as reply-driven and returns `cppr` for them, so the card printed "CPPR $319 / 0 replies" on a goal whose funnel has no reply step while the Audiences page hid the reply columns for that same brand. Same reason neither Overview sends `limit` (the server would pre-pick its top 3 on ITS column); the card sorts + slices on the shown metric. The tracker gate is load-bearing: no tracker → no attributed signups/form-submissions/sales → fall back to the measured visit cost, never an all-`-` column. Every number is features-service-owned via `fetchFeatureAudienceStats`: `evidence.{websiteClicks,positiveReplies,formSubmissions,signups,sales}` + `metrics.{cpcCents,cpprCents,cpfsCents,cpsCents,cpsaleCents}`, the outcome fields present ONLY for their own goal and `.optional()` on the wire (render `-` until the producer ships). A new outcome goal → a branch in `audienceRankMetric` + its 3 word entries + the table's `showXCols` pair + optional `evidence.X`/`metrics.cpXCents` on the api.ts type+Zod, and ask features-service for the per-audience attribution. Guards: `tests/audience-rank-metric.test.ts`. (#2632; features-service #554; top-audiences-goal-metric)

**The goal DISPLAY surfaces (Leads tabs, top stat cards, Outreach-activity graph bars, the leads table's per-tab column) read ONE source — `lib/goal-steps.ts` `goalSteps(goal)` — NOT a per-surface `isVisitDrivenGoal(...)` binary.** `goalSteps` returns the goal's ordered funnel steps (base→outcome), each declaring its per-surface mapping: `tab`/`signal` (Leads tab, outcome-first via `goalLeadTabs`), `chartKey` (activity bar via `goalChartMetricKeys`), `outcome` (stat-card count/cost fields via `goalOutcomeStep`). The `switch` is **exhaustive over `BrandOptimizationGoal`** → `tsc` catches a goal added to the config surfaces (items 1–7) but not wired here, so a new goal can't half-ship on the display side. Extend `goalSteps` (add the step + its surface bindings), never re-introduce a `goal === "x" ? … : …` branch inside `outreach-stat-cards`/`pipeline-activity-chart`/`engaged-leads-page`. A step has a `tab`/`chartKey` ONLY where the data exists per-lead/per-day: signup + form-submission outcomes are brand-level aggregate-only today (stat card yes; Leads tab / per-lead surface no) until features-service attributes them per-lead. (#2482)

**A CAMPAIGN-scoped surface keys those steps on its FUNNEL, never on the goal — `stepsFor(goal, funnelKey)`, and the goal is only the fallback.** The goal is the lossy vocabulary: `reply_meeting` and `visit_meeting` both answer to `sales_meetings`, so `goalSteps("sales_meetings")` has to cover BOTH paths (`[Outreach, Website Visits, Positive replies, Sales Meetings]`) and a goal-keyed campaign surface therefore hands the website-visit leg to a campaign whose chain starts at a positive reply. That shipped: a *Sales Meeting from Conversation · Sales Cold Email Outreach* campaign showed **Outreach 9,802 · Website Visits 44 · Cost per website visit $51 · Sales Meetings** — the wrong funnel's steps under the campaign's own name, and the reply it actually buys nowhere on the page. `funnelSteps(funnelKey)` in the same module is the fix, mapping each funnel onto the SAME step constants (identity, so a label/colour/rate cannot fork): `reply_meeting` → Outreach · **Positive replies · Sales Meetings**; `visit_meeting` → Outreach · Website Visits · Sales Meetings; `visit_signup` → Outreach · Website Visits · Signups; `visit_form` → Outreach · Website Visits · Form submissions. Five rules:
- **`stepsFor` / `leadTabsFor` / `chartMetricKeysFor` / `outcomeStepFor` / `outcomeTabFor` are what every funnel-aware surface reads.** An absent-or-null `funnelKey` returns the goal's answer byte-identically, so the brand Overview (a brand runs several funnels at once, so it states none) and a pre-funnel campaign (`funnelKey: null`) are untouched. That is what makes the prop safe to add everywhere.
- **Reads the WIRE spelling** (`SalesFunnelKeyWire`, via `normalizeSalesFunnelKey`), so the canonical rename lands for free.
- **Every funnel chain ends on its own terminal outcome**, so a campaign always carries an outcome pair — unlike the 1-step goals, which have none.
- **The stat cards decide each pair from the STEP LIST, not a `goal === "x"` test**: `showVisitPair = hasStep("website_visits")`, `showReplyPair = hasStep("positive_replies") && !isPositiveReplies`, and `isPositiveReplies = hasStep("positive_replies") && outcomeStep === null` (a reply that is TERMINAL — the `positive_replies` goal — versus a reply that is a MID-chain signal above its own Sales Meetings pair). The old `showReplyPair = goal === "sales"` is exactly what starved the reply→meeting funnel of its first step.
- **The chart, the Leads tabs and the Outcome line follow the same funnel or they contradict the row above them** — a Website-Visits bar under a card row that no longer mentions visits is the self-contradictory-surface bug one component over. The Leads page reads the funnel off `["campaign", campaignId]` (the key the campaign Overview and the top bar already poll → one deduped request), and its one-shot auto-tab latch WAITS for that read (`if (campaignId && campaignData === undefined) return;`) or it parks the user on a tab the chain does not offer.
Guards: `tests/campaign-funnel-steps.test.ts`. (campaign-funnel-vocabulary)

**One level deeper, and it is the same mistake wearing the next vocabulary: a CAMPAIGN-scoped surface walks the ARROW the campaign performs, not the whole funnel.** The rule above moved the stat cards off the goal onto the funnel because a goal cannot name which chain a campaign sells. A funnel cannot name which ARROW of it a campaign runs — it is sold leg by leg, so cold email puts a lead onto the reply funnel and does nothing else, while a closing team converts an attended meeting into a paid client and nothing else. The lead panel's **Funnel progress** section walked all four steps of `reply_meeting` on a cold-email campaign, offering a live `Happened / Won't happen` control for three arrows that campaign does not run — each of which already has its own page (`…/funnels/:key/legs/:legKey`), worked by whoever performs it. Nothing was red: every row rendered, every control wrote, and the writes were even accepted.

- **`leadFunnelLegStages(funnelKey, leg)` is the one reader** and it returns `{ stages, later }`; the full-funnel `leadFunnelStages` survives only for a caller that genuinely walks a whole funnel. The leg comes from `campaignLegFor(funnel, channel.legs)` — the SAME function that NAMES the campaign (#3664) — over the acquisition-channel catalogue the page already holds as a `useMemo` on `["features"]`, so scoping the panel costs no request.
- **An ENTRY leg states one step; an INTERNAL leg states the step it converts FROM alongside the one it converts TO, and the `from` step stays WRITABLE.** Somebody closing a deal legitimately has to say the meeting was attended before they can say the deal closed, so making it read-only would put the two halves of one person's job on two pages.
- **An unplaceable leg falls back to the WHOLE funnel** — a channel whose feature row predates the `legs` field, or one whose legs cannot be placed among this funnel's steps, is still a campaign selling this funnel. Same fallback `campaignLegLabel` makes, for the same reason. An out-of-range index falls back too and logs loud: it is a vocabulary drift worth seeing, never an empty panel or a slice taken from indices that index into something else.
- ⚠️ **The funnel's REMAINING steps are still carried, unrendered, as `laterStages`.** lead-service cascades a `never` across the WHOLE funnel, so the `Won't happen` control's `Also ends: …` list built from the rendered rows alone understates what one click ends — the difference between a decision and a surprise. It is never drawn; it exists for that one sentence, and a unit test walks every funnel asserting `stages ++ later` is exactly the funnel's own walk from the leg's start.
- The `Delivery` row is untouched: it is our own sending, and it sits above every funnel by construction.
- Guards: `tests/lead-funnel-stages.test.ts` (real unit tests — the module is alias-free, keep it that way) + `tests/lead-funnel-stage-panel.test.ts`, which pins the CALL SITE and not only the component. Generalise: **when a surface is scoped to a campaign, ask which ARROW it is about before asking which funnel** — the funnel is the campaign's context, the leg is its job. (#3728)

**The activity chart's first bar counts SEQUENCES LAUNCHED, never emails sent — it reads `New leads contacted` for that reason, and a run of zero bars beside real sending is the honest picture, not an outage.** `sequences` (features-service#416) counts a lead handed to the sending provider on the day it was handed over; the emails that leave the building that day are the queue plus the follow-up steps of leads launched days earlier, and Instantly holds a lead until its next weekday window. So the two series routinely disagree by everything: prod brand `75d7e3e8` sent 293 emails on 2026-08-17 and 6 on 08-18 while launching zero new leads on both days, and under the old `Outreach` label that read as "no email sent for two days" — the report that opened the investigation. The label is local to `pipeline-activity-chart.tsx` (its own `MetricDef` map, NOT `goal-steps.ts`'s `OUTREACH_STEP.label`, which the stat cards and Leads tabs read), so changing it moves the chart alone. Guard: `tests/pipeline-activity-chart.test.ts`.

⚠️ **Zero bars there DO also mean nothing new was launched, so check whether that is intended before closing the question** — the same two days were a real halt: chat-service `/complete` 429'd on Gemini free-tier quota (`limit: 0`, every model), so brand-service field-extraction failed, the workflow failed, no email was drafted, and no lead was pushed. The chain to walk, in order, is `runs` by `service_name`/`status` for the org (a failure spike names the failing service), then that service's `docker logs`. `campaign_service.campaigns.parent_run_id` and `instantly_campaigns.created_at` are the two tables that say whether a lead was launched at all. (#3425)

**A `SignalSeries.daily` is SPARSE — features-service writes a bucket only for a day on which a lead first carried the signal, so a quiet week is ABSENT rather than present at zero, and any chart reading it must fill the gaps before plotting.** `buildSignalSeries` groups the payload's lead rows by their own timestamp; a day nobody converted on has no row at all. On a categorical x-axis (`dataKey="label"`, which is every chart here) each present day takes the same width, so two consecutive days and a three-week gap render identically and the dates read as unevenly spaced. The cumulative Outcome line had the same defect twice over: it climbed diagonally across days on which nothing happened, where a cumulative total is flat across a gap and jumps where the outcome landed. `fillMissingDays` in `outcome-trend-card.tsx` inserts the absent days at zero before cumulating — that is READING the producer's contract (an absent day had no lead), not inventing a series, and it is the same client layer that already computes the running total. It fills up to the day before the dashed forecast starts too, so the quiet stretch between the last outcome and the projection is spaced like the rest. NOT affected: `roiHistory` (features-service drops only the LEADING days whose cumulative spend is still zero, never a middle day) and the activity chart (`pipeline-activity` serves a contiguous day window by construction). Verify a change here by lifting the helpers out of the transpiled component and running them, per the reproduce-the-render rule — a source guard proves the call is spelled right and says nothing about the shape of the series. Guard: `tests/pipeline-activity-chart.test.ts`. (#3662)

**The goal→outcome-NOUN (the plural word shown in "X <noun> / mo" budget pickers + "cost per <noun>" copy) is a SEPARATE label family from `goalSteps` and it DRIFTS — there are FOUR user-facing noun maps that MUST stay coherent, each keyed on `BrandOptimizationGoal`: (1) `onboarding.tsx`'s copy of `OUTCOME_UNIT` (inherited from the retired brand status bar, which owned it until the bar was deleted); (2) `strategy-model.ts` `outcomeNoun`/`outcomeNounPlural` (strategy card, best-model card, outcome-digest email, onboarding launch); (3) `onboarding.tsx` `OUTCOMES[].unit`/`label` (onboarding budget step + Google-Ads category labels); (4) `new-campaign-modal.tsx` `RUNTIME_GOAL_LABEL` (RuntimeGoal, not BrandOptimizationGoal).** Only `OUTCOME_UNIT` and `outcomeNoun*` are `Record`/exhaustive-`switch` (tsc-checked); a `ternary`/partial map silently mis-defaults a new goal (the Overview modal shipped a 4-branch ternary sending `website_purchase`/`form_submissions`/`sales` to **"meetings"** — count was grain-correct via `workflowOutcomeUnitCost`, only the noun wrong). **Canonical wording: `website_purchase` = "website purchases" everywhere (the `purchase`→`website purchase` rename, admin #2803) — NEVER "purchases" (stale) nor "sales" (that's the DISTINCT `sales` goal; `outcomeNounPlural` had `website_purchase`→"sales" colliding with it).** When adding/renaming a goal, grep the goal literal across ALL FOUR maps + prefer a `Record<BrandOptimizationGoal,…>` over a ternary so tsc forces the branch. (#2921)

**Corollary — tsc guards the KEYS of those maps, never the VALUES, so a wrong noun rots silently and is only ever caught by reading the rendered screen.** `OUTCOMES[]` is an array of object literals, so a nonsense `unit` typechecks forever: `positive_replies` shipped `unit: "contacts"` and the onboarding budget modal read **"50 contacts / mo"** for a goal that buys 50 interested replies — naming the people we email rather than what the budget buys, understating the tier by an order of magnitude, and contradicting the row's own label (`"Positive replies for sales meetings"`) two lines above. Fixed to `"positive replies"` in #3078, byte-equal with `OUTCOME_UNIT`. Same pass dropped `outcomeNounPlural(positive_replies)` from `"replies"` to `"positive replies"`: **a plain "reply" includes the negative ones, which are not the outcome the brand optimizes for** — the same distinction the OPENS-are-deprecated rule draws for the funnel. Test for a noun value: *does it name the thing the budget buys, or the people/action upstream of it?* (#3078)

**Fourth noun map, RETIRED with its surface: the Overview learning-window banner is deleted (see the learning-band section above), so the goal-aware noun set is `OUTCOME_UNIT`, `outcomeNoun*`, `OUTCOMES[].unit` and `RUNTIME_GOAL_LABEL`.** Keep the lesson it cost, because it generalises past that banner: **a surface that NAMES an outcome must also DISAPPEAR on that outcome — the sentence and the gate are one decision, never two.** That banner shipped hardcoded to "the first website clicks" for every goal, describing a step a `positive_replies` brand does not buy, and then hid on the first click while that brand was still waiting for its reply: the wrong noun and the wrong retirement shipped together, because they were written as two things. Two more of its rules still bind on the band that replaced it — a "is this even running?" gate reads MONEY (`useRunningDailyBudgetCents`), never a status flag nobody writes any more, and `null` there means "we cannot tell", which is never a licence to promise anything. (#3143, retired 2026-08-29)

**A daily budget is funded PER SALES FUNNEL, and the brand total is the SUM — nothing in the dashboard sets a brand-level budget any more.** billing stores a ceiling per `(org, brand, funnel)` and answers the EXISTING brand daily-budget read with their sum, which is why the launch gate (`billing-guard`), the credit runway, the credit alerts and the Overview tile never changed a line. Three consequences a future edit must not undo: there is no brand-level budget surface left at all (the status pill that STATED the sum is deleted along with its modal, its tier list and the whole `workflowProjection` query that only ever priced them — see the run-status-bar rule above); brand Settings has no daily-budget section, the field lives on the funnel it pays for; and no surface states the brand-wide SUM under a narrower thing's name — the campaign Overview's header states the CAMPAIGN's own ceiling (billing's `(offer, funnel, channel)` row) read-only, and Campaign Settings is the only writer of it (#3456, #3462). Zero is an ordinary value everywhere — it is how a funnel is put down without forgetting how it sells — and the per-funnel floors (`FUNNEL_MIN_DAILY_BUDGET_USD`, $24 meetings / $1 purchase + form) live once in the catalogue while billing's 400 decides. ⚠️ **A DAILY TOTAL states what may be spent TODAY, so it counts only the RUNNING campaigns' ceilings — and that figure is a JOIN the dashboard makes, because neither producer can answer it.** billing keys a ceiling on (funnel × channel × offer) and stores NO status; campaign-service stores the status and no money. So billing's served brand total (`GET /brands/:id/daily-budget`) is status-BLIND: a brand running one campaign at $50 beside one PAUSED at $10 answered $60, and the Overview header, the cost card's `/$budget` denominator and the monthly outcome projection all inherited it — every figure real, none of it spendable. `scopeTotalCents` (`lib/campaign-controls.ts`) is the one home of the rule and `useRunningDailyBudgetCents` the one reader, so brand, offer and campaign cannot state one number two ways; it costs no network, since both query keys are already polled by the controls trigger on the same page. This REVERSES the old "the dashboard never sums the ceilings itself" line, whose premise (billing's figure IS the answer) dies the moment status matters. Two things it does not touch: a PAUSED campaign's OWN header still states its own configured ceiling — the number Campaign Settings edits, beside a pill already saying it is paused — and the credit-runway alert keeps billing's total, because understating the burn would delay a low-credit warning. `null` while the reads are in flight, never zero: "we could not measure this" and "this brand spends nothing" are different statements. Guards: `tests/campaign-controls.test.ts` + `tests/campaign-controls-surface.test.ts` + `tests/campaign-budget-once.test.ts`. (per-funnel-budgets, running-daily-budget)

### Dynasty-First Display Rule (Workflows Only)

Always display `dynastyName` for workflows, never the versioned name — exception: settings/debug panels (show the version + versioned name alongside). Applies to titles, rows, cards, breadcrumbs. Features have no dynasty — they use `slug` + `name` directly.

## The sidebar bottom is a reward LADDER: one thing to DO, one thing to WATCH

`components/invite/rewards-card.tsx` (`RewardsCard`, mounted on all three brand-level sidebars) renders TWO cards, in that order. The invite row is a thing to do: one line, one click. The card under it is a state: money already committed to this org and how close it is to landing. They were one box briefly and the doing read as a footnote of the watching. 🎁 leads both, so they read as one ladder without being one control.

- **The invite row has NO button** (guarded). A button in a 224px rail spends a second line on a control whose whole content is "click me", and the row already is the control. It is a `role="button"` div, not a `<button>`, because the tooltip beside it is itself a `role="button"` span and a real button would nest one interactive element inside another. **Sizes are MEASURED, not guessed** — `gap-1 px-2.5` + `text-[11px]` leaves the label 142px and it needs exactly 142; widen any of them and `Give and get $500 credits` truncates.
- **The confirmation is a TOAST** (`components/toast.tsx`, the dashboard's first), because a one-line row has nowhere to put a "Copied" label. Green, `role="status"` + `aria-live="polite"` (without it a screen reader never hears the confirmation and the control reads as dead, the exact failure it exists to fix), and **centred at the bottom, never bottom-right** — the support FAB owns that corner on every page, so a toast there is the one message the user cannot read. Its timer is cleared on unmount.
- **The heading states billing's SERVED total** (`outstanding_total_cents`, billing-service v0.65.1), summed by billing on the same basis and in the same units as the rows it ships with, so the heading and the Billing list cannot state different figures. Summing the rows here is the compute-a-stat-in-the-browser bug — `reduce` is guarded against. Declared `.optional()`: an older body has no total and falls back to the nearest promise, which is true and understates rather than invents. **An absent total is not a zero.**
- **The bar and the line under it describe the NEAREST promise only.** billing returns them cheapest-bar-first, so the first row is the one the next payment moves; the rest are not listed, because a list of four promises in a 224px rail is a ledger and the ledger is the Billing page this card links to. That split is the goal-gradient reading (Hull; Kivetz and Nunes on endowed progress, and every loyalty counter that says "16 stars until your next reward"): one goal in view, stated as what is LEFT, over a partly-filled bar, with the prize named above it so the effort has a size.
- **The line names the amount it unlocks AND what that money is** (`promiseUnlockLine`, beside `promiseSubtitle` in the shared alias-free `lib/free-credit-promise-view`): `Unlock $347 free credits after $376 more in payments.` Billing's rows carry their amount on the right and keep `promiseSubtitle`; the sidebar has a TOTAL as its heading, so without the amount a customer holding three promises reads the bar as progress toward the whole. `free credits` is load-bearing rather than decoration — without it the sentence puts two dollar figures side by side and the first reads as something else the customer owes rather than as the gift the second one buys. Do NOT respell either sentence in a component.
- **A PROMISE renders in WHOLE dollars (`formatBillingCentsWhole`), a CHARGE keeps its cents (`formatBillingCents`).** Promise amounts are whole-dollar offers by construction ($5 welcome, $400 completion, $500 referral), so cents on them are noise in a 224px rail; the bar still to clear is genuinely fractional and is CEILED, because rounding it down would state a lower bar than the one billing holds. This does not touch the billing-page exemption: an exact amount someone paid must never round, which is the whole reason that page sits outside the adaptive rule.
- **No measured progress ⟹ no bar**, never a zeroed one, which reads as "you have paid nothing" for a promise we could not measure. Neither a promise nor a resolved invite code ⟹ nothing renders.
- **`["freeCreditPromises"]` is the Billing page's own key**, so the two dedupe to one request and the card paints from disk (the root is already in `PERSISTABLE_QUERY_ROOTS`). The Billing link reads the org from the URL, the per-tab source of truth, never `useParams` (the invite reads key on the INTERNAL org UUID from `billingAccount`, and a guard forbids `useParams` in this file for exactly that reason).
- Verified by REPRODUCTION at the real 224px rail (compiled-Tailwind + Playwright), not by grepping classes: zero rail overflow, invite row 35px on one line with the label at 142/142, bar fill 87 of 181 for a served 48%. Guards: `tests/referral-invite-wiring.test.ts` + `tests/free-credit-promise-view.test.ts`. (#3553, #3556)

## The landing answers agents as well as browsers: `Accept: text/markdown` on every statically-served page

`staticResponse()` (`apps/landing/src/lib/static-html.ts`) is the single choke point for all 22 static routes, so negotiation lives there and no page carries its own copy. A browser, and anything sending no `Accept` at all, gets exactly the bytes it got before. `text/markdown` gets the same page converted (`src/lib/html-to-markdown.ts`). Anything asking for neither gets a **406** rather than a document it said it could not read.

- **`Vary` cannot be set from the route handler.** Next owns that header on every app-router response and overwrites whatever a `Response` returned, so `Vary: Accept` is declared in `next.config.ts` `headers()` instead. That emits a SECOND `Vary` field line beside Next's RSC one; per RFC 9110 multiple field lines are one comma-joined value, and `fetch`/undici reads it joined (verified against the running build). Do not "fix" this by setting `vary` in the handler again, and verify any change by reading the response off a real `next start`, never by grepping the source: the source-level header was correct and the served one was not.
- **The markdown variant is `cache-control: no-store`, deliberately.** Cloudflare honours `Vary` only for `Accept-Encoding`, so a shared-cacheable markdown body could be handed to a browser asking the same URL for HTML. The HTML variant keeps its `s-maxage=86400`.
- **The converter takes no dependency.** It drops `script`/`style`/`svg`/`nav`/`header`/`footer` before converting, because these pages are hand-authored HTML full of inline assets and a whole-document conversion is noise. Alias-free, so `tests/unit/html-to-markdown.test.ts` is real unit tests.
- **`Accept: text/html;q=0` is a refusal.** Only `text/html` maps to the HTML variant; crediting a wildcard through an alias like `application/xhtml+xml` would serve the exact bytes the client said no to.
- **404 is a catch-all route handler** (`src/app/[...notFound]/route.ts` -> `404.html`), not `not-found.tsx`, because the body has to be negotiated and `not-found.tsx` cannot read `Accept` without making the root segment dynamic. `not-found.tsx` still exists for `notFound()` calls made from inside a page.
- **Organization JSON-LD comes from ONE helper.** `withCanonicalOrganization` parses each `ld+json` block, removes any hand-written `Organization` node (several pages carried one with no address and no logo, and the homepage carried none), and injects `organizationJsonLd()` from `src/lib/seo.ts` — the same object the React `layout.tsx` renders. An unparseable block is left alone rather than dropped.
- Agent-facing surfaces that must stay in lockstep with each other: `public/llms.txt` (what we are, when to use us, when NOT to, the developer URLs), `src/app/.well-known/mcp.json/route.ts` (no MCP spec defines a well-known discovery URI today, so this is a de-facto convention and the server is named in llms.txt too), and the `/about` + `/contact` trust pages. Guards: `tests/unit/agent-readiness.test.ts`.
